- **[Installed_Stack_Inventory.md](Installed_Stack_Inventory.md)** — what's actually *wired under the hood*? (10-pass audit: deps, migrations, routes, actions, integrations, env vars, CI, desktop, PWA)

When code lands ahead of a spec update, the repo appends a `[PENDING]` line to `COWORK_INBOX.md`. Walk those entries at the start of any Cowork session and apply each via the spec file it names, then mark `[DONE <date>]`.

## What this product is

**Setnayan** (spoken: SET-na-yan, brand-origin phrase *"Set na 'yan."* — Tagalog for "that's all set") is a Philippines-first life-events platform. V1 surface is weddings; the product is built for the broader event market (birthday, celebration, travel, corporate, burial) as event types unlock over time. One app, three role-routed doorways: **customers** plan events end-to-end, **vendors** run a free-during-launch business profile, **admins** (Setnayan team) run operations from a 7-surface internal console. The full 33-iteration spec spine is documented across `0000_*` through `0035_*` folders.

**Papic** (the candid-capture iteration `0012_papic/`) is one of the in-app SKU-driven services within Setnayan. Designated friends/family ("paparazzi") shoot unlimited photos and 5-second clips, tag guests via QR scan, deposit everything into a shared gallery on the couple's existing Setnayan landing page. Every guest gets their tagged photos in real time and can render a 1–30 second personal souvenir reel from a pre-made template library.

**Full Papic spec:** `02_Specifications/10_Papic_Feature_Specification.md` — read it when in doubt.

### Hard product constraints

- **5-second hard cap on video clips.** Capped client-side. Not configurable. UI must enforce.
- **Max 10 tags per photo.** Combined individual + table tags.
- **Untagged-still-delivered guarantee.** Every uploaded photo lands in the couple's gallery regardless of tagging status.
- **Personal Reels:** vertical 9:16 only (1080×1920), 1–30 seconds duration, max 5 guest picks + max 5 couple memorable clips, template-driven render (no per-render AI).
- **Music:** Setnayan-owned AI-generated catalogue only. No major-label music. No per-render music license fee.
- **DSLR pairing is 1 phone : 1 DSLR.** Multi-DSLR-per-phone is V2. WiFi-SDK only in V1; no USB tether.
- **Face detection is per-event-scoped.** Vector store never reused across weddings. Confidence ≥ 0.85 auto-tags; 0.65–0.85 surfaces a suggested tag; below 0.65 the photo uploads untagged.
- **Capture metadata is mandatory.** Every photo and clip stamps `captured_at`, `geo_*` (when fix available), `device_model`, `paired_camera_brand/model` (when paired). Geo is stripped on outbound shares; original on R2 retains it.

## Architecture summary

### Stack

- **Native apps:** iOS 16+ (SwiftUI + AVFoundation), Android 11+ (Compose + CameraX)
- **Backend:** existing Setnayan backend (extend it, don't fork it)
- **Storage:** Cloudflare R2 (PH-region buckets) — hot 90 days, IA cold 5 years
- **Render pipeline:** FFmpeg on Cloudflare Workers + R2 (or Hetzner VM pool fallback)
- **Auth for paparazzi seats:** wedding-scoped ephemeral session tokens via QR-code claim flow (not username/password)
- **QR scanning:** AVFoundation metadata output (iOS) / ML Kit Barcode Scanning (Android)

### Data model (key tables — full schema in spec Part 4.1)

```
Event(event_id, couple_id, paparazzi_tier{3|5}, templates_unlocked[], geolocation_enabled{default true})
PaparazziSeat(seat_id, event_id, claimer_user_id, claim_qr_token)
Guest(guest_id, event_id, assigned_table_id, personal_qr_token)  -- existing in Setnayan
Table(table_id, event_id, table_qr_token)
Photo(photo_id, event_id, paparazzi_seat_id, r2_object_key, type{photo|clip},
       captured_at, geo_lat, geo_lon, geo_accuracy_m, geo_unavailable,
       device_model, paired_camera_brand, paired_camera_model,
       auto_face_attempted, ...)
PhotoTag(photo_id, guest_id, source{individual_qr|table_qr|auto_face|manual_pick}, confidence?, ...)
Template(template_id, feel_category, manifest_json, paired_music_track_ids[])
EventTemplateUnlock(event_id, template_id, purchased_at)
PersonalReel(reel_id, event_id, guest_id, template_id, selected_photo_ids[], r2_output_key)
DslrPairing(pairing_id, event_id, paparazzi_seat_id|live_stream_camera_id,
            brand{canon|nikon|sony|fujifilm}, model, last_paired_at, status)
FaceEnrollment(enrollment_id, event_id, guest_id, source{rsvp_profile|guest_portal|checkin_kiosk},
               vector_blob, quality_score, captured_at, revoked_at?)
```

### Critical flows

**Paparazzi capture → upload → tag:**
1. Native app captures photo/clip → local SQLite WAL
2. Background uploader (BGTaskScheduler/WorkManager) PUTs to R2 via signed URL
3. Tag scanner sheet → scan guest QR (`setnayan:guest:{id}`) or table QR (`setnayan:table:{id}`)
4. Tag intents flush to backend with the upload payload
5. Backend fans out table-tag to all guests assigned to that table (capped at 10 total tags)

**Personal Reel render:**
1. Guest finalizes selections in the landing-page builder
2. POST `/reels/render` with `(event_id, guest_id, template_id, selected_photo_ids, target_duration)`
3. Validate: template unlocked? photos belong to event? guest is RSVP'd?
4. Enqueue render job (Cloudflare Queue)
5. Worker loads template manifest, loads photos+couple-clips+music, generates FFmpeg cmd, encodes 1080×1920 H.264 MP4
6. Output → R2 → notify guest

## Music & template assets

The music catalogue and template library are generated through a separate Cowork-driven workflow — see `14_Music_Catalogue_Cowork_Playbook.md`.

- **Music catalogue:** ~400 owned AI-generated tracks (Suno Premier, generated once, owned forever) across 6 categories — Bridgerton-Feel, Taylor-Swift-Feel, Michael-Jackson-Feel, Jazz, Sunday Morning Vibes, Hip Hop. Stored under `/music_catalogue/{category}/{filename}.mp3`. Manifest at `/music_catalogue/catalogue_manifest.json`.
- **Template library:** ~400 production-ready JSON manifests under `/template_library/{feel_category}/TPL_{nnn}.json`. Master index at `/template_library/library_index.json`. Schema documented in spec Part 4 / playbook Section 12.

When the backend serves a template selection UI to the couple, it reads from `library_index.json`. When the renderer needs music for a render, it picks from the manifest based on the template's `music_pairing_categories` and `music_pairing_bpm_range`.

## Cost-per-event cheat sheet (charm-priced V1)

| SKU | Setnayan cost | Margin |
|---|---|---|
| Save-the-Date Render (₱49) | ~₱5 (FFmpeg + R2 + ZIP) | ~90% |
| 3 Paparazzi (₱1,499) | ~₱195 | 87% |
| 5 Paparazzi (₱2,499) | ~₱265 | 89% |
| per Template add-on (₱199) | ~₱2/render × ~30 reels = ~₱60 | ~70% |
| Pro tier per Widget (₱99) | ₱0 (pure-margin animations / deep-links) | ~100% |
| Pro Bundle (₱199) | ₱0 | ~100% |
| Live Stream Base (₱2,499 — 3 cams × 3 hrs) | ~₱120 | 95% |
| Live Stream Base + 2 cams (5×3 ≈ ₱4,497) | ~₱180 | 96% |
| Live Stream Base + 2 cams + 2 hrs (5×5 ≈ ₱6,495) | ~₱280 | 96% |
| Live Stream Base + 2 cams + 5 hrs (5×8 ≈ ₱9,492) | ~₱430 | 95% |
| Live Stream Base + 2 cams + 9 hrs (5×12 ≈ ₱13,488) | ~₱630 | 95% |
| Custom Monogram Pack (₱1,999) | ~₱5 (one-time asset gen) | ~99% |
| Broadcast Style Pack (₱2,999) | ~₱5 (compositor template/LUT swaps) | ~99% |
| AI Video Highlight (₱1,999 per 60s) | ~₱10 (Claude API + ffmpeg) | ~99% |
| AI Edited Highlight (₱3,499 per 3-min · repriced 2026-05-16 from ₱4,999) | ~₱30 (Claude API + ffmpeg + theme template) | ~99% |
| Vendor Pro Weekly (₱499/wk) | ₱0 (analytics + landing styling) | ~100% |
| Sponsored Boost (₱1,499/wk · certified-only) | ₱0 | ~100% |

Per-render cost: ~₱2–₱5 (FFmpeg compute + R2 storage; music free, CDN egress free on R2).

**Live Stream cost is audience-independent.** YouTube absorbs all viewers at ₱0 marginal cost to Setnayan. Per-event cost scales only with camera count and stream duration; whether the wedding has 100 viewers or 1,000,000 viewers, Setnayan's bill is the same.

## Payment system (V1 — apply-then-pay)

Setnayan monetizes via **PHP-direct apply-then-pay** with manual reconciliation. The earlier token-wallet design (iteration 0003) is **retired as of 2026-05-11** — kept on disk for historical context only.

- **Payment rails (V1):** static BDO + GCash receiving accounts owned by Setnayan. Customer applies for a service / order → receives payment instructions email with unique reference code → pays externally → Setnayan Team manually verifies against BDO/GCash inboxes within 24-hr SLA → service activates.
- **PHP-only pricing.** No tokens, no in-app wallet balance, no spending primitive. Each order is a discrete PHP charge tied to a `service_orders` row with `service_key`, `customer_id`, `amount_php`, `reference_code`, `status ∈ pending_payment / paid / failed / refunded`.
- **Setnayan Pay convenience fee:** 3% added to customer invoice when they choose Setnayan as the payment processor for a vendor booking. Vendor receives full booking amount; fee is the customer's cost.
- **V1.5 roadmap:** automated reconciliation via GCash Merchant API (probable) or PayMongo integration (under evaluation). Activation latency drops from 24-hr to minutes.
- **Comp + Unlimited-Use Grants:** admin can issue free-render or unlimited-use grants to specific customer accounts. Grants are a `comp_grant_id` populated on `service_orders` that skip the payment-pending state.
- **Spec convention:** in all .md / .docx specs and design conversations, prices are written in PHP. Only the in-app UI and 0003's own spec talk in tokens directly.

## What's NOT in V1 (don't build, don't backdoor in)

- All-Guest Unlock tier (every guest can shoot via web)
- Native Pro Capture Pack (RAW, manual focus peaking, ISO/shutter)
- Roving Papic service tier (staff photographers)
- Premium Photojournalism + Photo Book
- AI Top-50 same-day curation
- Live Photo Wall venue projection
- Photo Mission system / crew leaderboard
- Cross-paparazzi de-duplication
- BYO music in Personal Reels (CapCut-style client-side render)

These are tracked in spec Part 6. Each is a future spec.

## Privacy & compliance

- PH Data Privacy Act (RA 10173) — guest consent at RSVP, opt-out flow, face-blur for opt-outs, 5-year retention
- Couple has 7-day review window (configurable) before public unlock
- NSFW filter is on by default and CANNOT be disabled
- DPO is the existing Setnayan DPO (registered with NPC)
- Data residency: Cloudflare R2 PH-region buckets

## Common pitfalls / gotchas for engineers

1. **Don't render reels server-side with major-label music.** Even with TOS click-through, server-side rendering makes Setnayan the direct infringer. Catalogue is owned-AI-generated only.
2. **Don't auto-delete photos within 5 years.** PH wedding photographers keep originals 5+ years; we match.
3. **Tag fan-out from table QR.** When a table has 12 guests but cap is 10, alphabetize by RSVP'd name and truncate. Surface the warning to the paparazzo.
4. **Untagged photos still go to the couple.** Don't filter the couple's gallery view by tag presence.
5. **Personal Reel duration is flexible (1–30s) but template slot durations don't all need to scale linearly.** Some templates have minimum slot durations; if guest picks 1s reel from a template with 4s minimum slots, swap to a shorter-template variant or surface an error.
6. **Wedding-scoped session tokens.** A paparazzi seat token only works for its bound event. Don't allow cross-event reuse.
7. **R2 free egress is a real architectural advantage.** Use Cloudflare's CDN end-to-end. Don't proxy through a different cloud unless absolutely necessary.

## Decision log

| Date | Decision | Why |
|---|---|---|
| 2026-05-08 | V1 SKUs locked at 3-Paparazzi (₱1,499) and 5-Paparazzi (₱2,499) only | Simplification — drop 8-tier complexity; ship V1 fast |
| 2026-05-08 | Templates priced per-unlock at ₱199 | Couple chooses variety; high-margin upsell; predictable revenue |
| 2026-05-08 | Music catalogue: AI-generated owned (Suno Premier one-time) | Avoid ₱150K/year licensing; no per-render music cost |
| 2026-05-08 | Personal Reel duration: 1–30s flexible (was 30s fixed) | Guest control; template auto-scales slots |
| 2026-05-08 | Template architecture: AI sequencer builds template once per wedding (changed to: pre-made library of 500 templates for V1) | Even more predictable; lookup not generation; faster ship |
| 2026-05-08 | Major-label music: never embedded in renders | Server-side rendering = direct infringer; TOS shield doesn't apply |
| 2026-05-08 | BYO music: deferred to V2 (client-side render only) | CapCut model — only legal path; native-app render needed |
| 2026-05-08 | LED Background offline mode = 5-hour event-length playback via 10-min master loop default (5/10/30 options) | A literal 5-hour 8K MP4 is ~80–110 GB — undeliverable to a venue tech on the day. 10-min loop is ~5.6 GB at 8K, fits on USB; venue's playback system repeats it 30× for the full reception. Photo Pool blend rotates couple's photos per loop iteration so the 30 repeats feel fresh. Custom tier (₱899) supports up to 90-min loops for couples insisting on zero looping. |
| 2026-05-08 | **Token wallet pivot.** All paid services in Setnayan route through a token wallet (iteration 0003). Couples buy packs upfront, spend tokens on services. Multiplier: 30 tokens = ₱1. PHP source of truth; tokens render-time display. "FREE tokens" framing on bonus, never "% off." | Bonus tokens drive larger up-front commitments (5–25% bonus on bigger packs); pull-forward revenue + breakage upside. Real cost of bonus is 1–3% (services run 80–100% margin); likely net positive after factoring breakage |
| 2026-05-08 | **Strict forward iteration sequencing rule.** Each iteration `NNNN` may only depend on iterations `< NNNN`. Forward references are reframed as "this iteration provides X; downstream iterations consume X." Backward dependencies (this iteration depending on a later one) are bugs | Ensures the folder sequence builds cleanly step-by-step. Code-Claude can implement them in numeric order with no circular refs |
| 2026-05-08 | **Cascading rename.** Token Wallet inserted as 0003. Subsequent folders shifted +1: 0003 widgets → 0004; 0004 LED → 0005; 0005 vendors → 0006; 0006 budget → 0007; 0007 seating → 0008; 0008 photo_delivery → 0009; 0009 mood_board → 0010. New: 0011 live_stream, 0012 paparazzi | Sequencing rule required Token Wallet (foundational) to ship before any paid surface |
| 2026-05-08 | Pro Widget pricing bumped from ₱99 → ₱100 and ₱199 → ₱200; Paparazzi from ₱1,499 → ₱1,500 and ₱2,499 → ₱2,500; Templates from ₱199 → ₱200 | Clean × 30 token math (3,000 / 6,000 / 45,000 / 75,000 tokens). Single-peso bumps; couples don't notice |
| 2026-05-08 | **Offline-first design pattern** for all event-day features. Pre-event sync of guest list to paparazzi devices; queued writes for RSVP/check-in/captures; service worker caches invitation site for view-time offline access; LED already offline (USB delivery); Live Stream cannot run truly offline — recommend portable 4G/5G hotspot + auto-fallback to local recording | Filipino wedding venues frequently have weak/no internet. Each event-day iteration includes an "Offline behavior" section in its spec |
| 2026-05-09 | **Custom Monogram Pack at ₱1,999, event-wide.** Renames and rescopes the existing Live Stream "Custom Overlay + Custom Standby" SKU. One purchase replaces the Setnayan logo with the couple's monogram across all media outputs: Live Stream broadcast + recordings, Paparazzi photo exports, Personal Reels, gallery chrome, future surfaces. 4 overlay designs × portrait/landscape variants = 8 layouts. Auto-generated default monogram if couple doesn't upload PNG. Registered as a service in 0003 wallet (`custom_monogram_pack`). | Aspirational ("your monogram on your wedding's media") not defensive ("pay to remove ours"). Preserves Setnayan marketing surface in default state; couples self-select to upgrade. Pricing parity with Live Stream's existing ₱1,999 SKU; one purchase covers all features. |
| 2026-05-09 | **Paparazzi rear camera only — front camera disabled by design.** | Photo quality: rear cameras have larger sensors, better optics, more usable resolution. Paparazzi shoot other people, not themselves — front camera has no use case. Removed flip button from action row; replaced with Pick (manual guest picker) shortcut. |
| 2026-05-09 | **Paparazzi gesture shutter — 4 modes via direction.** Tap = photo. Drag up = photo with flash. Drag right = 5-sec clip. Drag right → drag up (chord, before release) = 5-sec clip with flash. Mode pill removed; gesture itself selects the mode. | Cleaner UX than tap-vs-hold. Flash decision baked into the gesture, not a separate toggle. Diagonal "drag upper-right" rejected in favor of the chord (right-then-up) — easier to execute precisely than diagonal. |
| 2026-05-09 | **Paparazzi V1 trims.** Cut from V1 (deferred to 0013 V1.1 Polish): Photo Center curator role (no human owner at most events), wedding-day profile photo auto-update (face match works without it), extended sort/filters (V1 ships 4 essentials only — chronological / photos of us / untagged / type), battery escalation tiers at 10% and 5% (V1 ships only the 20% warning + manual handoff QR), delivered-status indicator + couple nudge. Kept in V1: bulk hide/unhide for the 7-day review window. | V1 was scope-creeping into a heavier product than needed. Trimmed to ship what wins the market — native rear-only candid capture, QR tagging, untagged-still-delivered, monogram branding, real-time delivery. Polish layer ships in 0013 once we have launch data. |
| 2026-05-09 | **Paparazzi storage default: app sandbox only, no auto-save to camera roll.** Opt-in toggle in settings to save copies; defaults off. After upload + 24h grace, local sandbox copy is purged. "My contributions" post-event view per paparazzo for personal copies. | Privacy-respecting default — guests' candid photos shouldn't sit in random friends' personal albums by default. Local SQLite WAL stays for retroactive tagging. Opt-in keeps the door open for friends who want their work as a portfolio piece. |
| 2026-05-09 | **Paparazzi battery handoff at 20% only (V1).** Single warning threshold; manual handoff QR mirrors Live Stream slot takeover (3-second grace, session token revoke + reissue). | Three-tier escalation (20% / 10% / 5% with crew push + auto-pause) was over-engineered for V1. Single warning + manual handoff covers 95% of cases. V1.1 adds the escalations once we see real event data. |
| 2026-05-09 | **Iteration drafting workflow locked.** Each iteration produces a folder `NNNN_short_topic_slug/` with three files sharing the iteration base name: `.md` (engineering spec), `.html` (web + mobile mockup using the canvas pattern from 0001), `.docx` (mirrors md content). Drafted both 0011 (Live Stream) and 0012 (Paparazzi) this session. | Memory rule: Setnayan deliverables ship web + mobile parity in every iteration. Three formats serve three audiences: md for engineering, html for design review, docx for stakeholder review. |
| 2026-05-09 | **0007 Budget & Expenses scope lock.** Three line items per vendor (Package, Crew Meal, Transportation), each with own deadline (couple-input in V1) and payment log. Payment records capture amount, date, method, reference, proof screenshot. Vendor's GCash/Maya/bank QR displayed per line for one-tap scan-to-pay (sourced from 0006). Rollup cards: total / paid / balance / next due. Calendar export via .ics (universal — Apple/Google/Outlook); default reminders 24h + 1h before deadline. Setnayan platform costs auto-populate from 0003 wallet (read-only). Misc free-form expenses supported. PHP only; no targets, no forecasting, no categories, no external sharing in V1. | User's stated goal was to curate every amount needing settlement — 3-line structure maps to actual Filipino vendor billing patterns. .ics over native EventKit/CalendarContract because no permissions/OAuth needed; works everywhere. Couple-only access matches existing wallet model. Targets/forecasting/multi-currency/sharing all V1.1+ candidates once we see usage data. |
| 2026-05-09 | **0007 calendar duplicate-avoidance — two layers.** Layer 1: stable VEVENT UIDs (`{event_id}-{line_item_id}@setnayan.ph`) so Apple/Google/Outlook update existing events on re-import instead of duplicating. Layer 2: per-line `tracked_in_calendar` boolean toggle. Bulk "Add all due dates" only exports lines flagged false; button text adapts ("Add 5 new dates" / "All on calendar — re-export?"). Couple manually toggles in either direction. | True calendar read access requires native app + permission OR OAuth per provider — V2 scope. The two-layer V1 approach gets ~95% of the value without the integration cost. UID dedup handles re-import duplicates automatically; manual flag handles cases where couple added to calendar outside Setnayan. |
| 2026-05-09 | **0010 Mood Board V1 — palettes only, Setnayan Guide rule engine.** Every guest-list role + venue gets a 6-color palette. Color input via hex / picker / name autocomplete (~300-entry library) / image extraction. 20 pre-template themes (Style: Classic/Pastel/Royalty/Nomadic/Sweet/Elegant/Dreamy/Muted/Boho · Color-led: Blue/Red/Green/Gold/Baby Blue/Army/Pink · Seasonal: Spring/Summer/Autumn/Winter). Setnayan Guide rule engine evaluates 7 categories on every change: cohesion (8-color hard limit + 60-30-10 soft target), contrast (per-tier minimums + inversion check), background-vs-foreground proximity (ΔE 10), temperature consistency, saturation hierarchy, photography color cast, cultural defaults (PH bride white/ivory + sponsor pairing). Engine returns pass/warn/contradict states with structured suggestions. Disable toggle per-event (default ON) and per-palette ("Use this color anyway"). Master palette dedups by hex AND named color. Stylist persona, inspirations paste board, role outfit galleries, venue segments with photos/videos, concept-vs-actual all DEFERRED until stylist persona exists (V2 / Din). Saved palettes V1=couple-scoped, schema-ready for stylist scope V2. Mobile thumb-zone UX (all primary touch targets in lower third). | Engine baseline grounded in color theory + WCAG contrast ratios + wedding photography best practice + PH cultural conventions. Opinionated by design — couples save themselves photos that don't work. Override paths for couples who want full control. 20 themes parallel content workstream like 400 Reel templates and music catalogue; iteration code consumes via JSON manifest. Couple-scoped saved palettes today, stylist-scoped library lights up later when stylists exist (Din Phase 3). |
| 2026-05-09 | **0006 Vendors taxonomy: hybrid (28 canonical + per-event custom).** Canonical list spans typical PH wedding services (ceremony/reception venues, catering, photo, video, SDE, drone, prenup, coordination, HMUA, gowns, suits, florals, cake, lights & sound, DJ/emcee, mobile bar, live band, acoustic performer, choir/quartet, officiant, bridal car, guest shuttle, invitation print, stationery/signage, photobooth, souvenirs, rings, honeymoon planner). Couples mark canonical services "not needed" or add custom rows scoped per event. | Canonical spine drives clean coverage reporting and predictable rollup into Budget (0007). Custom rows handle the long tail without forcing a "request canonical addition" gate. Per-event scoping keeps the global list clean. |
| 2026-05-09 | **0006 vendor payment model: flexible custom milestones, not fixed 3-stage.** Each vendor has any number of milestones with custom labels, due dates, amounts, paid status, payment method/reference. UI presets scaffold "Reservation + Down + Balance," "4 partials + Balance," "Lump sum" patterns. Balance + payment progress + overdue flag computed; surfaced in list and detail views. **Note:** 0007 Budget overlays this with a 3-line view (Package / Crew Meal / Transportation) for top-level budget UX; the underlying milestone schema in 0006 stays flexible. | PH wedding vendors structure schedules differently — fixed 3-stage forces couples to lie about reality. Schema flexibility costs little; UI presets keep entry fast for the common case. |
| 2026-05-09 | **0006 crew meals: count × per-meal cost = computed total**, with `vendor_provides_meals` toggle that zeroes contribution without losing headcount. Defaults to event-level rate (set once on catering vendor); editable per-vendor. Rolls up to event aggregate; consumed by 0007 Budget as the "Crew Meal" line per vendor. | Couple needs the rolled-up number for catering headcount and the budget. The opt-out matters because larger vendors sometimes bring their own crew meals. Don't double-count — vendor packages are separate budget rows. |
| 2026-05-09 | **0006 vendor payments are tracking-only — NOT integrated with token wallet (0003).** Vendor money leaves Setnayan (cash/GCash/bank transfer direct to vendor). Setnayan records what couple says they paid; never validates the transaction. Wallet stays reserved for Setnayan-charged services (Pro widgets, Paparazzi, Templates, Monogram, Live Stream, LED). Combined views live in Budget (0007), which reads vendor milestones + wallet spend without coupling them. | Mixing external vendor payments into the wallet would blur the source-of-truth boundary. Couples already think in two buckets: "what I paid the vendor" vs "what I paid Setnayan." Keep them separate; combine at the reporting layer. |
| 2026-05-09 | **0006 manual encoding only in V1; vendor self-input deferred to Din (Phase 3 supplier app).** Vendors are external; their accounts and inputs land in Din. V1's vendor records are couple-encoded and inherit cleanly into Din with no migration. | Building a half-baked vendor login surface in V1 would block launch and create a migration we'd then have to throw away. Couple-encoded data is sufficient for the planning workflow. Din ships in Phase 3 with the same backend records. |
| 2026-05-09 | **0006 meetings module added to vendor profiles.** Each vendor has a list of scheduled meetings (initial consult, tasting, walkthrough, fitting, etc.) with title, datetime, mode (in-person/video/phone), location/link, agenda, attendees, post-meeting notes. Soonest upcoming surfaces as "Next meeting" on the vendor card; past-but-still-scheduled renders amber and prompts confirmation. `vendor_meetings` table includes a `created_by_actor` column ('couple' in V1) so Din (Phase 3) can later write 'vendor' rows into the same table without migration. No .ics export in 0006 — that lives in 0007 Budget which already owns the calendar pattern. | Couples currently track meetings in scattered notes and message threads. Centralizing on the vendor profile mirrors how planners actually work. The forward-compatibility column makes Din's takeover data-only. .ics in 0007 means one combined feed (payments + meetings) instead of two. |
| 2026-05-09 | **0008 Seating Chart table catalog locked at 13 entries.** Round (8/10/12), Long banquet (6/8/10), Family head — long rectangular (12/14/16), Sweetheart (2), Serpentine (10/14/18). Family head tables are explicitly **long rectangulars, not ovals** — sized larger than the standard long banquet and reserved for the immediate family of the bride or groom. Capacity is bound to type — no per-table custom-capacity in V1. Custom shapes deferred to V1.1. | Covers 95%+ of Filipino reception venues. Avoids the rabbit hole of arbitrary geometry; serpentine added explicitly because Filipino entourage tables often use it; family-head ovals were a visual mistake — Filipino venues stock these as long banquet tables, not oval tops. |
| 2026-05-09 | **0008 floor plan carries full venue elements**, not just tables. Stage is required; band platform is optional (adjacent to or on the stage); dancefloor is an optional rectangle in front of the stage that no tables may sit inside; at least one main entrance door is required by Publish (side / service doors optional). Doors snap to walls only and render as arched gaps in the wall line. | The seating chart is the canonical reception layout, used by Print pack signs and by Paparazzi (0012) for orientation. Modeling stage / band / dancefloor / doors as first-class elements means the print-pack overview reads as a real floor plan rather than just a table grid. |
| 2026-05-09 | **0008 venue dimensions are optional.** Couples who already know their venue size flip a toolbar toggle to set room width × length in metres; the editor then renders walls at scale and bounds placement (no tables outside walls, on doors, or on the dancefloor). Couples who don't know yet leave it blank — placement is fully free-form, the canvas grows to fit content, no walls are drawn. Couples can set or clear at any time. | Forcing dimensions upfront would block planning for couples who book the venue but haven't measured it. Free-form is the early-planning default; bounded mode is a precision affordance for the home stretch. |
| 2026-05-09 | **0008 rotation rule by shape.** Round, sweetheart, family-head: rotation locked at 0°. Long banquet (`long_*`) and serpentine (`serpentine_*`): rotation in **45° increments** (0/45/90/135/180/225/270/315). | Filipino venues are rarely orthogonal — couples need 45° steps to align friend / entourage tables to non-orthogonal walls and aisles. Round and sweetheart don't benefit from rotation; family-head tables sit perpendicular to the stage by convention. |
| 2026-05-09 | **0008 alignment lock on by default.** Dragging a table reveals pink horizontal and vertical guide lines that snap the moving table's center, top edge, or left edge to the same coordinate as nearby stationary tables. Toggleable in the toolbar; complementary to (not a replacement for) the 10-pt grid snap. | Without alignment guides, a 60-table reception ends up with subtle misalignments that look bad in the printed overview. Lock-on-default mirrors how Figma / Sketch behave; couples turn it off only for pixel-precise edge cases. |
| 2026-05-09 | **0008 chair-level interaction.** Every table renders chairs as small circles around the perimeter — one per seat. Filled with profile photo when available; with two-letter initials on a side-coded gradient when not. Empty seats render dashed-outline white. **Gestures:** tap chair = swap that guest's seat (single-seat move); long-press chair = open guest details; tap table body = swap that whole table's position with another (everyone seated stays with their table); long-press table = table settings. Initials-only chairs surface the full name on selection via a floating name pop and a sticky banner at the top of the canvas. | The chair-circle model mirrors how couples actually think about seating — by person, not by abstract slot. Splitting tap (swap) from long-press (details) keeps the primary interaction (move someone to a different seat) one-handed and fast. The dual hit-zone (chair circles vs. table body) lets a single tap gesture mean two different things without a mode switch. |
| 2026-05-09 | **0008 serpentine geometry locked.** A serpentine segment is a quarter-donut wedge — one quarter of a ring — with **2 inner-cove chairs + 4 outer-edge chairs = 6 seats per segment**. Couples assemble multiple segments into C-shapes, U-cups, and longer arcs. Catalog ships 1/2/3-segment configurations (`serpentine_6` / `serpentine_12` / `serpentine_18` = 6/12/18 seats). Serpentines are **resizable in place** via the table-detail sheet — adding or removing a segment without deleting and re-creating the table; capacity = `6 × segments`. Earlier representations (S-curve strokes, line-only paths) were wrong — serpentines are real banded tables with width, fillet, and chairs on both inside and outside. | Matches reference photos of real Filipino-wedding entourage tables. The 2+4 chair pattern is dictated by the donut geometry: the inner arc is shorter, fits ~2; the outer arc is longer, fits ~4. Locking this prevents future "let's make it 5 chairs" drift, and the segment-based capacity makes the resize affordance trivial to spec. |
| 2026-05-09 | **0008 stage placement is free anywhere on the canvas.** Top, bottom, left, right edge, or true center for in-the-round receptions. Auto-fill ring distances recompute around wherever the stage lands. | Real Filipino venues run all four orientations plus the occasional in-the-round. Locking the stage to one edge would force couples to mentally rotate every venue into our model. |
| 2026-05-09 | **0008 auto-fill uses role-tier rings centered on the stage.** Tier 1 (principal sponsors + officiant + immediate family) innermost; Tier 2 (entourage + secondary sponsors + bearers + flower girl) second; Tier 3 (extended family) third; Tier 4 (friends, work, school) outermost. Sweetheart tables are excluded from the rotation. Locked tables are skipped. Already-placed guests are never moved. | Matches how Filipino weddings actually seat people. Family-side L/R splits and mobility-first heuristics were considered and deferred — V1 ships the dominant strategy. |

The 30-day grace gives the user time to verify the download succeeded before Setnayan's bandwidth/storage cost is reduced. | Cost-management rule. | 2026-05-10 | **Face detection auto-tag with layered enrollment.** Three enrollment paths feed one per-event face vector store: (1) RSVP profile photo from 0001/0002 — baseline ~90% guest coverage at zero friction; (2) pre-event guest portal upload (1–3 photos) via personal invitation page — opt-in, medium-to-high quality; (3) on-the-day check-in kiosk (laptop/tablet at venue entrance) — highest quality, controlled lighting and pose. Written to EXIF on JPEGs (`DateTimeOriginal`, `GPSLatitude/Longitude`, `Make/Model`, `UserComment` JSON) and QuickTime atoms on MP4s, plus the `photos` table and SQLite WAL. Couple-level geolocation toggle in event settings (default ON because wedding venues are public; off for private home weddings). Outbound shares (IG/FB/WhatsApp + Personal Reels) strip geo from the rendered share copy; the original on R2 retains it. Server-side geofence sanity check: photos > 50 km from event venue drop their geo metadata to prevent stale-fix paparazzi phones from stamping morning-coffee-shop coordinates onto wedding photos. | Wedding photographers stamp this data routinely; Setnayan matching the standard removes a lot of "where was this taken?" guesswork for couples and for downstream culling. Per-photo provenance (which paparazzo, which paired camera) is also operations-useful — supports debugging quality issues per body and per seat. | 2026-05-10 | **Adaptive compression + offline queue.** Phone runs a rolling 30-second bandwidth estimator and switches between three upload modes: strong (>5 Mbps) ships originals; medium (1–5 Mbps) re-encodes JPEG at 80% / H.264 CRF 23 1080p, queues RAW; weak (<1 Mbps) or offline queues everything locally and ships thumbnails first so the couple's gallery shows the moment was captured even before the original lands. Mode switches sticky (30 s minimum dwell) to prevent oscillation. Offline queue uses the existing SQLite WAL; persists across app suspend, force-quit, reboot, and battery handoff; resumes on foreground / network change via NWPathMonitor / ConnectivityManager.NetworkCallback; exponential backoff 5s→10s→30s→60s→5min steady; 7-day TTL on local-only items before "couldn't deliver" prompt with bulk re-try. RAW from DSLR pairings is never uploaded in medium mode — too large to not block the JPEG queue. Top-bar upload chip surfaces queue depth + network mode: "12 queued · 3 uploading · medium". | Filipino wedding venues frequently have variable connectivity; the offline-first design pattern (already a memory rule) demands captures don't depend on network state. Adaptive compression keeps the gallery feeling responsive on weak networks while preserving original quality when bandwidth allows. RAW handling protects the JPEG fast path; couples get gallery-ready stills first, archival originals follow. |

| 2026-05-11 | **0019 — in-app file viewers + dedicated thread-scoped storage.** Chat attachments extended beyond image/pdf/voice to include doc (docx via mammoth.js read-only HTML render) and spreadsheet (xlsx/csv via SheetJS virtualized table). All attachments live in a dedicated R2 bucket (`setnayan-thread-files`) with key prefix `threads/{thread_id}/{attachment_id}/{filename}` enforcing thread-scope. R2 bucket policy denies public reads — every download goes through an authenticated Setnayan endpoint that resolves the requesting user's `chat_thread_participants` row before issuing a signed URL. Preview generation pipeline: on upload, a Cloudflare Queue job runs LibreOffice headless to render first-page PNG previews for doc / sheet / pdf, written to `preview.png` next to the source. Per-file caps: image 25 MB, pdf 50 MB, doc 25 MB, sheet 25 MB, voice 60s. Per-thread cap: 5 GB rolling, oldest archived to R2 cold tier when approaching cap. Files NEVER cross thread boundaries; vendors active on multiple weddings see a separate file shelf per thread; nothing global. Coordinator removal revokes file access within 15 min (signed-URL TTL). | Couples and vendors currently exchange contracts and mood-board PDFs over Messenger / email / Drive — files end up scattered across services with inconsistent permissions. Pulling files in-app with thread-scoped storage means the file lives next to the conversation that produced it, AND the access boundary matches the conversation boundary (if you can read the thread, you can read its files; revoke thread membership = revoke file access). Native readers avoid the Drive integration trap (where Setnayan can't enforce coordinator-revocation once the file leaves the Setnayan boundary). Preview pipeline keeps the chat bubble useful even before the user opens the full file. |
| 2026-05-11 | **[AMENDED 2026-05-16: video latency targets retired — Daily.co/video feature removed; only Messenger-class chat targets remain in force. See 2026-05-16 marketplace/payment/verification lock row.]** **0019 performance — Messenger-class chat~~, Zoom-class video~~ as locked targets.** Locked latency targets (V1): chat message-send round-trip p50 < 150 ms / p99 < 500 ms; typing indicator < 100 ms; video call setup < 3 s; one-way video latency < 200 ms; video reconnect < 5 s. Achievable because the underlying tech (Supabase Realtime for chat, Daily.co SFU for video) is the same architecture pattern Messenger and Zoom use. Required infra: Supabase + Daily.co both hosted in Singapore region (closest to PH; ~50 ms RTT to Manila / Cebu / Davao); R2 in PH region per 0013. Required engineering patterns: optimistic-send chat composer, virtualized scroll in threads, ephemeral Realtime channels for presence/typing (never persisted writes), memoized message bubbles, Daily room pre-creation on scheduled meetings, indexed `chat_messages (thread_id, created_at DESC)`, WebSocket-kept-open-on-tab-focus. CI perf budget: 500-message thread paint < 600 ms p95 on throttled 4G; budget regressions block merge. | Speed is non-negotiable for chat / video — if Setnayan feels slower than Messenger or Zoom, couples and vendors will route conversations back to those apps and the in-app value-prop collapses. The good news: both targets are well-understood; it's an architecture-discipline problem, not a research problem. The discipline checklist is the spec contract — every chat / video PR validates against it. Maintenance discipline matters more than initial build — performance regresses easily, CI budgets enforce. |
| 2026-05-11 | **[AMENDED 2026-05-16: "video meetings" struck — Daily.co retired, only chat + file sharing + coordinator-join remain free-use. No per-feature charge, no per-meeting fee, no per-GB attachment surcharge, no premium tier. Marginal cost (Daily.co ~₱6 per 60-min 1:1, R2 storage in fractions of a peso per thread per month) is absorbed into the platform's general operating budget. The 0015 marketing site mirrors the promise: vendor perks list now reads "In-app chat, video meetings & file sharing — free." | Communication is the connective tissue of the entire platform. Gating it behind a paywall would push couples and vendors back to WhatsApp / Messenger and gut the in-app value-prop. The free-use promise is also a vendor-acquisition lever: combined with "free vendor registration during launch" (already locked), vendors get the entire client-management toolkit at zero cost — a demonstrably stronger offer than every PH wedding-tech competitor today. Revisited only if a single event exceeds 500 hrs/month sustained (at which point we'd consider per-event throttles, not paywalls). |

| 2026-05-12 | **Two-admin approval scope — major decisions only (§ 9.1).** The "four-eyes" two-admin pattern applies ONLY to high-stakes, irreversible, or fraud-adjacent actions; routine operational work is single-admin authority. **Requires two-admin:** promote a user to admin role · add internal account (`is_internal=TRUE`, § 10a) · add team-member to shared-pool eligibility (`is_team_member=TRUE`, § 10b) · issue unlimited_use_grant > ₱10K retail to external customer · modify Setnayan's static BDO/GCash receiving account numbers · mid-quarter SKU price change · vendor force-delisting · refund > ₱25K · re-publish a rejected vendor application. **Single-admin authority:** accept/reconcile payment (Transactions/Payments Handler) · approve vendor verification queue item (Verification Handler) · issue comp gift ≤ ₱10K · process refund ≤ ₱25K · suspend/restore customer account · vendor tier moves within normal policy · dispute mediation messages · inbox triage · audit log export. Flow mechanics: `admin_approval_requests` table tracks pending requests with rationale; Admin A initiates → status `pending_approval` → Admin B (different person, DB-enforced) approves/rejects within 7-day window; bootstrap exception when platform has <2 admins (founding Owner can self-promote spouse with `bootstrap=true` audit tag, single-use). | Payment reconciliation alone is dozens of approvals per day under the 24-hr SLA — gating each on a second admin would mathematically miss the SLA and create a permanent backlog. Vendor verification is queue-grindy in the same way. Comp gifts ≤ ₱10K are forgiving (worst-case over-issue costs less than the throughput hit); above that threshold the dollar value gets material and flips to two-admin. Major decisions (admin promotions, internal-account creation, payment-account changes, mid-quarter pricing moves) happen weekly or less — friction is a feature, not a bug. |

| 2026-05-12 | **Canonical entity ID format locked — `S89<TYPE>-<10-char Crockford>`.** All customer-facing identifiers across the platform adopt a uniform prefix-typed format: `S89U-XXXXXXXXXX` (users — covers customers/vendors/admins/guests), `S89V-XXXXXXXXXX` (vendor business profiles), `S89E-XXXXXXXXXX` (events), `S89O-XXXXXXXXXX` (orders — replaces both the original `STNYN-XXXXXXXX` reference codes from iteration 0034 and the interim `SET-XXXXXXXX` from the rename cleanup). Body is **10 characters of Crockford base 32** (`0-9A-Z` minus `I/L/O/U`) drawn uniformly at random, not sequential — sequential would enable enumeration attacks. Internal joins continue to use a hidden `bigserial id` per row; the `public_id` column is what shows up to customers, on receipts, in QR codes, in support transcripts. Canonical reference at `02_Specifications/Account_ID_Format.md` with the Postgres generator function `generate_public_id(type_letter)`. 9 type letters reserved for V1.5+: A/B/C/G/M/P/S/T/R. Iteration 0034 spec updated (column `reference_code` → `public_id`, length 8 → 10 chars, prefix flipped to `S89O-`). The `payment_inbox_messages` reconciliation regex updated accordingly. | Per-type prefix is debugging-friendly — an admin looking at a log line knows at a glance whether they're reading a user row, an order row, or an event row, without having to consult the schema. Random body prevents the enumeration attack the original "sequential" proposal would have enabled. Crockford base 32 (vs full base 62) was chosen for phone-call legibility — support reps reading IDs to customers don't have to disambiguate uppercase-A vs lowercase-a, and the omitted ambiguous chars (I/L/O/U) eliminate the common transcription errors. The `S89` brand prefix is stylistic — drops out in one config flip if it ever feels wrong. |

| 2026-05-12 | **No-filler template rule locked across all video-output SKUs.** Every template in the V1 catalog ships flagship quality. The ~10 free templates per SKU are 10 of the *best*, not the 10 cheapest — free is the marketing surface; couples upgrade to pro based on how good free already feels. Rejected templates are cut, not weakened to fit. Quality > breadth. The original "500 Personal Reel templates" target was replaced with a curated `~10 free + ~100 pro` per video-output SKU. SDE specifically is the flagship because delivering a story-edited 3–5min film during the reception is something even premium human videographers cannot do. The "Setnayan AI" brand wrapping is a positioning + IP move — couples don't know they're using DALL-E, which preserves Setnayan as the brand-of-record and means we can swap DALL-E for Recraft / Midjourney / a self-hosted model later without customer-facing breakage. Brief-locks-at-payment is critical anti-abuse — without it, one couple could pay ₱2,999 and generate logos for 5 different sets of initials. Custom Monogram Pack retirement consolidates 3 confusing tiers (Free / Pack / Bespoke) into 3 clear ones (Free / Pro Hero / Bespoke). Vectorizer.ai final step ensures the delivered SVG scales cleanly to 8K LED panels and print-pack signage. |

Setnayan delivers Basic at ₱1,999 (60% cheaper than entry-tier musician), Premium at ₱3,999 (73% cheaper than mid-tier), Wedding Suite at ₱9,999 (80% cheaper than composer · same-week delivery vs 4–16 weeks). The 3-tier ladder captures impulse-buy (₱1,999) through flagship (₱9,999) without overlapping with premium video SKUs (AI Edited Highlight ₱2,999, SDE ₱24,999). The lyric approval gate is what justifies Premium's ₱3,999 over Basic's ₱1,999 — couples get co-writer collaboration, not just prompt-and-receive. Mastering-pass + Personas-locked vocals + lyric through-line are what make 3 separate Suno generations feel like 1 intentional collection vs 3 random outputs. After event creation, customer sees a **four-option choice card** (DIY · 1-Week · 3-Month · 12-Month) with the savings math computed live and 12-Month flagged as BEST VALUE. **Schema additions on `events`:** `guided_planner_status TEXT` enum `('diy' | 'active' | 'expired')` default `'diy'` · `guided_planner_tier TEXT` enum `('1week' | '3month' | '12month' | NULL)` · `guided_planner_expires_at TIMESTAMPTZ NULL`. **Dashboard surface variants** (iteration 0021): DIY mode shows 10-tile grid + upgrade banner with savings ladder · Guided mode shows step-progress widget at top + next-action layout. **Daily cron job** sweeps `events` where `guided_planner_expires_at < NOW()` AND `guided_planner_status = 'active'` → flips to `'expired'` + emits in-app + email notification (depends on Phase 1 of Install_Sequence_V1 — Vercel Cron OR Cloudflare Cron Triggers). The 3-tier flat-price ladder beats the prior "₱49 × weeks_remaining until event" computation because it removes live computation, anchors stronger (1-Week ₱99 makes ₱1,999 12-Month look like a no-brainer at 61% off), and feels like buying access to a tool rather than signing up for recurring billing. (2) Single-field event creation removes friction for newly-engaged couples who don't yet know their date or venue — those fields are already Guided Planner Steps 1+2 OR easily filled in via Profile after-the-fact, so asking upfront was just a checkpoint. The math also pulls the most likely revenue-positive choice (12-Month flagship at ₱1,999) above the prior weeks-remaining model's typical 6-month payout (₱1,274) — net revenue per Guided customer goes up ~57% at the modal 6-month-out planning runway. | 2026-05-14 | **Caching & Offline Strategy locked.** Hard 100 MB per-user/per-install storage budget gated by `navigator.storage.estimate()` at startup (drops to 50% of available headroom if browser reports < 100 MB free). Allocation: ~75 MB images · ~20 MB JSON/data · ~5 MB headroom; splits soft, LRU within each layer. Two-layer architecture: (1) **Data layer** = TanStack Query + `persistQueryClient` to IndexedDB with stale-while-revalidate, per-query TTL, hard `maxAge` (7 days), and `buster` key bumped per schema change; (2) **Asset layer** = service worker (`apps/web/public/sw.js`) with route-scoped `CacheExpiration` (`maxEntries` + `maxAgeSeconds`) for images (500 entries / 30 days), JS chunks (100 entries / 7 days, stale-while-revalidate), fonts (20 entries / 1 year). **MUST cache:** app shell, JS chunks, fonts, public-read data (events, guests, vendor profiles, mood board, schedule, budget, save-the-date assets). **MUST NEVER cache:** auth tokens, Supabase session, payment intents, BIR receipts, contract files, API-gateway per-request-key responses, live chat (use Realtime). **Invalidation discipline:** every mutation MUST invalidate its query key — enforced via `useTrackedMutation` wrapper around `useMutation` with required `invalidates: QueryKey[]` field + ESLint rule `setnayan/no-raw-mutation` blocking raw `useMutation` in feature code. **Stale-time defaults** (overridable per query): hot lists (guests, day-of schedule) 60s · warm data (vendor profiles, mood board, budget) 5min · cold/immutable (BIR receipts, finalized themes) 1hr. **Eviction:** LRU per layer (assets by `lastAccessedAt`, data by `dataUpdatedAt`); no cross-layer fallback. **Out of scope:** native iOS/Android offline (Phase 2) · photo gallery archive downloads (0009 handles via direct R2 signed URLs + native share, not the PWA cache) · music catalogue files (R2 HTTP cache, not service worker). Canonical spec at `02_Specifications/Caching_and_Offline_Strategy.md`. Acceptance criteria for Sprint 0 validation: cold visit <1.5s on 4G (Lighthouse mobile) · warm return renders cached guest list <100ms before revalidation · quota-hit LRU verified by programmatic 75 MB fill test · schema buster wipes persisted blob on bump · ESLint rule blocks merge on raw `useMutation` · automated audit asserts no auth/payment/receipt/contract content in IndexedDB or SW caches · 200-guest day-of stress test bounds network with 60s staleTime. | Solves two predictable failure modes for return visits to a planning-tool app couples will use over 6–18 months: (1) cold start latency — returning users opening their dashboard shouldn't wait on network to see their guest list, they should see the last-known state in <100ms with background revalidation; (2) unbounded storage bloat — a user who has accumulated a year of vendor portraits, mood-board thumbs, and save-the-date previews shouldn't end up with 500 MB of Setnayan assets eating device storage. Two-layer split keeps eviction reasoning local — image churn doesn't pressure the data cache and vice versa. Sensitive data exclusion (auth, payment intents, BIR receipts, contracts) is non-negotiable for PH legal compliance + RA 10173. Mutation-invalidation wrapper makes the discipline impossible to bypass, which matters because cache-related bugs are notoriously hard to diagnose in production — surfacing them at lint time prevents shipping them. Consumed by iterations 0013 (platform stack) · 0021/0022/0023 (dashboards) · 0031 (day-of guest, offline-first PWA shell) · 0035 (observability emits `cache_layer` + `cache_outcome` metrics to PostHog without including cached content). |
| 2026-05-12 | **Setnayan Team — SHARED Monthly Consumable Pool locked (§ 10b).** ALL Setnayan team members **except the 2 owners** (owner + spouse, who hold unlimited grants under § 10a) draw from a **single shared monthly pool** of in-app service credit — NOT a per-person allowance. Pool size = `min(0.5% × prior-month total platform sales, ₱10,000)` — capped at ₱10,000 TOTAL across the whole team per month. **Use-it-or-lose-it** — unused balance forfeits at month-end; does NOT accumulate. **First-come-first-served** — any eligible team member can spend against the shared remaining balance until it hits zero; once exhausted, the rest of the month is at standard rates for everyone. Applies to Setnayan in-app SKUs only (Save-the-Date, Paparazzi, Live Stream, AI Highlights, Custom Monogram, Broadcast Style, Pro Widgets, etc.); vendor payments through Setnayan Pay charge normally (3% fee applies). Schema: `users.is_team_member = TRUE` flag + singleton table `team_shared_monthly_allowance (period_month YYYY-MM PK, allocated_php, consumed_php, remaining_php, created_at, closed_at)` — one row per month covering the whole team. Per-spend ledger `team_allowance_consumptions (consumption_id, period_month, user_id, order_id, amount_php, created_at)` tracks who spent what for audit. Scheduled job on 1st of each month at 00:00 PHT computes the pool. Admin badge 🟢 ("Team Member · Shared Pool Access"), distinct from owner/spouse 🟣 badge. Pool consumption surfaces in the admin audit log and in a Team Pool widget on team dashboards showing `allocated · consumed · remaining` in real time. Owner-pair (§ 10a) does NOT consume from this pool. | Single shared pool (not per-person) bounds Setnayan's monthly margin exposure at ₱10,000 regardless of how many team members are eligible — adding the 10th admin doesn't multiply the cost. Sales-linked formula aligns team perk with platform growth (the bigger Setnayan gets, the larger the pool, up to cap). First-come-first-served forces the team to coordinate on what's worth comping, which surfaces real internal demand signals. Use-it-or-lose-it discipline prevents unbounded credit accumulation and the operational complexity of carry-forward balances. Keeps the unlimited perk tied to ownership while still giving the team meaningful platform value. |

| 2026-05-14 | **Iteration 0019 — follow-before-message gate + pinned privacy notice locked.** Customer → vendor chat (per 0019_communications) now requires the customer to **follow** the vendor before opening a thread. Follow is **one-way** (no vendor approval), gates **thread-creation only** (an existing thread survives an un-follow; only NEW threads are blocked). Vendor side stays **passive** — vendors cannot cold-DM customers and cannot create threads; they can only reply within existing threads (preserves the customer-mediated privacy invariant from 0006/0022). Enforcement is **3-layer**: (1) UI — `Message` button on `/v/[slug]` and vendor cards is disabled with `Follow first` hint unless `vendor_follows(follower_user_id=auth.uid(), vendor_id=:vendor)` exists; (2) API — `POST /api/v1/messages/threads` checks the follow row and returns `403 FOLLOW_REQUIRED` with `next_action: "follow"`; (3) RLS — Postgres policy on `chat_threads INSERT` requires the follow row as defense in depth. **Schema:** new table `vendor_follows (follower_user_id PK1, vendor_id PK2, followed_at)` + index `vendor_follows_by_vendor (vendor_id, followed_at DESC)`. **Privacy notice — pinned, non-dismissible system message** at the top of every thread (couple-side AND vendor-side mirror, read-only), not part of `chat_messages`, doesn't count toward unread. EN canonical copy: *"All your event info is already in Setnayan — your vendor sees what they need from your profile. Please don't share private info (government IDs, card numbers, full addresses, OTPs, passwords) in chat. If a vendor asks for these, report it via Help."* Locale-resolved per 0015 (TL/CEB strings TBD in implementation). Report flow routes to 0023 admin Help inbox queue. **Acceptance** added to § Acceptance criteria in 0019. **Spec impact:** `0019_communications/0019_communications.md` (Scope + new § Gate + Data model `vendor_follows` table + Acceptance), CLAUDE.md decision log. Code implementation deferred to a fresh `claude/chat-follow-gate` agent worktree — migration + server action + RLS + UI changes on `/v/[slug]`, `/dashboard/[eventId]/messages`, `/vendor-dashboard/messages`. | The 0019 spec already had an implicit anti-spam stance ("couples message vendors after viewing the profile, not anonymous strangers") but it was advisory text, not an enforced gate. With launch approaching, the surface needs a real spam wall before vendors are exposed to thousands of cold messages from couples shopping around. Follow-before-message is the lowest-friction gate that still produces meaningful intent: it's a one-tap discovery action, costs nothing, and lets couples shortlist vendors without committing to a conversation. Booking-before-message would gate too late (couples need to chat to get a quote); requiring vendor approval would create a slow asymmetric DM-request flow that doesn't match how Filipino couples actually discover vendors (which is informal and high-volume). Privacy notice exists because the platform DOES hold the structured info vendors need — addresses for delivery, IDs for contracts, payment via Setnayan Pay — so there's no legitimate reason for vendors to ask for these in free-text chat. Making the notice pinned + non-dismissible (vs. one-time banner) preserves the reminder load-bearing across the months-long planning cycle when a vendor might pressure for off-platform info at week 14. Vendor-passive-cold-DM rule is also a tribute to vendor reputation: a vendor who can't cold-DM cannot be reported for unsolicited contact. | `0019_communications/0019_communications.md`, CLAUDE.md decision log. **`.docx` mirror NOT regenerated** (pandoc not installed in this environment) — flag for next Cowork session. **Implementation pending** — new agent worktree on `claude/chat-follow-gate` branch off `origin/main`. |
