| 2026-05-14 | **Boost-service launch gate locked: 500 verified vendors AND 10,000 active couple accounts.** Boost service does **not** open for signups until **both** thresholds are hit (AND gate). Hitting only one is insufficient — the fair organizer's value depends on real density on both sides (couples to see homepage feature + email blasts; vendors to engage Setnayan-issued discount funnel into the fair). **Pre-gate state:** `/for-vendors` live + converting per §11.1; `/for-event-creators` lives as a **waitlist surface** showing live counters and an interested-fair-organizer signup form; no `/fairs/*` URLs publicly indexed (routes reserved); no homepage featured-fairs strip; waitlist informs initial onboarding cohort once gate opens. **Post-gate state:** `/fairs` index + `/fairs/[fair-slug]` go live; homepage strip activates (capped at 3 per §11.3.1); boost-service signup flow opens; waitlist contacted in signup-order; `/for-event-creators` flips from waitlist to active-signup. **Same thresholds for Model A and Model B in V1** (uniform gate); optional later refinement: open Model A at lower threshold, Model B at higher. **Engineering:** counters `verified_vendor_count` + `active_couple_account_count` updated by daily cron; derived `boost_service_open` flag; admin manual-override allowed for strategic launch partners (e.g., Themes & Motifs deal landing pre-threshold) with reason logged. **Caveat:** thresholds are starting recommendations — owner should validate against real PH fair-operator conversations; smaller regional fairs may engage at lower numbers, majors may need higher. **Updates `[SPEC CHANGE]` flag #3** in playbook §11.6 to include gate logic + `/for-event-creators` waitlist surface (revises earlier §11 framing — pre-gate event-creators DO need a landing page, but as a waitlist surface, not an SEO acquisition page). Full launch-gate spec in playbook §11.7.1. | `02_Specifications/17_SEO_and_AI_Discoverability_Playbook.md` (§11.7.1 added; total now 1,142 lines), CLAUDE.md decision log. **`.docx` mirror NOT regenerated** (pandoc not installed) — flag for next Cowork session. |
| 2026-05-15 | **Dual-role customer ↔ vendor — self-purchase confirm + self-review hard-gate (locked).** Extends "one app, three role-routed entries" (2026-05-11) and the § 10a internal-account pattern (2026-05-12) explicitly to the customer ↔ vendor surface: a single `users` row may carry `account_type='vendor'` AND own/host events as a customer. Vendor application is **additive** — applying never closes the customer surface; event creation, cart, and booking flows stay open and the Switch-view pill toggles between the two roles (same shape as admin = customer-with-admin-grants per § 10a). **Self-purchase confirm step (at checkout).** When any `cart_items` row resolves to a `vendor` whose `owner_user_id = auth.uid()` OR whose `vendor_service_agents.member_id` includes `auth.uid()`, the cart inserts a confirm modal with two CTAs: **"Pay full price"** (standard payment flow, no special treatment, no discount) OR **"Comp for myself"** (skip payment-pending; new `comp_grants.source='vendor_self_comp'`, audit-logged, single-admin authority via 0023, rate-limited to 12 comps per vendor per quarter — admin can raise the cap with reason logged). Self-comp orders are excluded from vendor-side conversion analytics so vendor dogfooding does not inflate their reported numbers. **Self-review HARD-gated at three layers.** (1) **Schema:** CHECK constraint `no_self_review` plus `BEFORE INSERT` trigger `block_related_account_review()` on `vendor_reviews` that refuses the row when reviewer shares ANY of [vendor ownership (`vendors.owner_user_id`), team membership (`vendor_service_agents.member_id`), payment method (matching `service_order_payments.payer_account_number`), device fingerprint (matching new `user_devices.device_hash`), household address (matching new `users.address_normalized`)] with the vendor owner OR any team member. (2) **API:** `POST /api/v1/reviews` catches the trigger exception and returns `403 SELF_REVIEW_BLOCKED` with `matched_signal ∈ {owner_self, team_member, payment_match, device_match, household_match}` and `next_action: "contest_via_help"`. (3) **UI:** the "Leave a review" CTA renders disabled with hint *"You can't review your own services"* on the booked-vendor card when the event flips to Wrap stage; tap-on-disabled opens the appeal flow. **Appeal path.** Filipino households legitimately share GCash numbers, devices, and addresses, so the gate WILL produce false positives. Customer contests via 0023 Help inbox → admin investigates (genuine related-account vs. coincidental shared payer reference) → admin override-publishes the review with reason logged in `admin_audit_log`. Single-admin authority; falls below the two-admin threshold per § 9.1. **Admin parity.** Admins are already customers carrying `is_internal=TRUE` (§ 10a); the self-review gate applies symmetrically — an admin who books a vendor on the customer side cannot review that vendor if the admin sits on the vendor's team. **Schema additions to support the gate:** new table `user_devices (user_id, device_hash, last_seen_at)` (1 row per (user, device) pair, populated on session-start) and new column `users.address_normalized TEXT` (computed from existing address fields, lowercased + whitespace-collapsed for matching; null-safe in the trigger — match never fires on null). | Closes the fake-review fraud vector (vendor astroturfs own product with 5-star reviews) without blocking legitimate self-purchase. A flat ban on self-purchase taxes honest vendors (grand opening dogfooding, internal booking-flow QA, gifting a service to a friend's event) and pushes determined fraudsters to second accounts anyway — harder to detect than a same-account purchase. The surgical fix gates the **review**, not the **purchase**, because the review is where the harm actually lands on the marketplace's decision quality. Hard-gating cross-account at review time (vs. logged-not-gated in moderation) was the owner's call on 2026-05-15: false-positive risk on legit dual-account households is acceptable because (a) the moderation appeal path is cheap (single-admin override-publish via 0023 Help inbox), and (b) the inverse — letting fake reviews land and removing them later — has already polluted the marketplace decision the review was supposed to inform. Confirm modal with two CTAs (vs. flat "always full price") lets vendors self-comp when dogfooding their own checkout, with an audit trail that prevents the comp from becoming a budget hack; the 12/quarter rate-limit anchors the cap somewhere reasonable for a launch vendor and admin can raise it for documented cases. Excluding self-comp orders from vendor analytics keeps reported conversion honest. Mirrors the admin dual-role pattern from § 10a: one `users` row, multiple roles, real customer codepath with safeguards at the harm point rather than at the entry. | `CLAUDE.md` decision log, `0006_vendors_management/0006_vendors_management.md` (Reviews schema: `no_self_review` CHECK + `block_related_account_review()` BEFORE INSERT trigger + new § "Dual-role customer ↔ vendor — review gate"), `0034_payments_and_cart/0034_payments_and_cart.md` (new § 3.1a Self-purchase confirm modal + new § 10c Vendor self-comp grant type + `comp_grants.source` CHECK expanded with `vendor_self_comp` + new `user_devices (user_id, device_hash, last_seen_at)` table + new `users.address_normalized` column), `0021_couple_dashboard_fully_purchased/0021_couple_dashboard_fully_purchased.md` (§ 2.2d table: new row "Self-review (vendor owner / team member viewing own bookings)" — always blocked, appeal copy), `0023_admin_console/0023_admin_console.md` (new § Review moderation queue with related-account signals column + override-publish action under single-admin authority; § 3.3 Payments queue: self-purchase + self-comp flag badges; surface for adjusting per-vendor self-comp quarterly rate-limit). **`.docx` mirrors NOT regenerated** (pandoc unavailable in current environment) — flag for next Cowork session. **Implementation pending** — backend migration (CHECK + trigger + `user_devices` table + `users.address_normalized` column + `comp_grants.source` CHECK extension + `service_order_payments.payer_account_number` column added) and UI (cart confirm modal + disabled review CTA + admin appeal flow + self-comp rate-limit surface) deferred to engineering worktree. |
| 2026-05-15 | **V1 platform expansion — native apps on Windows · macOS · iOS · iPadOS · Android added to launch scope.** Owner directive expands V1 delivery surfaces beyond the prior locked scope (responsive web · Papic-only native iOS/Android per 0012 · day-of PWA shell per 0031) to include **dedicated native applications on five platforms**: Windows desktop, macOS desktop, general-purpose iOS, iPadOS-optimized iPad app, general-purpose Android. Pre-existing Papic mobile apps stay focused on capture; the day-of PWA shell remains as the offline-first browser fallback; the new general-purpose native apps wrap the full dashboard experience. Public marketing site (0015) updated in this commit to advertise multi-platform availability via a new § Section 12 — Available everywhere you plan with download badges and per-store URLs to be filled at submission time. **Recommended build strategy (to be ratified in a new iteration):** Tauri / Electron / React Native shells reusing the Next.js codebase where possible; native Swift / SwiftUI for iOS / iPadOS / macOS only where Tauri/RN can't meet platform-specific UX targets; Kotlin/Android already needed for parity with Papic camera APIs. **Cost layer:** Apple Developer Program ($99/yr · macOS notarization), Microsoft signing cert, Google Play signing, App Store Connect + Google Play Console + Microsoft Store accounts; APNs / FCM / WNS push notification infrastructure added to 0013 platform-stack scope; annual OS-update maintenance burden acknowledged. **Owner accepted the 3–6 month engineering expansion beyond original V1 scope** when explicitly briefed (cost · store submission · signing · update infrastructure · push notifications · maintenance). | Broad platform coverage supports the "no one else comes close" positioning being drafted for the public site (this session). Couples plan from phones + laptops, vendors run business from desktops, and capping V1 at web + Papic-mobile-only under-serves both sides of the marketplace. Strategic intent is to make Setnayan the most accessible Filipino events platform across every device surface a couple or vendor might pick up — laptop on Sunday afternoon planning, phone for guest-list edits in transit, tablet for venue walkthroughs, desktop for vendor calendar management. Native apps also unlock OS-level integrations (file system, reliable push, deep-link handlers) PWA installs can't fully deliver — especially relevant for vendor desktop workflows (calendar sync · proposals · contracts). Web-first installable PWA was the alternative I recommended; owner's call is the broader native delivery — intentionally trades launch-timeline schedule for launch-day platform coverage. | `CLAUDE.md` decision log (this entry); `0015_main_website/0015_main_website.md` (this commit: § "What this iteration ships" count 11 → 12; new § Section 12 — Available everywhere you plan inserted after § Section 11; Routes section unchanged in this pass — store URLs filled at submission time); **FLAGGED for follow-up Cowork sessions:** `0000_app_shell_and_navigation.md` (extend shell strategy beyond web), `0012_papic.md` (ratify capture-only scope vs. new general-purpose mobile apps), `0031_day_of_guest.md` (reconcile PWA shell vs. native app coverage), `0013_platform_stack_and_sync.md` (add APNs / FCM / WNS push notification infrastructure); **NEW iteration `0043_native_apps_delivery` (proposed slug)** — covers Windows + macOS + iOS + iPadOS + Android shells, build strategy (Tauri / Electron / RN vs. fully native), store submission workflow, code signing, update mechanism, push notifications, OS-update maintenance plan. **`.docx` mirrors NOT regenerated** (pandoc unavailable in current environment) — flag for next Cowork session. **Engineering implementation pending** — no native-app code committed in this session; entry establishes scope decision only. |
| 2026-05-15 | **Dual-role public-stats exclusion + role-switch in event switcher (locked, extends earlier 2026-05-15 row).** Third 2026-05-15 row, complements the morning self-purchase/self-review row above. **Three rules.** **(1) Public marketplace count excludes the vendor's own people — broadest definition.** The vendor's public-facing "completed events" credibility number filters out any booking where the buyer is the vendor's `owner_user_id`, anyone in `vendor_service_agents.member_id`, an internal account (`users.is_internal=TRUE`) where that user owns or sits on the team of this vendor, or any order whose `comp_grant_id` resolves to `comp_grants.source='vendor_self_comp'`. Encoded as a materialized view `vendor_public_completed_events_stats` (sibling of `vendor_review_stats`). Hard rule, platform-enforced, no vendor opt-out — same fraud-prevention shape as the self-review block. **(2) Vendor-backend toggle — default OFF (matches public).** The vendor's own dashboard "Completed events" card defaults to the team-excluded number (same as public). A toggle **"Include team bookings"** (per-vendor setting at `vendors.show_team_bookings_in_backend_count BOOLEAN DEFAULT FALSE`) flips it ON to show the unfiltered count via the sibling view `vendor_full_completed_events_stats`. When ON, the card renders the delta inline ("+4 team / internal") and keeps the public-count footnote visible so the vendor admin always knows what the public profile actually shows. Public count is always team-excluded with no toggle. **Personal/customer side is unaffected** — team members' own customer-side dashboard history shows all their events regardless of the vendor's toggle. **(3) Event switcher gets role-switch rows + revised empty state.** Iteration 0000's event-switcher sheet (long-press monogram → bottom sheet on mobile / caret popover on desktop) gains two new role-switch rows: **Shop console** (visible when user is a vendor owner OR sits in any `vendor_service_agents.member_id` row → routes to iteration 0022) and **Admin console** (visible when user has any admin grant → routes to iteration 0023). The role-switch rows complement the global "Switch view" pill from CLAUDE.md 2026-05-11 — pill is always-visible chrome; switcher rows are inside the sheet for vendor admins who think in events. **Empty-state monogram split by role (AMENDS 2026-05-14 chrome row).** Zero events + customer-only → existing `+` create-event circle preserved. Zero events + vendor → monogram renders as the vendor's logo and routes directly to Shop console (no switcher; vendor's home base IS the console). Zero events + admin → monogram renders as the Setnayan admin badge (🟣 disc) and routes directly to Admin console. Multi-role users with at least one event get the standard switcher regardless of which role they're currently in. | Closes the second fake-reputation vector (vendor's own staff bookings inflating their public completed-events) without breaking the dual-role legitimacy from the earlier 2026-05-15 lock — team members are still real customers, their own backend reflects that, only the vendor's PUBLIC profile is filtered. The default-OFF toggle keeps the vendor admin honest about what their public number actually says (turn it ON if they want to see total activity for ops, but the public number doesn't move). Event-switcher role rows acknowledge that vendor admins who live in their shop console still want fast access from any event context — pill is global, switcher rows are local to the "which event am I in" workflow. The zero-event monogram split acknowledges that vendor and admin users may legitimately never create a personal event on Setnayan — their home is the console, not a wedding dashboard, and forcing them through a `+ Add event` empty state is friction for no reason. | `CLAUDE.md` decision log, `0006_vendors_management/0006_vendors_management.md` (new § "Public marketplace stats — completed-events count" with `vendor_public_completed_events_stats` materialized view + `vendor_full_completed_events_stats` sibling view + filter SQL excluding owner / team / internal-tied / self-comp), `0022_vendor_dashboard/0022_vendor_dashboard.md` (new § 2.4a "Completed events card — public vs. private view" with `vendors.show_team_bookings_in_backend_count` toggle + delta-inline display + audit-logged toggle changes), `0000_app_shell_and_navigation/0000_app_shell_and_navigation.md` (chrome § amended at empty-state monogram + event-switcher contents: role-conditional empty-state routing + Shop/Admin console rows inside the switcher). **`.docx` mirrors NOT regenerated** (pandoc unavailable in current environment) — flag for next Cowork session. **Implementation pending** — materialized views + vendor-row toggle column + 0022 card UI + 0000 chrome role-router amendments deferred to engineering worktree. |
| 2026-05-15 | **Public website (0015) Section-by-section spec replaced wholesale with research-grounded ideal-content synthesis; Section 3 stats count-gated; Section 12 platform tiles hide-until-live.** Closes the 2026-05-15 public-website redesign work-stream. Owner requested external research grounding before locking content; a research agent (WebSearch + WebFetch) fetched live `setnayan.com` + 2024–2026 best-practice patterns + 5 reference sites (`stripe.com` / `linear.app` / `airbnb.com` / `zola.com` / `shopify.com`). Resulting synthesis became the new Section-by-section spec. **Three sections dropped from the homepage:** previous § Section 4 (One app, every moving piece — 4-tab walkthrough table folded into new § Section 6 Maria & Juan), § Section 7 (Outsourcing, pacing, scheduling — content moves to `/features` deep-dive), § Section 8 (The vendor flywheel — content moves to `/for-vendors` as vendor-side narrative). **Twelve sections in new order:** 1 Announcement bar · 2 Hero (three-question framing) · 3 Real numbers (count-gated) · 4 The chaos we're fixing · 5 Built for both sides of the celebration · 6 Maria & Juan: see how it works · 7 In-app services · 8 Vendor compatibility & verification · 9 Event-type readiness board · 10 PH coverage map · 11 Dual CTA conversion module + brand-origin footer · 12 Available everywhere you plan. **Stats section gating (Section 3) — AND gate, all 4 thresholds:** `verified_vendor_count >= 100` AND `celebrated_event_count >= 25` AND `active_couple_count >= 1,000` (90-day active) AND `ph_cities_live >= 5`. Pre-threshold renders muted one-line placeholder ("Real Filipino weddings shipping on Setnayan — soon."); post-threshold renders 4-stat strip with floor-rounded "+" suffixed numbers (e.g., "100+ vendors · 25+ events · 1,000+ couples · 5 cities"). Derived flag `stats_section_visible` on new `site_visibility_flags` table; daily cron at 00:00 PHT recomputes; admin manual override with reason logged in `admin_audit_log`. Mirrors the 2026-05-14 boost-service launch-gate pattern. **Platform availability strip (Section 12) revised — hide-until-live tiles (SUPERSEDES the "Coming soon" pills pattern from this morning's V1-platform-expansion entry).** Per-platform `is_visible` flag on new `platform_availability (platform_id PK, store_url, is_visible, published_at)` table; tile renders only when `is_visible=TRUE AND store_url IS NOT NULL`. V1 launch: only Web tile visible; native tiles materialize as App Store / Google Play / Microsoft Store approvals land. Auto-trimming sub-line below tiles enumerates remaining unpublished platforms; disappears entirely after 5 platforms ship. **Cross-cutting standards added to 0015:** mobile-first canvas (PH 80%+ mobile per DataReportal Digital 2024 Philippines); sticky `Start planning · free` button pinned to mobile thumb-zone (Heyflow + Apple HIG); 44–48px tap targets on primary CTAs / 24px floor on secondary (WCAG 2.2 SC 2.5.8); Core Web Vitals budget locked (LCP <2.5s · INP <200ms stretch <100ms · CLS <0.1) tested on mid-tier Android over LTE in Manila/Cebu — not MacBook over Wi-Fi; WCAG 2.2 AA from day one (visible focus SC 2.4.11 · no drag-only SC 2.5.7 · 4.5:1 body / 3:1 large-text + UI-component contrast); Taglish-tolerant voice (EN structural primary, no forced pure-Tagalog formality — matches GCash / BPI / Globe convention); language switcher uses self-names "English · Tagalog · Sugbuanon" (Smartling / Lionbridge / Digital.gov localization best practice; replaces "Cebuano" exonym). **Three-path hero (Section 2) flagged for post-30-day A/B test:** three-question framing is novel — no widely-referenced 2024–2026 marketplace homepage uses three role-paths. Variant B for A/B test if launch traffic supports: two-path framing ("Are you planning an event, or are you a vendor?"). **Header CTA context-aware:** `Create account` routes to couple signup on `/` `/about` `/features` `/vendors`; routes to vendor signup on `/for-vendors`. Label stays "Create account" — only destination changes; Next.js `usePathname()` in shared header. | Three drivers. **First**, the brief had been accumulating quickly across this single session (multi-platform expansion · "no one else comes close" positioning · stats section · dropped pricing on public site) and owner asked for external research grounding before locking content. The research validated more than half the drafted brief (problem-aware three-question hero per CXL awareness research · postpone stats per V1-launch-state reality · hide pricing per Toptal / Vendr precedent · two-page architecture per Airbnb / Shopify · multi-platform strip per Slack / Notion / Linear · single primary + ghost secondary CTA per DesignStudioUIUX), challenged it on cross-cutting standards (mobile-first · CWV budgets · WCAG 2.2 AA · Taglish voice — all absent from the brief), and added concrete patterns (logo-strip placeholder above-the-fold à la Stripe · post-launch swap of readiness board to social proof à la Webstacks · `/for-vendors` polish ≥ main page per Airbnb host-page convention · UI imagery beats stock photography for SaaS-flavored proof — already present in the live Maria & Juan dashboard preview, single biggest don't-break asset). **Second**, stats + platform-tile gating mirrors the 2026-05-14 boost-service launch-gate pattern already canon in this decision log — build the surface, hide it until thresholds are met. Mid-tier thresholds (100 / 25 / 1,000 / 5) sit between aligned-with-boost-gate (500 / 100 / 10,000 / 10 — too conservative; section may never appear in first year) and early-trust (25 / 5 / 250 / 3 — small numbers actively hurt vs. empty placeholder). Mid-tier numbers solidly hit 2–3 digit, look "real," reachable in 6–12 months of organic growth. For platform tiles, "Coming soon" pills aged poorly in observed cases (Slack's early "macOS coming" pill survived in pseudo-state for 18+ months and read as missing rather than promised); hide-until-live keeps the strip honest at every render state. **Third**, the three dropped sections were a section-overlap audit: "One app, every moving piece" duplicated the 4-tab walkthrough that's stronger sitting under the Maria & Juan live demo (new Section 6); "Outsourcing, pacing, scheduling" was generic operations-layer copy that lands cleaner as `/features` deep-dive content for customers who actually want to read it; "The vendor flywheel" was vendor-side narrative that doesn't belong on the couples-led main page — it lands cleaner on `/for-vendors`. The vendor-side narrative migration also matches the research recommendation that `/for-vendors` should be at least as polished as the main page (Airbnb host LTV > guest LTV precedent). | `CLAUDE.md` decision log (this entry); `0015_main_website/0015_main_website.md` (this commit: § "What this iteration ships" bulleted list 12-item rewritten; § Section-by-section spec wholesale replacement — 12 new sections; new Section 3 with `site_visibility_flags` schema; new Section 12 with `platform_availability` schema + auto-trimming sub-line; revised Section 11 single-primary CTA hierarchy; mobile-first / CWV / WCAG 2.2 / Taglish standards embedded throughout); **FLAGGED for follow-up Cowork sessions:** `/for-vendors` full page draft (research recommends polish ≥ main page; covers vendor-side narrative from dropped Section 8 + outcome-led merchant framing per Shopify / Airbnb host page); `/features` page draft (covers content dropped from Section 7 outsourcing/pacing/scheduling — operations-layer deep-dive); `0000_app_shell_and_navigation.md` context-aware header CTA (`Create account` route prefix detection); A/B test framework for three-path vs. two-path hero (variant B post-30-day trigger). **`.docx` mirror NOT regenerated** for `0015_main_website.md` (pandoc unavailable in current environment) — flag for next Cowork session. **Engineering implementation pending** — `site_visibility_flags` table migration, `platform_availability` table migration, daily cron for `stats_section_visible` recompute, Section 6 4-tab walkthrough wiring, sticky-mobile-CTA implementation, hide-until-live tile logic, WCAG 2.2 AA audit pass; deferred to engineering worktree on `Setnayan-App`. **Sequencing note:** This is the THIRD 2026-05-15 row added in a single day (after dual-role self-purchase / self-review gate + V1 platform expansion + dual-role public-stats exclusion). Owner is moving fast on multiple V1-launch-blocking decisions; recommend a reflective Cowork session to consolidate and sequence implementation before engineering starts. |
| 2026-05-15 | **UI Theme system — default accent swapped to burgundy + new fifth theme "Forest Theme" + public marketing site palette now inherits from primary admin's active theme (locked).** Sixth 2026-05-15 row, evolves system **(1) UI Theme** from the 2026-05-12 three-aesthetic-systems lock without disturbing systems (2) Event Palette or (3) Video Template. **Three changes.** **(a) Default theme accent tokens swapped from terracotta to deep burgundy; display name "Setnayan Default" kept (no rename).** New tokens: `--accent: #7A1F2B` (deep burgundy / wine — commitment, intimacy, the rose-gold-to-burgundy palette family dominant in PH weddings since the mid-2020s; intentionally avoids the pink/girlish register), `--accent-deep: #4F1019`, `--accent-soft: #D9B8BD`. Cream base (`#FAF6F0`), ink (`#1A1A1A`), and the Manrope-body / Cormorant-italic-display / DM-Mono editorial typography stack from the prior Setnayan Default are preserved. Icon stroke remains 1.75. Schema CHECK constraint key `'setnayan_default'` is unchanged — no rename, no migration needed. Reconciled with 0015's "no competing accents" discipline by limiting Default to a single burgundy accent — no secondary gold/sage. Previous terracotta tokens (`#C97B4B / #8C4A28 / #E8C9B0`) retired from the default; admin can re-seed terracotta as a custom theme via `brand_config_versions` if desired. **(b) New fifth UI Theme: "Forest Theme" (`[data-theme="forest_theme"]`).** Vendor-grounded / professional register distinct from the romantic register of Default. Tokens: `--page-bg: #F4F0E8` (warm off-cream with subtle green cast), `--surface: #FFFFFF`, `--ink: #1A2520`, `--ink-soft: #5C6660`, `--accent: #2D4A3A` (deep forest — trust, calm, grounds vendor / contract / payment surfaces), `--accent-deep: #1A2F23`, `--accent-soft: #B8C9BF`, `--gold: #C9A66B` (reuses Victorian's existing `--gold` token for a single platform-wide champagne value). Body: Manrope 400/500/600 · Display: Cormorant Garamond italic 500 · Mono: DM Mono 400 (matches editorial restraint of Default, not Victorian's ornate Playfair register). Icon stroke: 1.75. Page-level accent is always forest; gold is constrained to existing tint roles already speced in 0015 § Section 8 (Boosted-vendor gradient · Certified-vendor badge · celebration-affordance moments) — preserves "no competing accents at the page level" discipline. **(c) Public marketing site palette (0015 § Voice) now inherits accent from primary admin's active UI Theme via `brand_config_versions`** (already speced in 0023 § 3.7 for color tokens with two-admin approval per § 9.1). Previously 0015 L151 locked a fixed terracotta accent; that hard-coded accent is replaced with admin-controlled token inheritance, with **Setnayan Default as the seed default**. The platform admin (iteration 0023, NOT couple admin or vendor admin) selects one of the five UI Themes as the platform's brand-active theme; the marketing site reads `brand_config_versions.active_theme_id` and applies the corresponding accent. New customer / vendor accounts without a personal `users.theme_preference` selection inherit the platform's brand-active theme on first dashboard load. **Overlap acknowledged but not collapsed.** Victorian (burgundy `#8B1E3F` + gold leaf + ornate Playfair + flourishes — Bridgerton / heritage) and Setnayan Default (burgundy `#7A1F2B` + modern editorial Cormorant — Aesop / Aman register) share the burgundy color family but are distinguished by hex (one shade deeper / cooler), typography (Playfair 700 italic vs Cormorant italic 500), gold-leaf secondary (Victorian-only), icon stroke (2.25 vs 1.75), and intent (heritage-formal vs modern brand baseline). Classy (`#B8945F` champagne primary) and Forest Theme (`#C9A66B` champagne as constrained secondary tint on forest primary) share the champagne family but differ in base color, typography (Cinzel display vs Cormorant italic), and gold-as-primary vs gold-as-tint role. **Outstanding pre-existing drift partially addressed:** COWORK.md L127 previously listed the fourth theme as "Modern Minimalist" (corpus inconsistency with Theme System Implementation Spec / 0025 schema / Installed_Stack_Inventory all of which name it "iOS"); this edit pass corrects COWORK.md to "iOS" while restating the five themes — flagged as part of this lock rather than deferred to a separate cleanup. | Reconciles owner directive ("make this our default color · burgundy and cream only · forest with champagne gold as another") with the 0015 "no competing accents" editorial discipline and the existing 4-theme system without exploding the surface. Burgundy reads "this is serious / committed" and lands the wedding/life-events emotional register without the saccharine pink default that haunts most wedding-tech UX; forest grounds the planning-anxiety temperature on vendor-side surfaces; cream evokes paper invitations / abaca / lace — premium-Filipino texture rather than sterile SaaS. Default keeping modern editorial typography while Victorian keeps ornate Bridgerton typography preserves the "five distinct theme intents" goal — two themes sharing burgundy doesn't reduce choice if their typography and gold-leaf treatment land in different aesthetic worlds. The five themes now span: modern editorial brand baseline (Default) · heritage-formal Bridgerton (Victorian) · understated champagne luxury (Classy) · system-native iOS (iOS) · vendor-grounded forest + gold (Forest Theme). Wiring the public website palette to the primary admin's setting via `brand_config_versions` unifies aesthetic governance — admin already owns logo / wordmark / tagline / color tokens via 0023 § 3.7 — instead of forking website-palette decisions into a separate edit surface. Two-admin approval gate already applies to brand-mark changes per 0023 § 9.1; the spec extension makes UI Theme switches at the platform level subject to the same gate (the gate did not previously cover theme switches because the public-site palette was hard-coded). | `CLAUDE.md` decision log (this entry); | 2026-05-15 | **Vendor public-visibility state machine + Website editor widget architecture (locked).** Two coupled decisions extending the morning's stack. **(1) Vendor public-visibility correction.** Vendors who have registered but are not yet verified now show on the public marketplace as **"Coming soon"** cards instead of being hidden. New column `vendors.public_visibility ENUM('hidden','coming_soon','verified','archived') DEFAULT 'coming_soon'` (iteration 0022 § 2.1c). States: `coming_soon` (default at registration · profile publicly listed with muted "Coming soon" badge · no booking CTA · read-only preview) → `verified` (set by admin via Verification Queues 0023 § 3.2 · fully bookable) · `hidden` (admin suspension / withdrawal · not surfaced anywhere) · `archived` (terminal · removed from browse · existing relationships preserve referential integrity). DIY-mode marketplace browse popup (0006 § DIY-mode filter popup) gains a **"Verified only"** toggle OFF by default — couples see both states; toggle ON filters to verified-only. **`verified_vendor_count` metric** (Section 3 stats threshold + Section 1 announcement bar auto-hide + boost-service launch gate 2026-05-14) continues to count only `public_visibility='verified'` rows; `coming_soon` vendors are visible publicly but do NOT contribute to the verification-count threshold metrics. State transitions are audit-logged in `admin_audit_log` (action `vendor_visibility_change`). **(2) Widget architecture refactor + admin Website editor (iteration 0023 § 3.10 = 8th admin surface).** The 12 marketing-site sections in 0015 are generalized as widgets in a registry table `site_widgets (widget_id PK, page, display_order, is_enabled, gate_type, config JSONB, updated_at, updated_by_admin_id)`. Page-scoped (`home` · `for_vendors` · `features` · `about` — extensible). Three gate types: `NULL` (always-on widget) · `'count'` (gated by count thresholds — Section 3 stats) · `'per_tile'` (per-tile visibility — Section 12 platforms). **V1 admin-editable fields:** `is_enabled` + `display_order` ONLY. Per-widget config (stats thresholds · platform store URLs · copy overrides · A/B variants) stays code-locked in V1; admin editing of `config` JSONB deferred to V1.1. **Admin Website editor surface at `/admin/website`** (iteration 0023 § 3.10) — list of widgets per page · drag-drop reorder · enable/disable toggle · audit-logged via `admin_audit_log` actions `site_widgets_toggle` / `site_widgets_reorder` · single-admin authority per § 4.3 · mobile lives under "More" tab. **Iteration 0023 admin surface count 7 → 8** (§ 1 table extended with new row 8). **Replaces** the standalone `site_visibility_flags` table from the morning's stats-gating entry — folded into `site_widgets.is_enabled` + the derived `count_gate_passes` check on `gate_type='count'` widgets. `platform_availability` table (Section 12 per-tile detail) stays as sibling table linked logically to `home_platforms` widget by convention. | Two drivers. **First**, the vendor `public_visibility` default shift from `hidden` → `coming_soon` addresses the chicken-and-egg problem of a launching marketplace: pre-verification vendors who completed registration but haven't yet been admin-reviewed need somewhere to land that's neither "fully bookable" nor "invisible." A "Coming soon" surface state lets couples see real coverage forming on the marketplace before the verified vendor pool reaches critical mass, while honestly signaling that the vendor isn't yet bookable. Honest-but-visible beats hidden-until-perfect for two reasons: couples gain confidence the platform is growing; vendors are incentivized to complete verification quickly to flip status. The `verified` count metrics (Section 3 stats gate, announcement bar auto-hide, boost-service launch gate) stay strict — `coming_soon` vendors don't count toward thresholds — so trust signals remain honest. **Second**, the widget architecture refactor was already implicit in the morning's per-section gating tables (`site_visibility_flags` for Section 3, `platform_availability` for Section 12); generalizing it to all 12 sections gives admins a single place to manage marketing-site composition without separate per-section toggles in code. Limiting V1 admin scope to `is_enabled` + `display_order` (not config / copy / variants) keeps the editor surface tight while solving the immediate need (turn sections off when they're not yet relevant; reorder for A/B if needed). The decision NOT to support per-widget config editing in V1 is deliberate — keeping the iteration spec as the source of truth for content means engineering doesn't have to build a "marketing CMS" that competes with spec authority. Editor surface at iteration 0023 § 3.10 fits the existing admin console pattern (one URL prefix `/admin/[surface]` per admin function); mobile "More" tab handles the surface-count growth without forcing a 5+ tab bottom nav. | `CLAUDE.md` decision log (this entry); `0022_vendor_dashboard/0022_vendor_dashboard.md` (vendors table: new `public_visibility` column + new § 2.1c Vendor public-visibility state machine); `0006_vendors_management/0006_vendors_management.md` (§ DIY-mode filter popup: new "Verified only" toggle OFF by default with coming-soon vendor UX note); `0015_main_website/0015_main_website.md` (new § Widget architecture with `site_widgets` schema + seed data table + widget render logic + supersedes note for `site_visibility_flags`); `0023_admin_console/0023_admin_console.md` (§ 1 admin surface count 7 → 8 + new row 8 Website editor; new § 3.10 Website editor with UI sketch + audit log + mobile placement). **`.docx` mirrors NOT regenerated** (pandoc unavailable in current environment) — flag for next Cowork session. **Engineering implementation pending** — `vendors.public_visibility` column migration with backfill of existing rows to `'verified'` (preserve current public state); state-transition admin actions in 0023 Verification Queues; `site_widgets` table migration + seed data INSERT; daily cron extension to compute `count_gate_passes` per gated widget; admin Website editor UI in 0023 (`/admin/website` route + drag-drop component + toggle handlers + audit-log writers); DIY-browse "Verified only" toggle UI + filter logic in 0006; deferred to engineering worktree on `Setnayan-App`. **Sequencing note:** This is now the **SIXTH** 2026-05-15 row added to the decision log today (after dual-role self-purchase / self-review gate · V1 platform expansion · dual-role public-stats exclusion · public-website wholesale redesign · UI theme system rebrand · this vendor visibility + widget refactor entry). The cumulative engineering surface for these six decisions is substantial; the recommendation from prior 2026-05-15 entries for a reflective Cowork session to consolidate today's stack of locks before engineering starts holds more firmly than ever. |
| 2026-05-15 | **Event lifecycle locked: no self-serve event deletion · multi-event creation supported.** Seventh 2026-05-15 row. Users cannot delete an event from any user-facing UI (couple dashboard, profile settings, event switcher, vendor or admin role-routed dashboards). The deletion path is **admin-only** — couples / customers needing removal contact support, who escalates to the admin console Delete action (0023, PR #9). Generalizes the rule already implied by 0025 § "Active event within next 30 days" (which previously gated only on imminent events with the copy *"You have an active wedding scheduled on [date]. Contact support to discuss deletion"*) — that contact-support path now applies regardless of how soon the event is, not only the 30-day window. **Adding additional events remains supported** via the event switcher's `+ Add event` row already speced in 0000 § event switcher. V1 keeps the event-type picker restricted to **Wedding only**; the other five tiles (baptism · debut · birthday · anniversary · religious_event) stay visible with a "Coming soon" badge until iteration 0041 (Multi-Event Vendor Catalog) ships in V1.5. | Two drivers. **First**, an event aggregates months of planning, vendor contracts, guest data, paid render outputs, and (often) third-party deposits; an irreversible self-delete is too dangerous to expose. Support-mediated removal forces a deliberate human-in-the-loop checkpoint and matches the lifecycle pattern already established for account deletion in 0025 (delete is gated behind an explicit support conversation when an active event exists). **Second**, multi-event creation is core to the "Filipino-first life-events platform" positioning — the same household plans wedding → baptism → anniversary on a single account; locking that affordance now (even though only Wedding is currently selectable on the event-type picker) prevents future spec drift toward single-event-per-account assumptions and keeps the existing 0000 `+ Add event` switcher row authoritative. | `CLAUDE.md` decision log (this entry); `0021_couple_dashboard_fully_purchased.md` (new § 9 "Event lifecycle — delete vs. add (locked 2026-05-15)" inserted before § 7 Companions); `0025_profile_settings.md` (no edit this pass — existing § "Active event within next 30 days" copy is consistent with the generalized rule and stays the canonical support-contact prompt); auto-memory: new `project_setnayan_event_lifecycle.md` + MEMORY.md index row. **`.docx` mirrors NOT regenerated** (`which pandoc` returns not found in current environment) — flag for next Cowork session; files needing mirror regen: `CLAUDE.md`, `0021_couple_dashboard_fully_purchased.md`. **Engineering implementation pending** — the no-delete half requires nothing built (the admin Delete action already ships per PR #9). The multi-event-add half has confirmed build gaps discovered in this same session — see BUILD GAPS below. **BUILD GAPS (discovered 2026-05-15 same session, visually confirmed in production by owner screenshot):** **(A)** 0000's event-switcher entry-point — the per-couple monogram → caret ▾ affordance — is **NOT wired in production**; shipped UI anchors the top-left with the global Setnayan brand logo (`[S] SETNAYAN` wordmark) instead of the per-couple monogram, so the user has no path to reach the `+ Add event` row from the chrome. Tapping the back-arrow on the `← Maria & Juan (Demo)` pill below the brand logo does NOT lead to an events index / add-event surface either, so multi-event creation is unreachable in production despite being speced in 0000 § event switcher. **(B)** The top-nav is rendered as **two stacked rows** in production (row 1: `[S SETNAYAN] ............... [Sign out]`; row 2: `[← Maria & Juan (Demo)] .......... [🔔] [I-avatar]`), violating the **2026-05-14 "Top-nav redesign locked + token-wallet pill removed from chrome" decision-log row** which mandated a *single persistent strip* with the monogram on the left and the avatar / notifications on the right. Both gaps live in iteration 0000 chrome. Together they make the "adding events is supported" half of this 2026-05-15 lock **paper-only** as of today — the rule is in the spec, but no user can reach it. **Engineering follow-up needed:** reconcile 0000 shipped chrome with (i) 0000 § event switcher (wire the per-couple monogram + caret + event switcher dropdown including the `+ Add event` row); (ii) the 2026-05-14 single-strip top-nav lock (collapse the two stacked rows into one persistent strip); OR amend both specs if the shipped two-row chrome is the new intent. Also flag for `App_Build_Status.md` row 39 (0000) — status downgraded from "✅ Shipped" to "✅ Shipped (chrome drift)" with both gaps enumerated. The no-self-delete half of this lock stands on its own — it's a do-not-build rule, no UI dependency, unaffected by these chrome gaps. **Sequencing note:** This is the SEVENTH 2026-05-15 row added to the decision log today; the recommendation from prior 2026-05-15 entries for a reflective Cowork session to consolidate today's stack of locks before engineering starts holds. The newly-discovered 0000 chrome drift is logged here (not as a separate eighth row) because it's a build-status correction against existing locks, not a new decision. |
| 2026-05-16 | **0024 Save-the-Date reframed from video-render SKU to free landing-page Phase 1 hero · 0004 widget pricing reset · render pipeline (item #6) eliminated.** Owner walkthrough item #6 closed without a Cloudflare Containers / Fly.io / browser MediaRecorder render-pipeline decision because the entire 0024 video-file primitive is retired. **(1) Save-the-Date reframe.** The ₱99 Save-the-Date Render SKU is retired. 0024's three-aspect-ratio MP4 render pipeline (FFmpeg + R2 + ZIP per 0024 § 5) is retired. 0024 collapses into iteration 0002 as the **Phase 1 hero layout** of the lifecycle-phased landing page already speced in 0024 § 5a (Phase 1 Save-the-Date → Phase 2 Invitation → Phase 3 Logistics → Phase 4 Post-event). The landing page itself is **free for every event** at `setnayan.com/{couple-slug}`, one page per event. Phase 1 hero is web-tech only — animated trace SVG monogram + countdown + calendar-add + Open Graph metadata + music — no FFmpeg, no R2 render storage, no aspect-ratio multiplexing. **(2) 0004 widget pricing reset.** All 11 0004 widgets ship Basic-tier free in V1. Paid-tier upgrades in V1 reduced to **two**: **(a) Monogram Hero ₱1,999 no-refund** — renamed from "Pro Monogram animation" — bundles (i) animated SVG-stroke trace reveal of the monogram on page load (each path animates `stroke-dasharray` + `stroke-dashoffset`, staggered timing ~3s end-to-end, cubic-bezier easing for organic pen-drawn feel), (ii) custom video OR photo background for the monogram hero section (video: 15–30s MP4 H.264, max 30MB, autoplay-muted, no controls; photo: JPG/PNG max 5MB, ≥1920×1080 recommended; either renders behind monogram via `object-fit: cover`). **SVG-only: PNG monogram uploads rejected at upload time for the Monogram Hero upgrade** (the trace animation cannot be path-driven on rasterized assets — no PNG fade-in fallback). **All sales final, no refund window** (apparatus-rule clean — one purchase = one tool unlock). **(b) Live Schedule ₱999** — re-priced from ₱99 → ₱999. Feature unchanged ("Happening now" highlight + auto-scroll on the Schedule widget). **Retired V1 SKUs:** `pro_widget_story` (Our Story scroll parallax — folded to free as zero-marginal-cost); `pro_widget_bundle` ₱199 (bundle math doesn't work with 2 widgets at vastly different price points — couples pick what matters); `save_the_date_render` ₱99 (feature gone). **Future V1.5+ widget additions reserved (price inherits from parent iteration):** Panood widget (when 0011 ships), Papic widget (when 0012 ships), Patiktok widget (when 0017 ships) — widget type not pre-seeded; added to `invitation_widgets` registry only when parent iteration activates. **V1.1+ paid upgrade pricing for the remaining 9 widgets (Greeting · Our Story · Countdown · QR Code · RSVP · Event Details · Venue · Dress Code · Photo Moments)** — TBA post-launch once V1 usage data identifies which widgets convert. **(3) Item #6 status: closed without a render-pipeline decision.** Cloudflare Containers vs Fly.io vs browser MediaRecorder no longer relevant — page is web tech only. Save-the-Date "social-native clip" (15s teaser MP4 for IG Reels / TikTok) deferred to V1.1+ as an optional add-on; couples in V1 share the landing-page link (Open Graph card renders beautifully on FB / WhatsApp; lower reach on IG / TikTok accepted as V1 launch tradeoff). | Three drivers. **First**, the video-file primitive was the wrong fit for the actual job-to-be-done. Couples don't want a video — they want guests to literally save the date. A landing-page hero with one-click calendar-add, soft-RSVP intent, and reminders solves the JTBD better than an MP4 that gets watched once and scrolled past. **Second**, the original 0024 spec already imagined the landing page as one lifecycle-phased surface (§ 5a) — the reframe just deletes the unnecessary MP4 intermediary and makes Phase 1 a first-class layout instead of "the place where the rendered MP4 embeds." Free landing page also becomes a marketing surface — every shared invitation link is a Setnayan-hosted page advertising the platform. **Third**, the original 0004 Pro tier (3 widgets at ₱99 each + ₱199 bundle) under-priced the Monogram Hero feature relative to its actual value (a couple paying ₱1,999 for a designer-grade animated trace + custom video hero gets a fundamentally different visual product than a free page; PH monogram designers charge ₱2K–10K for static work, so ₱1,999 for animation + video background is market-appropriate). The 10× re-price on Schedule (₱999) similarly captures the wedding-day moment-of-glory value — Live Schedule glows at the venue when "Cake Cutting" is happening NOW, and that emotional payoff is worth more than ₱99. Dropping Our Story Pro to free aligns with the long-standing apparatus-rule sub-principle that zero-marginal-cost client-side rendering effects shouldn't be paywalled. Revenue model shift: original ₱99 STD × ~2 renders × 1,000 weddings = ~₱198K/mo line is replaced by two premium V1 SKUs (₱1,999 + ₱999) with lower attach rate but higher ASP per converted couple, plus the V1.1+ widget upgrade tier opening 9 more revenue surfaces post-launch — net likely higher long-term ARPU. | `CLAUDE.md` decision log (this entry); `0024_save_the_date/0024_save_the_date.md` (gutted to a thin redirect pointing at 0002 Phase 1; preserves only historical context for the retired SKU); `0002_qr_invitation_system/0002_qr_invitation_system.md` (formalizes the four-phase lifecycle as first-class hero layouts; absorbs § 5a from 0024); `0004_invitation_widgets/0004_invitation_widgets.md` (widget pricing table rewritten — Monogram Hero ₱1,999 row + Live Schedule ₱999 row + Our Story dropped from Pro + Pro Bundle retired + 3 reserved-for-V1.5+ rows; `hero_monogram.config_json` schema extended with `pro_background_type` / `pro_background_photo_r2_key` / `pro_background_video_r2_key` / `pro_background_video_poster_r2_key` / `pro_background_uploaded_at` / `pro_background_uploaded_size_bytes` fields; SVG-only validation rule added; no-refund clause added to Pro purchase flow; SKU code rename `pro_widget_hero` → `monogram_hero_upgrade`); `App_Build_Status.md` (0024 row flipped to "✅ Folded into 0002 Phase 1; render pipeline retired"); `API_Integration_Checklist.md` (Tier 5.1 Save-the-Date render-pipeline row removed); `V1_Gap_Analysis_Status.md` (0024 + 0004 rows updated to reflect reframe). **`.docx` mirrors NOT regenerated** (`which pandoc` returns not found in current environment per 2026-05-15 standing flag) — flag for next Cowork session; files needing mirror regen: `CLAUDE.md`, `0024_save_the_date.md`, `0002_qr_invitation_system.md`, `0004_invitation_widgets.md`. **Engineering implementation pending** — 0024 render-pipeline code (FFmpeg + R2 + queue handlers) never built, nothing to remove; "All sales final" disclosure for Monogram Hero added to 0034 checkout copy; deferred to engineering worktree on `Setnayan-App`. **Sequencing note:** This is the first 2026-05-16 row — closes item #6 of the owner walkthrough started 2026-05-15. | 2026-05-16 | **Monogram Hero PNG-to-SVG conversion via Potrace · amends earlier SVG-only-hard-block stance.** Second 2026-05-16 row, amendment to the first. The earlier entry's *"PNG monogram uploads rejected at upload time for the Monogram Hero upgrade"* hard block is **softened** to a **hybrid policy with preview gate**: PNG uploads are accepted, server-side Potrace conversion runs, the couple sees a preview of the converted SVG + trace animation, and the couple explicitly approves the converted version before checkout proceeds. **Locked flow (6 steps):** (1) Couple in Monogram Hero checkout, has PNG monogram. (2) Editor offers "Convert your PNG to SVG so it can animate? We'll show you the result first." (3) Couple taps Convert → server runs Potrace via Cloudflare Worker (WASM build) → ~2 second wait. (4) Preview screen: side-by-side original PNG + animated SVG trace preview (looping ~3s). (5) Three CTAs: **Use this SVG · Try a different file · Use auto-generated instead**. (6) On *Use this SVG*: checkout proceeds with the converted SVG stored as canonical (`hero_monogram.converted_svg_url`); **PNG original kept as backup** under `hero_monogram.uploaded_url` for audit / archive / future re-conversion if Potrace settings improve. **Engine choice: Potrace (open-source).** Wrapped as a Cloudflare Worker WASM function. Free per conversion (~₱0 marginal cost). Mature, battle-tested (Inkscape's Trace Bitmap is built on it). Quality 80–90% on high-contrast monogram-style content. Alternatives considered + rejected: Vectorizer.ai API (~₱11/conversion, vendor lock-in), ImageTracerJS (lower quality), Adobe Image Trace API (overkill + expensive). **No-refund clause still stands** for the Monogram Hero SKU — the preview gate IS the consent mechanism (couple sees exactly what they're paying for before commit), so all-sales-final remains morally defensible. **Schema additions to `hero_monogram.config_json`:** `converted_svg_url` (R2 path to Potrace output), `converted_svg_approved_at` (ISO timestamp of couple's preview-gate approval), `converted_svg_potrace_settings` (threshold / turdsize / optcurve / opttolerance — captured for reproducibility if we re-run conversion with improved defaults). **API integration:** Potrace WASM build deployed as a Cloudflare Worker function (`/api/monogram/convert`); reads PNG from R2 `setnayan-media`, writes SVG to same bucket under `hero_monogram/{event_id}/converted.svg`, returns preview-renderable URL. Engineering effort ~3 days (Potrace WASM compile + Worker wiring + preview-gate UI in the upgrade flow + acceptance approval write to schema). | The earlier hard-block was the right *default* but unnecessarily restrictive on what is technically possible. Many couples receive their monogram as PNG from their wedding-branding designer (the designer keeps the SVG source); forcing those couples to either (a) go back to the designer for SVG, (b) use auto-generated instead, or (c) skip the ₱1,999 upgrade entirely all leak conversion. The preview gate protects the quality bar without locking out couples who only have PNG — they self-select away from bad conversions because they see the result before paying. Potrace specifically (vs the commercial alternatives) keeps marginal cost at ₱0, which protects the 95%+ margin on the ₱1,999 SKU. The 10% of conversions that look mediocre will be revealed at the preview gate and rejected by the couple before payment; the no-refund clause therefore stays clean. PNG-as-backup is a low-cost safety net (PNG is small; R2 storage cost negligible) that enables future re-conversion if we improve Potrace settings or add a better engine in V1.1+. | `CLAUDE.md` decision log (this entry); `0004_invitation_widgets/0004_invitation_widgets.md` (`hero_monogram` schema extended with conversion fields; Monogram Hero purchase flow rewritten to surface the 6-step preview-gate path instead of the SVG-required hard-block; the earlier *"SVG-only: PNG monogram uploads rejected at upload time"* line replaced with the hybrid policy; acceptance criteria updated to test the conversion + preview-approval flow); `API_Integration_Checklist.md` (new Tier 5 entry — Potrace WASM build + Cloudflare Worker deploy + R2 routing); ~~`0002_qr_invitation_system/0002_qr_invitation_system.md`~~ no edit needed (mentions "SVG-only" only in passing reference to 0004's rules). **`.docx` mirrors NOT regenerated** (pandoc unavailable). **Engineering implementation pending** — Potrace WASM compile (npm `potrace-wasm` exists; verify size + license); Cloudflare Worker `/api/monogram/convert` endpoint; preview-gate UI component in the Monogram Hero checkout step (side-by-side PNG vs animated SVG trace); schema migration for the 3 new `hero_monogram` config fields. **Sequencing note:** Amends the first 2026-05-16 row's *"SVG-only no fallback"* line; both rows together describe the final V1 Monogram Hero policy. |
| 2026-05-16 | **Papic V1.5+ architecture lock — 207-camera mesh · Drive transfer at T+30d · cold-tier 90-day safety window · 150-credit/guest · Auto-Recap (FFmpeg no-AI) · pooled credit pool 5K/10K · ALL Papic SKUs deferred to V1.5+ but architecture frozen now.** Third 2026-05-16 row. Papic V1 native iOS/Android build is **deferred to V1.5+** (no V1 engineering bandwidth for native apps) but the architecture is **locked now** so the V1.5+ build cannot re-litigate decisions already argued through. **(1) 207-camera per event** = 5 paid paparazzi + 200 guest cameras + couple seat; each guest gets **150 captured-photo credits**, bundled free in the Premium Guest Camera Pack. **(2) Drive transfer at T+30d** — couple OAuths their Google account; Setnayan pushes the full archive (photos, videos, Auto-Recap, XMP/EXIF metadata) to the couple's `Setnayan/[Event Name]/` folder root. Couple owns the archive from T+30d onward. **(3) Cold-tier 90-day safety window** — Setnayan retains a cold-tier R2 mirror from T+30d through T+120d (cuts long-tail storage cost ~80% vs hot-tier indefinite retention); after T+120d cold-tier lapses and the couple's own Drive is canonical. **(4) XMP/EXIF tag embedding on Drive transfer** — face-tags, table tags, capture timestamp, geo coordinates, photographer ID embedded into XMP sidecars per-photo + EXIF UserComment where supported, so Lightroom / Photo Mechanic / Capture One / Photos.app read metadata natively; couple's archive is queryable without Setnayan in the loop. **(5) Auto-Recap (FFmpeg, NO AI · free with Premium Guest Camera Pack)** — 60-90 second highlight reel rendered T+24h post-event using deterministic ordering (capture timestamp + photo-quality heuristic: sharpness + face-count + exposure-curve median); no Anthropic / no scene-prompt costs; music = Setnayan-owned AI track; downloadable from couple dashboard + bundled into Drive transfer. **(6) Folder structure:** `Setnayan/[Event Name]/00_Cover/01_Pre-event/02_Ceremony/03_Reception/04_Auto-Recap/` (phase-bucketed for Lightroom-style ingest; Cover = best-of-show; Auto-Recap = the 60-90s reel). **(7) File naming:** `{couple-slug}_{ISO-8601 capture timestamp}_{photographer-id}_{capture-id}.{ext}` (e.g. `maria-juan_2026-08-15T14-32-08+0800_p3_c00421.jpg`) — sorts chronologically by photographer per session. **(8) Pooled credit pool per event:** 5,000 credits for 3-Paparazzi pack · 10,000 for 5-Paparazzi pack — paid paparazzi **share the pool** (matches event reality where one shoots heavy in ceremony while another shoots lighter in cocktail; per-paparazzo quotas would force premature shutter-discipline in high-value windows). **(9) Soft warning at 80% pool utilization** + extension SKU **₱299 / +1,000 credits** (`paparazzi_credits_addon`, multi-purchase) lets couple top up live. **V1.5+ SKU table (deferred build, locked pricing):** `paparazzi_3_seats` ₱1,499 (5K pool · 3 paparazzi · 200 guest cams · 150 credits/guest), `paparazzi_5_seats` ₱2,499 (10K pool · 5 paparazzi · 200 guest cams · 150 credits/guest), `paparazzi_camera_addon` ₱999 (one extra paid paparazzi seat · multi-purchase — repriced from prior ₱1,499 Pro Camera Bridge), `paparazzi_credits_addon` ₱299/+1,000 pool credits (multi-purchase), `premium_guest_camera_pack` ₱1,499 event-wide (every guest gets Lifetime Archive + Drive sync + Auto-Recap + watermark-free downloads + HD video upload — single event-wide flag replaces per-guest gating), `personal_album_per_guest` ₱49 (digital album · opt-in per guest · low-friction upsell), `memory_book_per_guest` ₱249 (printable hardcover memory book PDF · opt-in per guest · higher-touch upsell). Pro Camera Bridge for DSLR pairing rolls into the paid-paparazzi seat's capability set (no longer a standalone SKU). | Two drivers. **First**, V1 ships without native iOS / Android engineering bandwidth; locking architecture now prevents the V1.5+ build from re-litigating "decide once, build many times" decisions (camera count, Drive-as-canonical-archive, cold-tier safety window, XMP/EXIF embedding, Auto-Recap-without-AI). **Second**, the pooled-credit model matches real-event capture patterns; the 5K / 10K pool sizes are calibrated for Filipino-wedding capture counts (typical 200-guest event = 3K-5K paparazzi shots; pool gives 1.5-2× headroom). 80% soft-warning + ₱299/+1K extension handles overruns without forcing another full paparazzi pack purchase. Auto-Recap stays FFmpeg-only / no-AI to keep marginal cost ~₱0 per recap; bundled with Premium Guest Camera Pack (₱1,499 event-wide) it is an attractive moment without a separate per-event charge. Personal Album (₱49) + Memory Book (₱249) per-guest upsells convert engaged-guest spend that would otherwise leak to off-platform photo-printing services. **Tax-tier note:** All V1.5+ Papic SKUs net ~65-72% margin under V1 tax tier (PT 3% + LBT 1% + Income 25%); cold-tier R2 + 90-day window cuts long-tail storage cost ~80% vs indefinite hot-tier retention. | `CLAUDE.md` decision log (this entry); `0012_papic/0012_papic.md` (new top-of-file § "V1.5+ Architecture Lock — locked 2026-05-16" inserted before § What this iteration ships: 207-camera mesh + Drive transfer + cold-tier 90-day window + XMP/EXIF + Auto-Recap + folder structure + file naming + pooled-credit-pool + soft-warning + V1.5+ SKU table); `0034_payments_and_cart/0034_payments_and_cart.md` (`service_catalog` seed extended with `paparazzi_camera_addon` 99900, `paparazzi_credits_addon` 29900, `premium_guest_camera_pack` 149900, `personal_album_per_guest` 4900, `memory_book_per_guest` 24900 + `paparazzi_*_seats` rows + 5 new rows all marked V1.5+ with comment "architecture locked 2026-05-16"); `App_Build_Status.md` (0012 row note updated to architecture lock); `V1_Gap_Analysis_Status.md` (0012 row appended). **`.docx` mirrors NOT regenerated** (pandoc unavailable — standing flag since 2026-05-15). **Engineering implementation pending** — entire Papic V1.5+ build remains queued; this entry is the build-time bible. **Marketplace commission row INTENTIONALLY EXCLUDED from this commit batch** (still in active Cowork discussion · separate commit pending). **Sequencing note:** Third 2026-05-16 row. |
| 2026-05-16 | **Panood V1 SKU lock — drop Cloudflare Stream Live composite · BYO YouTube via OAuth · per-day pricing (Daily Broadcast ₱499 · Camera Sync ₱99) · Annual Streaming ₱2,999 / Annual Streaming Plus ₱3,999.** Fourth 2026-05-16 row. **Architecture pivot:** drop the Cloudflare Stream Live SFU + server-side ffmpeg compositor + RTMP relay through Setnayan's master `@SetnayanWeddings` channel. Replace with **couple's own YouTube channel via OAuth** — couple grants Setnayan YouTube Live write permission to *their* channel, Setnayan provides the broadcaster web UI + multi-cam switching + auto landing-page embed, but the video stream lives on the couple's YouTube forever. Setnayan never composites or relays; the broadcaster UI pushes one active feed at a time to the couple's YouTube via standard RTMP from the currently-selected camera, and multi-cam switching is the operator tapping between phone feeds inside the Setnayan broadcaster UI. **(1) Daily Broadcast** ₱499/day (per-day multi-purchase — couples buy as many days as they need: prep day + ceremony day + reception day = 3 days × ₱499 = ₱1,497). **(2) Camera Sync** ₱99/day (Panood multi-cam only — unlocks the Setnayan broadcaster UI's multi-cam switching for that day; without it a Daily Broadcast is single-cam). **(3) Annual Streaming** ₱2,999/year (single-cam unlimited days for one year — vendor-friendly for vendors who livestream regularly to their own YouTube). **(4) Annual Streaming Plus** ₱3,999/year (multi-cam unlimited days for one year). **Retired from prior spec:** `live_stream_base` ₱2,499 + `live_stream_camera_addon` ₱999 + `live_stream_hour_addon` ₱999 + `broadcast_style_pack` ₱2,999 — all features that depended on Cloudflare-composite (lower-thirds, scene cards, 4-mode style switching, ffmpeg compositor overlays) are no longer available because the composite step is gone. Couples wanting the old style-pack experience use YouTube's own built-in production tools or third-party OBS-style apps writing to their YouTube. **Architecture impact:** Setnayan iteration 0011 becomes a **thin broadcaster orchestration layer** (multi-cam phone client connecting to YouTube via the couple's OAuth + landing-page IFrame embed of the couple's YouTube Live URL) rather than a media compositor. Custom Monogram Pack (₱1,999) still applies for landing-page chrome but no longer touches the broadcast video itself (couple sets their own YouTube channel branding). Same-Day Edit (₱24,999) SKU dependency on Cloudflare-Stream-recorded feeds is severed; SDE now reads from the couple's YouTube archive via OAuth + Papic photo stream — engineering re-scope needed at V1.5+ SDE build time. AI Video Highlight (₱999/60s) and AI Edited Highlight (₱2,999/3min) similarly consume the couple's YouTube archive. **Day-of-build engineering surface drops by ~60%** — no Cloudflare Stream Live ingest, no SFU subscription, no server-side ffmpeg compositor with monogram overlay, no RTMP relay to a master Setnayan channel; broadcaster web UI + landing-page IFrame embed are the entire V1 build. | Three drivers. **First**, composite costs were the largest variable-cost line in the Panood economics: Cloudflare Stream Live ingest at ~$1 per 1,000 minutes per camera (5 cams × 5 hrs × 60min = 1,500 minutes × ~₱60 = ~₱90 per typical event), plus server-side ffmpeg compositor compute, plus RTMP relay. Pushing the composite to YouTube (where it is free at unbounded scale) and shifting Setnayan's role to broadcaster orchestration drops the variable cost line to near zero. **Second**, the couple-owns-the-video model resolves a long-standing tension around "what happens when Setnayan eventually wraps or rebrands or rotates the @SetnayanWeddings channel?" — by storing the video on the couple's own YouTube, the broadcast is durable independent of Setnayan's platform lifecycle. **Third**, per-day pricing aligns with how couples actually plan multi-day Filipino weddings (prep day + ceremony + reception are often 3 distinct days at distinct venues); buying coverage day-by-day is more honest than a 3-hour base + hour-addons model that mismatches a 2-day or 3-day event. The Annual SKUs (₱2,999 / ₱3,999) open a vendor-side ARR line — vendors who livestream their own portfolio events year-round have a single-purchase option that's break-even after ~6 days/year of use. **Tax-tier note:** Daily Broadcast nets ~67-72% margin under V1 tax tier; Annual SKUs net ~70-72% (higher because COGS is amortized across many broadcasts per year). | `CLAUDE.md` decision log (this entry); `0011_panood/0011_panood.md` (Pricing section — base + add-ons table rewritten to the 4 new SKUs: Daily Broadcast ₱499/day, Camera Sync ₱99/day, Annual Streaming ₱2,999/year, Annual Streaming Plus ₱3,999/year · Cloudflare-composite SKUs `live_stream_base` + `live_stream_camera_addon` + `live_stream_hour_addon` + `broadcast_style_pack` marked RETIRED · "Delivery architecture" section rewritten from Cloudflare Stream Live + ffmpeg compositor + master channel to BYO YouTube via OAuth · Pipeline subsection retired · Setnayan's master YouTube channel subsection retired · Embed implementation simplified to IFrame embed of couple's YouTube Live URL · Pro Camera Bridge cross-iteration reference to 0012 preserved · SDE engineering re-scope flagged at V1.5+); `0034_payments_and_cart/0034_payments_and_cart.md` (`service_catalog` seed: retire `live_stream_base` / `live_stream_camera_addon` / `live_stream_hour_addon` / `broadcast_style_pack` rows · add `panood_daily_broadcast` 49900, `panood_camera_sync_daily` 9900, `panood_annual_streaming` 299900, `panood_annual_streaming_plus` 399900 rows);
