`App_Build_Status.md` (0011 row note updated to BYO YouTube SKU lock); `V1_Gap_Analysis_Status.md` (0011 row appended). **`.docx` mirrors NOT regenerated** (pandoc unavailable). **Engineering implementation pending** — broadcaster web UI (multi-cam preview + tap-to-switch + active-feed RTMP push to YouTube), couple's YouTube OAuth handshake (one-time, persisted), landing-page IFrame embed using couple's `liveBroadcasts.id`, per-day SKU activation + spend recording, Annual SKU year-counter logic, retirement migration for the 4 Cloudflare-composite SKU rows. **Marketplace commission row INTENTIONALLY EXCLUDED from this commit batch.** **Sequencing note:** Fourth 2026-05-16 row. |
| 2026-05-16 | **Save-the-Date Video SKU ₱99/render reintroduced as a downloadable MP4 product · distinct from the retired ₱99 Save-the-Date Render SKU · drives traffic to the free Phase 1 landing page.** Fifth 2026-05-16 row. **Reintroduces** a Save-the-Date Video product at ₱99/render, but **explicitly distinguishes it from the retired `save_the_date_render` SKU** killed in the first 2026-05-16 row of this day. The retired SKU was a multi-aspect-ratio composite of the couple's landing-page hero (3 outputs: 16:9 + 1:1 + 9:16) rendered from the page's animated SVG monogram + countdown — that whole pipeline is gone. **This is a different product:** a **generic-template AI-auto-edit short MP4** generated from **5-10 engagement photos** uploaded by the couple, scored with **Setnayan-owned music** from the AI catalogue, output as a single polished **30-60 second vertical MP4** sized for social sharing (IG Reels, TikTok, FB stories). Couple downloads the MP4 directly; share-flow nudges them to also share the **free landing-page URL** at the end of the MP4 (closing 2-second card with `setnayan.com/[couple-slug]`) so every social share drives traffic back to the free landing page. **Input:** 5-10 engagement photos uploaded via the Setnayan upload widget (per-photo basic validation: ≤5MB, JPG/PNG, ≥1200×1200 recommended). **Template:** 8-12 generic templates in V1 (modern minimalist, romantic, vintage, cinematic, fun, etc.) selected at purchase time; templates are FFmpeg compositions with Lottie overlays for animated text — no per-couple custom design. **AI auto-edit:** scene-ordering heuristic (faces detected · landscape vs portrait crop · color-palette match) selects the per-photo order automatically; couple does not manually sequence. **Music:** Setnayan-owned AI catalogue (~400 tracks); couple picks category at purchase (Bridgerton / Pop / Hip-hop / Jazz / Acoustic / Cinematic). **Output:** single 30-60s 1080×1920 vertical MP4 (H.264, no watermark), downloadable from couple dashboard within ~5 minutes of upload completion. **SKU:** `save_the_date_video_render` ₱99 / one render (multi-purchase if couple wants multiple variants — e.g. one for IG, one for TikTok with different music). **Naming clarity:** the new SKU code `save_the_date_video_render` is **deliberately different** from the retired `save_the_date_render` SKU code to keep the audit trail clean (the retired SKU never shipped; if it had, OR voids / refunds would need to keep the old code distinct). | Three drivers. **First**, the free landing-page Phase 1 hero (locked first 2026-05-16 row) covers the *guest-facing* job-to-be-done (couple shares a link, guests open and save the date) but does not cover the *couple's own social-feed* job-to-be-done — couples still want a short, polished video to post on their personal IG / TikTok feed announcing the engagement. A 30-60s MP4 generated from engagement photos with Setnayan-owned music + closing-card-link to the landing page solves that job *and* doubles as marketing surface for the free landing page (every social share is a Setnayan-branded MP4 with a `setnayan.com/[couple-slug]` end-card driving traffic back to the platform). **Second**, the ₱99 price point is intentionally a low-friction social-share upsell, not a margin product — Setnayan's break-even is ~₱5-10 per render (FFmpeg compute on Cloudflare Workers + R2 storage + Setnayan-owned music = ~₱0 licensing cost), so the SKU still nets ~85-90% gross margin and ~70% net margin under V1 tax tier. The volume play is what matters: at a ₱99 price point and 30% attach rate on the 1,000-event V1 cohort, that is ~₱30K/mo in pure-funnel revenue *plus* every share is a Setnayan marketing surface. **Third**, distinguishing it from the retired SKU as a *different product* (different SKU code, different input model, different output model) keeps the audit trail clean. The retired SKU was a "render the landing-page hero into 3 video aspect ratios" feature; this is "AI-edit 5-10 engagement photos into one social-share clip" — fundamentally different inputs, fundamentally different outputs, different engineering pipelines. The naming distinction matters for OR / refund / audit / SKU history hygiene. | `CLAUDE.md` decision log (this entry); `0024_save_the_date/0024_save_the_date.md` (new § "Save-the-Date Video SKU ₱99 — reintroduced 2026-05-16 (separate product)" inserted after the retired-SKU table · explicitly distinguishes the new product from the retired one · documents inputs / templates / AI auto-edit / music / output / SKU code · preserves the existing page-reframe content above it — the free landing-page Phase 1 hero is NOT undone, this is an additive standalone MP4 product); `0034_payments_and_cart/0034_payments_and_cart.md` (`service_catalog` seed: add `save_the_date_video_render` 9900 row, category `save_the_date_video`, is_multi_purchase TRUE · retired `save_the_date_render` row left in place as an audit row but with comment "retired 2026-05-16 - replaced by save_the_date_video_render with different scope"); `API_Integration_Checklist.md` (Tier 5.2 FFmpeg render pipeline row appended with Save-the-Date Video render queue note · new `std-video-render-queue` Cloudflare Queue + Worker reading from R2 `setnayan-media/std-video-uploads/` and writing to `setnayan-media/std-video-renders/`); `App_Build_Status.md` (0024 row note updated to reflect that ₱99 MP4 SKU is back as a distinct product); `V1_Gap_Analysis_Status.md` (0024 row updated). **`.docx` mirrors NOT regenerated** (pandoc unavailable). **Engineering implementation pending** — upload widget for 5-10 engagement photos in the couple's add-ons flow, template picker UI (8-12 templates), music category picker, FFmpeg + Lottie render Worker on Cloudflare with R2 routing, 5-minute render SLA wiring, MP4 download surface on couple dashboard with "share this MP4" nudge that includes the landing-page URL end-card overlay. **Marketplace commission row INTENTIONALLY EXCLUDED from this commit batch.** **Sequencing note:** Fifth 2026-05-16 row. |
| 2026-05-16 | **Patiktok V1 SKU lock — dual-tier (Setnayan TikTok ₱999/day vs Personal TikTok ₱1,999/day) · 40-video soft cap per booth per day · ₱49/+10 overage · per-day multi-purchase.** Sixth 2026-05-16 row. Patiktok pricing model **replaces** the prior single-SKU ₱2,499/booth/5hr model (and its ₱999 additional-station + ₱499/hour add-on tier) with a **dual-tier per-day** model based on **who owns the videos**. **(1) Setnayan TikTok tier — ₱999/day:** videos auto-post to **Setnayan's master TikTok handle `@SetnayanWeddings`** (or similar); Setnayan retains ad-revenue upside if the compilation goes viral; couple gets the post link + downloadable MP4 with Setnayan-owned music. Same posting flow as the prior single-SKU spec. **(2) Personal TikTok tier — ₱1,999/day:** **couple BYO TikTok via OAuth** (one-time OAuth handshake during purchase flow); videos auto-post to the couple's own TikTok handle; couple owns all videos + analytics + ad-revenue upside. **(3) Soft cap: 40 videos per day per booth** — protects Setnayan margin (compilation render + storage + per-clip face-lock processing scales with video count); booth dashboard shows a live counter; at 40 the operator sees a soft-warning ("You've captured your day's allotted 40 videos · keep recording with the overage add-on") and can either stop or buy overage. **(4) Overage SKU: ₱49 per +10 videos** (multi-purchase — operator can stack as many +10 blocks as they want at the venue without leaving the booth flow). **(5) Per-day multi-purchase for multi-day events** — Filipino weddings often span 2-3 days (prep + ceremony + reception each at a different venue); couple buys per-day Patiktok exactly like Panood Daily Broadcast (₱999 × 3 = ₱2,997 for the Setnayan-tier 3-day, ₱1,999 × 3 = ₱5,997 for the Personal-tier 3-day). **Sound selection mechanics from the prior spec preserved:** couple curates 2 trending sounds (primary + backup) at purchase time; printable QR for the operator dashboard; multi-performer face-lock; downloadable backup copy with Setnayan-owned music (for off-TikTok sharing). **External display + dual-view UX preserved** (split-screen / PIP for venues with monitors). | Three drivers. **First**, the prior single-SKU ₱2,499/booth/5hr model under-served couples with multi-day weddings (a 3-day event meant either buying multiple full stations or skipping the booth on most days) and over-served couples with single-event-day weddings (5 hours is more than most receptions need). Per-day pricing matches event reality and the per-day Panood pattern decided in the same session. **Second**, the dual-tier split by video-ownership resolves a long-standing tension in the original spec — some couples want the viral marketing upside of `@SetnayanWeddings` (Setnayan amplifies their wedding to the platform's audience for free), other couples want their own TikTok to be where the wedding content lives forever (and want the ad-revenue + analytics). Charging differently for these two ownership models reflects the real value difference: BYO TikTok means Setnayan loses platform-marketing upside, so the price is ~2× to compensate. **Third**, the 40-video soft cap is calibrated to typical Filipino-wedding guest counts (200 guests × ~20% participation rate ≈ 40 unique submitters per booth per day); the soft cap rather than a hard cap with ₱49/+10 overage protects margin on outlier high-engagement events without forcing couples to buy a whole second booth purchase. The ₱49 overage charm-pricing keeps the in-event upsell low-friction. **Tax-tier note:** Setnayan-tier ₱999/day nets ~70% margin under V1 tax tier; Personal-tier ₱1,999/day nets ~72% (higher % because the OAuth + per-couple TikTok adds ~₱0 marginal cost vs the Setnayan-handle posting); overage SKU at ₱49 nets ~80%. | `CLAUDE.md` decision log (this entry); `0017_patiktok/0017_patiktok.md` (§ Pricing rewritten — old ₱2,499/booth/5hr table retired · new dual-tier per-day table inserted with the 4 SKU rows above · TikTok integration § rewritten to describe **two posting paths**: Setnayan-tier auto-post to `@SetnayanWeddings` (preserved from old spec) + Personal-tier OAuth-based auto-post to couple's own handle (new section) · sound-selection / multi-performer / external-display / downloadable-backup subsections preserved · soft-cap + overage subsection added · Coordinator workflow section updated to reference per-day multi-purchase · Anti-abuse rules table updated — the prior 500-submission-per-station-per-event cap is replaced by the 40-videos/day soft cap + overage); `0034_payments_and_cart/0034_payments_and_cart.md` (`service_catalog` seed: add `patiktok_setnayan_daily` 99900, `patiktok_personal_daily` 199900, `patiktok_video_overage` 4900 rows · the prior implicit ₱2,499/₱999/₱499 SKUs were never seeded — no retirement needed at the SQL level); `API_Integration_Checklist.md` (Tier 7.2 OAuth + PKCE row appended with TikTok OAuth scope details — `user.info.basic` + `video.upload` + `video.publish` scopes for the Personal-tier Patiktok BYO TikTok flow); `App_Build_Status.md` (0017 row note updated to dual-tier SKU lock 2026-05-16); `V1_Gap_Analysis_Status.md` (0017 row appended). **`.docx` mirrors NOT regenerated** (pandoc unavailable). **Engineering implementation pending** — operator dashboard live-counter for video count, soft-cap warning UI at 40 videos, overage upsell button at the booth, TikTok OAuth handshake at purchase time for Personal-tier (one-time, persisted in `oauth_grants` table), per-day SKU activation logic, per-tier posting routing logic (`if tier == 'personal_tiktok' then post via couple's OAuth else post via @SetnayanWeddings master credentials`), Anti-abuse rule update from 500-per-event to 40-per-day. **Marketplace commission row INTENTIONALLY EXCLUDED from this commit batch.** **Sequencing note:** Sixth 2026-05-16 row. |
| 2026-05-16 | **V1 blended net margin lock at V1 tax tier — ~67-72% net across most SKUs · 90% gross margin (COGS ≤10%) achieved · 90% NET mathematically impossible under PH tax law · projected ~₱4M annual net profit at 1,000 events × ₱6K avg revenue.** Seventh 2026-05-16 row. Closes today's V1 SKU lock burst with a margin-discipline lock to prevent future scope drift toward "90% net margin" framing that's mathematically impossible under PH non-VAT V1 tax. **(1) V1 tax assumption locked:** Percentage Tax 3% (NIRC § 116 · annual gross ≤₱3M threshold) + Local Business Tax 1% (LBT — Manila / Makati rate) + Income Tax 25% (graduated table corporate rate post first ₱5M). Total tax wedge ~28-29% of gross revenue. **(2) Achievable net margin range:** ~67-72% across most V1 SKUs. **(3) Achievable gross margin range:** **~90% (COGS ≤10%)** — this is the "90% margin" headline that's accurate when properly labeled as **gross**, not net. **(4) Why 90% NET is impossible:** PH non-VAT tax wedge (~28-29%) + non-zero COGS (storage / compute / SMS / OAuth-API quotas) puts the theoretical max net margin at ~72% even with 100% gross margin SKUs. The headline "90% margin" claim only holds at gross. **(5) Annual net profit projection at V1 scale:** 1,000 events × ₱6,000 avg revenue per event = **₱6M annual gross**; minus 10% COGS = ₱5.4M gross profit; minus 28% tax wedge = **~₱4M annual net profit**. **(6) Worst-case tax tier (V1.5+ when annual gross crosses ₱3M VAT-registration threshold):** 12% VAT + 35% income tax (top bracket) puts the wedge at ~42-45%; net margin floats to ~52-57% across SKUs. **All SKUs remain profitable** even at worst-case tax tier — no V1 SKU is below break-even under V2 tax regime, so the upgrade path does not force re-pricing. **(7) Per-SKU net-margin snapshot at V1 tax tier:** Save-the-Date Video ₱99 = ~70% net · Monogram Hero ₱1,999 = ~70% net (Potrace ~₱0 cost / minimal R2 storage) · Live Schedule ₱999 = ~71% net · Panood Daily Broadcast ₱499 = ~67-70% net (broadcaster compute) · Panood Camera Sync ₱99/day = ~70% net · Panood Annual ₱2,999 = ~70-72% net · Panood Annual Plus ₱3,999 = ~70-72% net · Patiktok Setnayan ₱999/day = ~70% net · Patiktok Personal ₱1,999/day = ~72% net · Patiktok overage ₱49/+10 = ~80% net · all V1.5+ Papic SKUs land in the 65-72% net band per row 3 above. **(8) Disciplined revenue framing rule:** internal docs + investor materials + marketing copy must label margin claims explicitly as "gross" or "net"; the bare "90% margin" headline without qualifier is **retired** from V1 messaging because it conflates the two and creates expectation-management debt. | Three drivers. **First**, the V1 tax tier (non-VAT · ₱3M-gross threshold · 3% Percentage Tax) is a deliberate choice the corporate filing has committed to, not a stretch assumption — but it sets a mathematical ceiling on net margin that is lower than the colloquial "90% margin" framing in pre-launch decks would suggest. Locking the assumption now (rather than discovering it during BIR Form 1701Q quarterly filing) lets the revenue model + investor narrative be honest from launch. **Second**, locking the per-SKU net-margin snapshot at the V1 tax tier with the 5 SKU locks already decided today (Papic V1.5+ pricing · Panood per-day · Save-the-Date Video · Patiktok dual-tier) consolidates today's decision burst into one verifiable economic story — every SKU lock today rolls up to the ₱4M annual net projection at 1,000 events. The projection is a back-of-envelope but it is traceable to today's specific lock decisions, not aspirational marketing. **Third**, calling out the worst-case V2 tax tier (12% VAT + 35% income at the top corporate bracket) and showing the model is still profitable under that regime — net floats to 52-57% — locks in that V1 SKU prices do NOT need to be revised when Setnayan crosses the VAT-registration threshold. The upgrade path is "more taxes, less margin, but still profitable across the board" rather than "have to re-price the SKU table." | `CLAUDE.md` decision log (this entry); `05_Financials/Cost_vs_Revenue_Analysis.xlsx` (margin tab to be regenerated next pricing-workbook pass with the new SKU rows from today's batch + the V1 tax tier formula assumption); `05_Financials/Pricing_Workbook_Set_Your_Prices.xlsx` (per-SKU net margin column to be regenerated with the V1 tax wedge); `05_Financials/Revenue_Projection_Model.xlsx` (annual scenarios updated to the ₱4M-at-1,000-events baseline projection); auto-memory: consider extending `project_setnayan.md` with a one-line note that V1 margin discipline is "90% gross / 67-72% net at V1 tax tier" so future sessions don't drift back to "90% net" framing. **`.docx` mirrors NOT regenerated** (pandoc unavailable). **No engineering implementation pending** — this is a margin-discipline / messaging lock, not a build item; the workbook regenerations are a Cowork bookkeeping task. **Marketplace commission row INTENTIONALLY EXCLUDED from this commit batch** (when that decision unlocks in a future Cowork pass, the marketplace SKUs + take-rate will be incorporated into the same margin model with their own tax-tier net-margin snapshot). **Sequencing note:** Seventh 2026-05-16 row · closes today's V1 SKU lock burst (rows 3-7 are the 5-item SKU framework lock; rows 1-2 were the earlier 0024 reframe + Monogram Hero PNG-to-SVG amendment). |

Closes the marketplace commission model deliberately excluded from row 7's batch and lays out the full V1 marketplace + payment + verification + vendor-tier framework as a single comprehensive lock. **(1) Setnayan Pay commission model — 5.5% on top of vendor price (NOT deducted from vendor):** vendor lists e.g. ₱100,000, couple pays ₱105,500 at checkout, Setnayan keeps the ₱5,500 gross (pays its own taxes from this — Percentage Tax 3% + LBT 1% + Income Tax 25%) → V1 tax tier net ~₱3,960 per ₱100K (3.96% effective), worst-case (V2 tax tier 12% VAT + 35% IT) net ~₱3,143 (3.14% effective). **No per-booking commission** taken from vendor side. Admin-configurable per payment method (cheap rails default 5.5% · premium rails up to 6.5%). **(2) Vendor's side of the ledger:** vendor receives vendor price minus terminal/gateway fee minus BIR Marketplace Withholding 0.5% — no Setnayan deduction whatsoever from the vendor's principal. Worked examples: Maya QR Ph (1.5% gateway) → vendor receives ₱98,000 on ₱100K booking (2% effective burden); Credit card (2.5% gateway) → vendor receives ₱97,000 (3% effective burden). **(3) BIR Marketplace Withholding:** 0.5% of vendor principal (1% × 50% under RMC No. 8-2024); Setnayan is the withholding agent and remits to BIR; vendor receives BIR Form 2307 quarterly which is creditable against the vendor's own income-tax liability. **(4) Payment gateway sequencing:** V1 launch = manual reconciliation (current 0034 spec: BDO / GCash QR + screenshot upload + admin approve workflow). V1.5+ primary gateway = **Maya Business**. Maya QR Ph (1.5% gateway fee) is the **preferred default rail** at checkout. Per-method admin config: Maya QR / Bank transfer / GCash direct / eWallet / Credit card / OTC. Gateway fees passed through to vendor transparently. **(5) Outbound disbursement (Setnayan → vendor):** Setnayan absorbs the ₱15-25 disbursement fee per payout — vendor sees nominal "net of terminal + BIR" figure without an additional disbursement deduction. **(6) Video meetings RETIRED:** Daily.co decision from 2026-05-11 is **CLOSED — feature removed entirely from V1+**. Couples and vendors use external tools (Google Meet, Zoom, Messenger, WhatsApp) for video. 0019 retains chat + file attachments + in-app file viewers + coordinator-join + force-majeure flow. Vendor Pro Weekly "Video meetings via Daily.co" benefit is **removed** from the feature list. The 2026-05-11 decision-log entry is **amended** with a strikethrough + cross-reference to this row. **(7) Vendor Verification (NEW V1 spec section):** **Initial Verification = FREE** (treated as customer acquisition cost; Setnayan absorbs ~₱535/vendor across ID liveness + selfie + sanctions screening + admin time). **Annual Re-verification = ₱1,499/year** (paid by vendor). **Re-verification after demotion = ₱2,499** (paid by vendor — higher to discourage demotion churn). **All-or-nothing — no partial verified state.** Required documents (all 12, no exceptions): (a) DTI Business Name Certificate — auto-validated via DTI Database lookup; (b) BIR Form 2303 (Certificate of Registration); (c) Mayor's Permit (current year); (d) Valid government ID of owner — verified via Persona / Veriff / Onfido at ~₱200 per check; (e) Bank account proof — Maya / GCash micro-deposit verification; (f) 5-10 portfolio samples with reverse image search check; (g) 3-5 past client references — Setnayan calls 1-2 randomly; (h) Live selfie + ID liveness check; (i) 15-min Google Meet with admin; (j) Phone SMS OTP + email confirmation; (k) Social media presence (Instagram or Facebook business page); (l) Sanctions / PEP screening (AMLC watchlist API). Category-specific extras: wedding venues require insurance ₱1M+ liability; catering >100 guests require Health Department certification; wedding coordinators require industry certification or 5+ years experience proof; high-value vendors (₱500K+ avg booking) get a background check on the owner. Vendor effort 30-45 min · Setnayan SLA 3-5 business days · documents stored in `setnayan-vendor-verification` R2 bucket. **(8) Verified vendor perks (locked):** Verified badge in marketplace · Setnayan Pay unlocks for couples (couples can ONLY use Setnayan Pay with verified vendors) · custom partial payment plans · Pro Weekly subscription (₱499/wk) · Sponsored Boost eligibility · Boosted Ads eligibility · All Tools Unlock bundle access · immediate full payout (no 3-stage hold) · higher marketplace search ranking · Featured Vendor program eligibility · coordinator-join permission in couple threads. **(9) Coming_soon vendor limitations:** Setnayan Pay locked (couples pay direct off-platform) · fixed Setnayan-managed 3-stage payout (20% / 60% / 20%) · "Coming Soon" badge · no Pro Weekly subscription · no Boosted Ads or Sponsored Boost · lower search ranking · no tool access. **(10) Vendor payout model (locked):** Verified = immediate full payout (less terminal fee + BIR withholding), T+1 to vendor's chosen disbursement method. Coming_soon = 3-stage milestone release — Stage 1 Reservation deposit (20%) released at booking confirmation · Stage 2 Pre-event check (60%) released at T-14 days when couple confirms vendor's prep on track · Stage 3 Post-event balance (20%) released at T+7 days when couple confirms delivery and no dispute. T+14 and T+7 dispute windows: 7-day couple response, auto-release on silence. Demote-to-coming_soon trigger: 3+ disputes within 30 days. **(11) Vendor Marketing tier ladder:** Boosted Ads (weekly, by radius, verified-only, stacks with Pro Weekly, cancel anytime) — 5km ₱4,999/wk · 10km ₱7,999/wk · 20km ₱14,999/wk. Sponsored Boost (premium, 30km radius, long commitment, verified-only, stacks with everything) — Quarterly ₱249,999 / 3 mo · Annual ₱799,999 / 12 mo (~20% discount vs quarterly × 4). The prior weekly ₱1,499/wk Sponsored Boost tier is **retired** in favor of the long-commit ladder. **(12) All Tools Unlock bundle (NEW SKU):** ₱9,999/year. Includes Mood Board · Palette · Seating Arrangement · QR Reader · Advanced Pricing Tier. **Open to ALL paying vendors (not verified-only — capability tools support new-vendor growth).** Saves ~61% vs buying each tool at ₱99/wk individually. À la carte ₱99/wk tool integrations remain available. **(13) Anthropic API setup (Item #9 lock):** Owner signs up for Anthropic Console account · workspace "Setnayan" · spend caps **$500/month soft alert · $2,000/month hard cap · $100/day soft cap** · primary model **Claude Sonnet 4.6** (vision tasks) · budget model **Claude Haiku 4.5** (Contract Intelligence text tasks) · OpenAI GPT-4 as V1.5+ fallback. Unblocks 0032 Contract Intelligence for V1 ship. AI Highlights remain V1.5+ (waiting on Papic build). **(14) AI Edited Highlight 3-min repriced:** ₱4,999 → **₱3,499** to land cleanly between the prior conflicting spec values (Strategy B had ₱2,999; charm pricing had ₱4,999) and establish a tighter tier ladder. **(15) Setnayan Pay margin slot (per row 7's commission framework):** 5.5% gross on every booking transacted in-app · ~3.96% net at V1 tax tier · ~3.14% net at worst-case V2 tax tier · slots cleanly into the ₱4M annual net projection from row 7 once marketplace booking volume comes online. | Three drivers. **First**, the 5.5%-on-top model preserves vendor pricing autonomy — vendors set their own list prices without absorbing platform commission, removing the most common cause of "race-to-the-bottom" pricing on competitor PH wedding marketplaces. The convenience-fee framing is also clearer to couples ("you're paying a small platform fee for guaranteed coverage + dispute resolution") than the alternative "vendor markup absorbs commission" model that hides the cost. **Second**, splitting verification into FREE initial (treated as CAC absorbed by Setnayan) + paid renewal (₱1,499/yr) + heavier re-verification after demotion (₱2,499) keeps the onboarding funnel frictionless while creating real cost-of-trust gates downstream. The 12-document checklist is intentionally high-bar at FREE entry: it filters out non-serious vendors at zero financial cost to Setnayan's revenue model, and the rigor (12 docs · 15-min Google Meet · sanctions screening) compounds the marketplace's trust signal vs WhatsApp-based competitors. **Third**, retiring the weekly Sponsored Boost (₱1,499/wk) in favor of the Quarterly ₱249,999 / Annual ₱799,999 long-commit ladder is a deliberate price-discipline move: weekly Sponsored Boost was being treated as "fancy Boosted Ads" and competing with the new Boosted Ads tier; the quarterly/annual tier creates a distinct premium product (30km radius, multi-month commit, ~₱83K/month effective rate) that's properly differentiated from the weekly Boosted Ads ladder (5/10/20km, single-week commit, ~₱5-15K/week effective rate). Together these three tiers — Boosted Ads weekly · Pro Weekly subscription · Sponsored Boost long-commit — give vendors a clean ladder from "try this week" → "habitual subscriber" → "marquee premium presence." **Fourth (operations)**, the Daily.co retire is forced by economics: per-event meeting volume was projected to grow non-linearly with vendor count, and gating video behind external tools (Google Meet / Zoom / Messenger / WhatsApp) doesn't degrade vendor UX since 0019 chat + file attachments + coordinator-join + force-majeure flow are still in-app — the moment a video call is needed, the in-app chat hands off to whichever external rail the parties already use. This decouples 0019's recurring cost from event volume entirely. **Fifth (Anthropic API)**, locking Sonnet 4.6 (vision) + Haiku 4.5 (text) with hard $2K/mo cap and $100/day soft cap puts a real ceiling on AI cost: Contract Intelligence at scale (V1.5+ event volumes) should not exceed ~$50-150/mo of Haiku usage at projected contract upload rates; Sonnet vision spend is bounded to AI Highlights which are still V1.5+ — so V1 AI spend is firmly inside the $500/mo soft alert with a 4× safety margin. The choice of Haiku 4.5 over GPT-4 for V1 reduces marginal cost per contract by ~80% while retaining comparable extraction accuracy on Filipino-language wedding contracts (the Setnayan benchmark suite). **Sixth (AI Edited Highlight reprice)**, the ₱4,999 → ₱3,499 lock kills a long-standing internal price conflict (Strategy B had ₱2,999, charm pricing had ₱4,999, in-app SKU page had two different anchors); ₱3,499 lands cleanly between, charms at ₱X,499, and creates a tighter ladder between ₱99 Save-the-Date Video (entry) → ₱1,999 Monogram Hero (mid) → ₱3,499 AI Edited Highlight (premium) without re-pricing adjacent SKUs. | `CLAUDE.md` decision log (this entry); | 2026-05-16 | **Website copy reconciliation — couple-side "no commission on vendor bookings" line on `setnayan.com` is RETIRED; replaced with explicit 5.5% Setnayan Pay convenience-fee disclosure at the transparent-pricing section. Vendor-side "no commission, no monthly bill" copy STAYS unchanged per the 2026-05-11 grandfather promise.** Ninth 2026-05-16 row. Follow-up reconciliation to row 8's marketplace commission lock — the live site at `setnayan.com` was still serving the pre-lock copy `"No subscription, no per-guest fee, no commission on vendor bookings."` in its transparent-pricing section, which became misleading the moment the 5.5% Setnayan Pay convenience fee was locked. The couple sees the line "no commission on vendor bookings" and reasonably concludes that the vendor's listed price IS the total they pay — but the checkout adds 5.5% on top, so a couple seeing a ₱100K vendor price actually pays ₱105,500. **The fix splits semantics by audience:** couple-side gets a new transparent-pricing section that *explicitly* surfaces the 5.5% fee + worked example; vendor-side keeps the "no commission, no monthly bill" framing intact because — under the row-8 model — the 5.5% is NOT deducted from the vendor's listed price (the vendor receives ₱100,000 minus only terminal fee + BIR withholding, exactly as they would on any payment platform). The 2026-05-11 grandfather promise to the launch cohort is therefore still accurate and stays. **Canonical copy locked (couple-side `setnayan.com` transparent-pricing section):** Section headline "Transparent pricing." Sub-claim "Free to plan — the planning tools are free forever. Vendor bookings add a **5.5% Setnayan Pay convenience fee** at checkout, shown on the order summary before you confirm. No subscription, no per-guest fee, no hidden charges." Three-column transparency strip (Planning tools = Free forever · Add-on apparatus = À la carte · Vendor bookings = +5.5% at checkout). Worked example block: ₱100,000 vendor price → ₱5,500 fee → ₱105,500 customer-paid → vendor receives ₱100,000 listed price minus their own terminal + BIR. **Mobile copy collapse:** three-column strip stacks; worked-example block becomes a "See how it works" accordion. **Vendor-side surfaces unchanged:** the 6 occurrences of "no commission · no monthly bill" in `0015_main_website.html` + 4 occurrences in `0015_main_website.md` Section 5 ("Free listing · no monthly fee to start") stay verbatim — the row-8 model preserves vendor pricing autonomy and the line is still factually correct. | Two drivers. **First**, transparency-debt avoidance: leaving the "no commission on vendor bookings" line live while the locked model is a 5.5% on-top convenience fee creates the kind of bait-and-switch perception that destroys couple trust the first time a couple notices the ₱5,500 surprise on their checkout summary. Better to surface the 5.5% prominently up-front than to hide it and face refund / complaint debt downstream. **Second**, audience-split semantics — the 5.5% is *couple-paid on top* and *not deducted from vendor*, so the same phrase ("no commission") is honest from the vendor's POV ("we keep our listed price") and misleading from the couple's POV ("the vendor's price is our total"). Splitting the surfaces by audience preserves the row-8 model's clean economics + the 2026-05-11 grandfather promise simultaneously. | `0015_main_website/0015_main_website.md` (new Section 8.5 "Transparent pricing" inserted between Section 8 and Section 9 — canonical headline + sub-claim + three-column strip + worked-example block + mobile-copy-collapse note + engineering note flagging the live-site `apps/web/app/(marketing)/pricing/page.tsx` copy patch); `apps/web` engineering patch (pending PR — apps/web marketing pricing surface needs the deprecated "no commission on vendor bookings" line replaced with the new canonical copy per Section 8.5); **`.docx` mirror NOT regenerated** (pandoc unavailable — flagged for future session). **Vendor-side spec surfaces left intact:** `0015_main_website.html` lines 1385/1619/2058/2190/2400/2616, `0015_main_website.md` Section 5 "Free listing · no monthly fee to start", `0019_communications.md` line 106, `0020_interaction_prototype.html` line 1090. **Engineering implementation pending** — locate the live-site marketing pricing copy in apps/web (the prototype HTML at `0015_main_website.html` has the vendor-side surfaces only; the couple-side transparent-pricing section that's currently live on `setnayan.com` is in apps/web app-code, not the spec corpus prototype — the engineering PR finds + replaces it). **Sequencing note:** Ninth 2026-05-16 row · follow-up reconciliation to row 8's marketplace commission lock · walkthrough item #11 closed. |

The PR #7 model (`users.deleted_at` soft-delete + 100-year `auth.users.banned_until`) is retired across both spec files; the new model is two distinct single-admin-authority actions in `/admin/users`. **(1) 0023 § 3.4 Users — full rewrite of the actions list:** **🗑 Delete** (hard-deletes `auth.users` cascading to `public.users` · email is FREE for re-signup including as a different `account_type` — e.g. former vendor returning as customer) + **🚫 Blacklist** (hard-delete + insert into new `blacklisted_emails` table · email is LOCKED · signup gate `apps/web/lib/blacklist.ts → isEmailBlacklisted(email)` intercepts via `?error=blacklisted`) + **↩ Unblacklist** (removes the row from `blacklisted_emails`; no auth row restored — user signs up fresh). Owner directive while reviewing the V1 create-event flow. **(1) Tile list swap.** The visible tile set on `/dashboard/create-event` changes from `wedding · birthday · celebration · travel · corporate · burial` (six tiles, locked in the original iteration 0000 spec) to `wedding · birthday · celebration · travel · corporate · tournament · christening` (seven tiles). The carousel never ends — Tournament/Christening flow back into Wedding seamlessly. **(3) Event-name input gated by selection.** The Event-name `<input>` no longer renders until a tile is picked. Before selection the form area shows a dashed placeholder reading "Pick an event type above to name it." Once Wedding is picked, the form appears with a "Planning a **Wedding**. Change" affordance so the couple can re-open the picker without leaving the page. Submitting still goes through the existing `createWeddingEvent` server action which enforces `ALLOWED_TYPES = ['wedding']` server-side. **(4) ENUM swap migration shipped.** `supabase/migrations/20260516260000_iteration_0000_event_type_swap.sql` renames the existing `public.event_type` enum, recreates it with the new value set (wedding/birthday/celebration/travel/corporate/tournament/christening), swaps the `events.event_type` column over via text round-trip cast, and drops the orphaned old enum. Defensive `UPDATE events SET event_type = 'celebration' WHERE event_type::text = 'burial'` runs first so the cast cannot fail on stray rows (zero expected since the V1 UI only ever inserts `wedding`). TypeScript unions in `packages/shared/src/index.ts → EventType` and `apps/web/lib/events.ts → EventRow.event_type` updated to the new shape. `apps/web/public/llms.txt` lede copy swapped: "birthdays, travel, corporate, and burial events" → "birthdays, celebrations, travel, corporate, tournament, and christening events." **(5) Iteration 0000 spec updated in three places:** § 2.5 picker prose + table now lists seven tiles in carousel framing, with the Event-name-gated-by-selection behavior called out explicitly; § "Three columns on events" schema block now references the `public.event_type` ENUM directly instead of the prior `TEXT NOT NULL CHECK (...)` shape (corpus catch-up: the engineering shipped an ENUM at iteration-0000 launch, not a CHECK constraint, so the spec text is being reconciled here at the same time); acceptance criteria row 1 lists the new enum values, row 2 lists seven tiles + infinite-carousel + gated input. | Two drivers. **First**, the owner-side justification for swapping burial → tournament/christening is product framing: burial events as a Setnayan future surface tested poorly in informal owner-side conversations (couples uniformly read it as "wrong app, wrong moment" when they encountered it on a wedding planner's create-event picker), while tournament + christening map to two real life-event categories Filipino families already plan with elaborate logistics (sports league championships · infant christenings combined with family fiestas) — both fit Setnayan's "event-platform play, not just a wedding app" positioning better than burial did. **Second**, the carousel + gated-input change addresses a measurable UX friction the prior static grid created: with all six tiles + the Event-name input visible simultaneously, couples were typing into Event-name before mentally committing to "this is the event type I'm planning," and then second-guessing themselves; gating the input behind a tile pick forces a 1-tile-of-attention moment that's been validated as the right pattern in other modal pickers (the Setnayan Pay payment-method picker uses the same gate-the-next-field-on-pick pattern). The old `guided_planner_*` SKU codes are retired (marked `is_active=FALSE` in service_catalog rather than deleted — audit-trail hygiene per the row-12 / row-15 SKU convention). **(4) Schema rename + state expansion (events table):** `guided_planner_status` → **`concierge_status`** with enum widened from `('diy'|'active'|'expired')` → `('diy'|'preview'|'active'|'expired')` (new `'preview'` state for the card-less 7-day taste); `guided_planner_tier` → **`concierge_tier`** with enum updated `('1week'|'3month'|'12month'|NULL)` → `('essentials'|'complete'|NULL)`; `guided_planner_expires_at` → **`concierge_expires_at`**. **Engineering note:** if the `guided_planner_*` columns have already shipped to production via an Install_Sequence_V1 migration, a real `ALTER TABLE ... RENAME COLUMN` + enum-swap migration is required (text round-trip cast pattern per the row-14 ENUM swap); if no production migration has shipped yet, the rename is design-phase-only and lands on the next iteration-0016 implementation pass. **(5) The `'preview'` state surfaces the full Complete feature set with a banner reading "Preview · X days left → Continue with Essentials (₱2,499) or Complete (₱4,999)"; daily cron sweep flips it to `'expired'` at T+7** (same sweep as the `'active'` expiry path; no new cron infra needed). **(6) Service catalog updates (iteration 0034):** retire `guided_planner_1week` · `guided_planner_3month` · `guided_planner_12month` rows (mark `is_active=FALSE` with `-- retired 2026-05-16 · replaced by concierge_essentials + concierge_complete` comment); insert `concierge_essentials` (249,900 centavos · category `concierge` · is_multi_purchase TRUE) + `concierge_complete` (499,900 · same shape). Try 7 days free →" inline link below the picker for the preview entry-point. Complete tile flagged as BEST VALUE with prominent visual treatment. **(8) Pricing-page anchor strategy (new — `apps/web/app/(marketing)/pricing/page.tsx`):** the page must always render the **human-coordinator price anchor** next to the Concierge price ("Wedding coordinator: ₱25,000+ · Setnayan Concierge: ₱4,999") so couples mentally compare against the real alternative (human planner) instead of FREE international apps (The Knot · Zola · Joy). **Per-day breakdown surfaced** ("₱13.69/day for your entire planning runway") because Filipino budgeting psychology responds strongly to daily-cost framing. **Wedding-line-item comparison strip** ("Less than your invitation suite · Less than your prenup shoot · 5× cheaper than a coordinator"). **(9) Settings tab (iteration 0025) renamed** "Guided Planner" → "Setnayan Concierge" with the activation/extension UI updated to the 2-tier picker + preview-start CTA in the DIY state. **(10) Dashboard variants (iteration 0021 § 2.0b)** expanded from 3 states to 4: **DIY · Preview · Active · Expired**. The 9-step journey checklist structure unchanged; Preview state shows it fully populated with the "this access expires in X days" treatment + Continue-with-Essentials/Complete CTAs in the days-remaining strip. | Two drivers reshape the prior 2026-05-14 lock. **First, the ₱99/₱999/₱1,999 ladder cheapened the brand against the wedding category.** Owner observation: in a market where couples spend ₱200K–2M on the actual event, anchoring entry at ₱99 reads as "sari-sari / load-card territory" — couples mentally compare Setnayan to FREE international apps (The Knot · Zola · Joy · Bridebook) instead of the real alternative (₱25K+ human wedding coordinator · ₱80K–300K full-service planner). The 3-tier discount ladder also signaled SaaS-style "we'll keep upselling you" rather than the premium-product feel the wedding category responds to. The repriced ₱2,499 / ₱4,999 ladder lands in the **"reasonable wedding-line-item zone"** (₱3,999–7,999, alongside invitation suites · prenup shoots · custom monograms · premium wedding websites) where Filipino couples already spend without blinking. ₱4,999 flagship is **5× cheaper than the cheapest human coordinator** — the strongest single anchor in the comparison set — while ₱2,499 Essentials saves face for tighter budgets without reintroducing cheap-app perception. **Second, two tiers (not three) signal product confidence.** Apple sells one iPhone per model, not a 3-SKU ladder; the prior 3-tier ladder created "discount stack" psychology that competed with FREE apps mentally instead of with human planners. Collapsing to two tiers + a free preview is the Apple/Notion pattern — confident pricing, optional preview, no recurring hidden charges. **Third, the card-less 7-day preview replaces the ₱99 1-week pass as the try-before-buy lever.** ₱99 was the floor anchor that cheapened the brand; the 7-day preview is the same conversion lever without the brand cost — couples get the full Complete experience for free, then must pay to continue. Conversion will be softer than a card-on-file trial (which requires V1.5 auto-renew rails that don't ship until GCash Merchant API lands), but the preview-without-card is V1-compatible and avoids the bait-and-switch perception of "free trial → surprise charge." **Revenue framing:** prior modal payout was ~₱1,274 (6-month-out couple buying the 12-month tier at ₱1,999 under the old ladder); new modal is ~₱4,999 if couples pick Complete (most-likely choice under the new ladder per the "save-face cheap option exists but flagship is obvious value" pattern) or ~₱2,499 if Essentials. Net revenue per converted Concierge customer materially improves at the cost of (a) sharper price discrimination — some couples who'd have bought ₱99 to try it out simply preview-and-bounce, and (b) higher pricing-page conversion-rate dependency on the human-coordinator anchor landing well. Both risks accepted; the pricing page must lead with the coordinator anchor + per-day math + wedding-line-item comparison for the conversion math to hold. **Filipino-specific consumer psychology applied:** daily-cost framing (₱13.69/day) outperforms total-cost framing in PH market budgeting decisions; English-language premium copy reads as more upmarket than Tagalog in the wedding category specifically (per the marketing-site voice rule in COWORK.md line 74 — "EN-primary, luxurious-Filipino-modern"). | `CLAUDE.md` decision log (this entry) · iteration table row #0016 (rewritten to 2-tier "Setnayan Concierge" framing); | 2026-05-16 | **Setnayan Pay convenience fee repriced 5.5%/6.5% → flat 5.0% on top of vendor price · Option B (vendor-absorbs-gateway) confirmed over Option A (Setnayan-absorbs-gateway) · supersedes the morning's 8th 2026-05-16 row.** Sixteenth 2026-05-16 row. Owner-ratified design target finalized — **3% net to Setnayan after all taxes at worst-case tax tier**. **(1) Math:** required gross = 3% ÷ (1 − tax_wedge) → V1 small-biz 28% wedge needs 4.17% gross · V2 VAT-registered 35% wedge needs 4.62% gross · extreme 40% wedge needs 5.00% gross. **5.0% flat is the minimum gross that holds the 3% net floor under every plausible tax scenario** with cushion for chargebacks / failed transactions / disbursement-fee absorption. **(2) Yields by tier at 5.0% flat:** V1 (28% wedge) → 3.60% net (1.67× the 2.16% operating breakeven); V2 realistic (35% wedge) → 3.25% net (1.50×); extreme worst (40% wedge) → 3.00% net (1.39×). **(3) Option B (vendor-absorbs-gateway) confirmed** over Option A (Setnayan-absorbs-gateway) because (a) Stripe Connect / Airbnb / Lazada Marketplace / every PH marketplace works this way so vendors expect it · (b) it insulates Setnayan from gateway rate risk forever (if Maya raises QR Ph from 1.5% → 2%, vendor absorbs not Setnayan) · (c) it lets the customer-facing headline stay at a clean flat 5.0% without rail-specific math at the cart · (d) Vendor Studio subscribers can be offered a gateway-absorbed perk in V1.5 as a subscription-conversion lever without disturbing V1 economics. Under Option A, Setnayan would have needed 6.21% gross to net 3% — bigger headline, more rail-rate risk, no upside. **(4) Annual revenue impact** vs the morning's 5.5%/6.5% lock at 500 paying couples × ₱300K avg booking through Setnayan Pay: −₱750K gross / −₱540K net per year — accepted because the 3% net target is achieved precisely and the round-number 5.0% headline strengthens marketing. **(5) Three rate epochs now in the decision log** within iteration 0034 itself: original 3% (≤ 2026-05-15, retired — below breakeven at every tax tier) · morning 5.5%/6.5% (2026-05-16 AM, retired — over-collected against the 3% net target) · evening flat 5.0% (2026-05-16 PM, this row, LIVE). **(6) Schema unchanged structurally:** `service_orders.fees_centavos` still derived as `subtotal × fee_bps_for_method / 10000`; `payment_method_config.setnayan_fee_bps` default 500 (was 550/650 dual-rate); per-method override capability retained for future rail-cost shocks. | Two drivers. **First, the morning's 5.5%/6.5% rate over-collected against the owner's actual 3% net design target.** The morning lock was tuned for a "safe profit per booking" intuition rather than a stated net target. Once the owner ratified 3% net as the design goal in the afternoon conversation, recomputing the required gross gave 4.17–5.00% depending on tax tier — the morning's 5.5% gave ~3.58% net at V1 / ~3.25% at V2 worst case, exceeding the target by 0.58pp / 0.25pp. Flat 5.0% is the disciplined answer: exact-on-target at extreme tax, comfortable cushion at realistic tax. **Second, Option B is strictly better than Option A on every dimension except a marginal vendor-recruiting story.** Option A requires Setnayan to absorb the 1.5% Maya gateway fee out of its own portion → required gross climbs to 6.21% to hit 3% net → higher customer headline + exposure to gateway rate risk + no insulation from rail-cost shocks. Option B lets the rail cost pass through to the vendor (who pays it on any platform anyway — Setnayan is not the cause of the gateway fee), keeps the headline at flat 5.0%, and treats "gateway-absorbed" as a future Vendor Studio perk rather than a baseline cost. **Flipside accepted:** vendor sees a 2.0% effective burden on Maya QR Ph payouts (0.5% BIR + 1.5% gateway) — addressed by naming the gateway pass-through explicitly in the vendor agreement so vendors understand "Setnayan doesn't deduct a commission; | 2026-05-17 | **V1.5+ vendor disbursement automation locked via Maya Business Bulk Fund Transfer — three-rail routing (Intra-Maya instant+free · InstaPay <1min+₱10 absorbed · PESONet EOD+₱15 absorbed) · default rail per payout type · vendor override in 0022 with ₱5 rebate-for-downgrade / pay-for-upgrade pricing · Maya Bank vendor-recruiting copy added to 0006 Vendor Payout model + 0022 onboarding tour + verification approval email · new `disbursement_batches` table + `vendor_payouts.{rail, rail_chosen_by, batch_id, status}` columns in 0034 § 6.9 schema.** First 2026-05-17 row. Adds the outbound-disbursement half of the marketplace payment architecture, complementing the inbound Setnayan Pay flow locked yesterday (rows 16-17 of 2026-05-16). **(1) Three rails, three speeds, three costs:** **Intra-Maya** (vendor has Maya Bank account → instant + free + 24/7 for both vendor and Setnayan); **InstaPay** (Maya → any PH bank → < 1 min + 24/7 incl. weekends + ~₱10 bulk rate absorbed by Setnayan); **PESONet** (Maya → any PH bank → EOD same banking day (or T+1 weekends/holidays) + ~₱15 bulk rate absorbed by Setnayan). **(2) Default routing per payout type:** verified vendor immediate payout → InstaPay; coming_soon vendor 3-stage milestone → PESONet; any vendor on Maya Bank → intra-Maya regardless of payout type; payouts < ₱500 or > ₱500K (above InstaPay cap) → PESONet. **(3) Vendor override** in 0022 Vendor Dashboard payout preferences — verified vendors can downgrade default to PESONet for ₱5/payout rebate credit (Setnayan saves ₱5, vendor earns ₱5, both incentivized); coming_soon vendors can upgrade individual milestone to InstaPay by absorbing the ₱5 difference. **(4) Maya Bank vendor-recruiting pitch** — canonical copy "Get paid instantly, free, 24/7 — open a Maya Bank business account · Or get paid in under a minute to any PH bank, including weekends (InstaPay) · Standard payout settles end of business day (PESONet) — default for milestone releases." Surfaces on verification approval email + 0022 onboarding tour + payout-preferences settings. Strongest vendor-recruiting incentive in the payments architecture — every Maya Bank account opened means instant+free payouts for the vendor AND zero disbursement cost for Setnayan, forever. **(5) Schema (0034 § 6.9):** new `disbursement_batches` table (batch_id · generated_by_admin_id · rail enum (instapay/pesonet/mixed) · csv_r2_key · recipient_count · total_centavos · status enum (pending_upload/uploaded/partial_success/complete/failed) · maya_batch_reference · row_outcomes JSONB · timestamps); new columns on `vendor_payouts` (`rail` enum, `rail_chosen_by` enum, `batch_id` FK nullable, `status` enum widened to pending/batched/disbursed/failed/manual_reconciliation); two new indexes for batch-status queries. **(6) Failure handling:** if Maya rejects any row in a bulk batch (invalid account number, KYC mismatch, frozen account), that payout's status reverts to `pending` and rejoins next-day batch. Partial-success batches log per-row outcomes in `disbursement_batches.row_outcomes`. Three consecutive batch failures for same vendor trigger admin alert + automatic move to manual reconciliation for that vendor until they update their disbursement details. **(7) Annual disbursement cost absorbed by Setnayan (planning assumption, reconciles to actual Maya quote once business account is approved):** ~₱2,500/yr at 200 couples · ~₱6,500/yr at 500 couples · ~₱13K/yr at 1,000 couples · ~₱65K/yr at 5,000 couples — every scenario stays under 0.3% of platform revenue. **(8) Operational efficiency unlock (the real value):** per-payout admin time collapses from ~5 minutes (manual click-through) to ~5 seconds (one CSV row in daily batch upload). At 500 couples = ~37 hours/yr saved; at 5,000 couples = ~370 hours/yr saved. Admin headcount doesn't grow proportionally with booking volume. | Three drivers. **First, vendor payouts at scale require batching automation that the current V1 manual reconciliation flow does not provide.** The manual flow is acceptable for V1 (low booking volume, single admin) but breaks operationally at ~50+ payouts/week. Maya Business Bulk Fund Transfer (the user-shared product page lock) is the cleanest fit because (a) it sits on top of the same Maya Business merchant account that V1.5+ inbound Setnayan Pay already requires, (b) it routes through BSP's standard InstaPay + PESONet rails so vendors receive money via the rails they already trust, and (c) the bulk CSV upload pattern fits Setnayan's daily-batch admin cadence without requiring a real-time disbursement API. **Second, the three-rail routing isn't arbitrary** — each rail maps to a specific BSP-defined cost/speed trade-off (InstaPay = sub-minute real-time gross settlement; PESONet = end-of-day batch settlement; intra-Maya = zero-cost internal book transfer). The default routing rules (verified→InstaPay, coming_soon→PESONet, Maya-Bank→intra) optimize for what each vendor segment actually wants: verified vendors want speed (Setnayan absorbs the ₱10 to keep them happy); coming_soon vendors haven't earned speed yet (PESONet is the right default + saves Setnayan ₱5/payout); Maya Bank vendors are the strictly-best outcome for everyone. **Third, the Maya Bank vendor-recruiting copy is genuinely aligned, not promotional** — every Maya Bank account opened means an instant+free payout experience for the vendor AND a zero-fee payout cost for Setnayan, forever. This is the rare case where the marketing pitch and the platform economics point the same direction without any compromise. Vendors who don't switch to Maya Bank still get fully functional payouts (InstaPay/PESONet); Setnayan still absorbs their disbursement cost; nobody loses. But the more vendors who do switch, the better the marketplace economics get for everyone. **Pricing note:** Maya Business does not publish Bulk Fund Transfer pricing publicly — quote-based per business based on monthly volume commitment. Locked numbers (~₱10 InstaPay / ~₱15 PESONet) are V1.5+ planning assumptions derived from (a) Maya's published individual rates (₱15 InstaPay outbound · ₱15-25 PESONet outbound) and (b) industry-standard 30-40% bulk discount; reconciled to actual Maya quote once the business merchant account is approved (2-4 week SLA per Owner Admin Punch List). | `0034_payments_and_cart/0034_payments_and_cart.md` § 6.7 expanded to two subsections — `Inbound — customer pays Setnayan Pay` (unchanged from yesterday's flat 5.0% lock) + new `Outbound — Setnayan pays vendor` (full three-rail routing table · vendor override pricing · annual aggregate cost projection · Maya Bank vendor-recruiting copy · failure handling for bulk batches); | 2026-05-16 | **Vendor convenience-fee absorption opt-in locked — `vendors.absorbs_convenience_fee BOOLEAN DEFAULT FALSE` · "No Convenience Fee" badge on marketplace profile when TRUE · cart hides the fee row + customer sees vendor's listed price flat · Setnayan revenue unchanged at 5% gross either way · snapshot at order-creation time onto `service_orders.vendor_absorbed_fee`.** Seventeenth 2026-05-16 row. Adds a first-class vendor-side choice on top of the evening's flat 5.0% + Option B (vendor-absorbs-gateway) lock. **(1) Schema:** new boolean column `vendors.absorbs_convenience_fee` (defined in 0006) + new snapshot column `service_orders.vendor_absorbed_fee` (defined in 0034 § 6.9) so toggle flips don't retroactively alter in-flight orders. **(2) Cart math change for vendor bookings where `vendor.absorbs_convenience_fee = TRUE`:** customer-facing `fees_centavos = 0` (no fee row shown); `total_centavos = subtotal_centavos` (all-in price); but Setnayan's revenue ledger still records `subtotal × 500 / 10000` as platform revenue — the ₱5,000 on a ₱100K booking comes out of the vendor's payout, not the customer's bill. **(3) Worked example at ₱100K booking** (canonical example for the spec + the live-site `/pricing` page): Option A (vendor covers) → vendor receives **₱93,100** at Maya QR Ph 1.5% / **₱92,150** at max-2.5% rail (₱100K listed − ₱5K Setnayan fee absorbed − 0.5% BIR Withholding on ₱95K service base − terminal fee on ₱95K service base); Option B (default · customer pays fee) → vendor receives **₱98,000** at Maya QR Ph / **₱97,000** at max-2.5% (₱100K listed − 0.5% BIR − terminal on ₱100K). Vendor sacrifices ₱4,900 / ₱4,850 (~5%) for the "No Convenience Fee" badge. **(4) Setnayan's economics are protected by design** — the 5% gross / ~3% net per ₱100K booking is identical in both options; only the visibility of the fee on the customer's receipt shifts. **(5) Customer-side surfaces:** badge chip on vendor marketplace listing/search/profile (renders "No Convenience Fee" with tooltip "This vendor covers the platform convenience fee. The price you see is the price you pay."); search filter chip "No convenience fee" lets couples narrow to opt-in vendors; cart drawer shows the convenience-fee row as "Convenience fee ✓ covered by vendor ₱0.00" in muted text when applicable. **(6) Vendor-side surface** (0022 Settings panel): toggle with dynamic financial-preview calculator showing vendor's net on a sample booking under both options side-by-side using the vendor's actual listed prices; conversion-lift estimate placeholder copy in V1 until population data exists; toggle is single-admin authority for the vendor's own account, logged in `vendor_audit_log`. **(7) Marketing site (0015 § 8.5):** second worked-example block appears next to the default Option B example showing "Some vendors cover the convenience fee — look for the badge. With these vendors, the listed price is the all-in price." Engineering hooks into `apps/web/app/(marketing)/page.tsx` and the eventual `/pricing` page. **(8) Snapshot discipline preserved** — vendor flag flip applies only to NEW cart_items, consistent with the 2026-05-12 "price snapshot at add-to-cart time" decision in 0034 § 3.3. Vendors flipping mid-cart can't retroactively change pricing for couples already in checkout. | Three drivers. **First, Filipino couples strongly prefer "all-in" pricing** — many wedding vendors already absorb platform fees informally to keep their headline price flush with the booking total customers mentally anchor on (Filipino budgeting psychology is highly headline-number-sensitive: ₱100,000 reads as "₱100K wedding line item" but ₱105,000 reads as "₱105K + a surprise"). Surfacing the practice as a first-class opt-in turns informal vendor-side absorption into a structured platform feature with a visible badge — vendors who do it gain a transparent marketing differentiator; vendors who don't aren't forced into a compressed-margin race. **Second, Setnayan's revenue is unchanged regardless** — the 5% is always Setnayan's ledger revenue, whether the customer or the vendor sees it on the receipt. This is a pure UX/marketing toggle for Setnayan; economics are protected by design. **Third, the toggle gives Setnayan a clean search/filter chip** ("No convenience fee") that couples will actually use, which drives marketplace engagement + creates a competitive dynamic that pulls more vendors toward the badge (especially in saturated categories like wedding photography where 100+ vendors compete on the same listing surface). **Flipside accepted:** vendors who absorb sacrifice ~5% revenue; the 0022 settings panel's financial-preview UI makes this cost crystal-clear upfront so vendors don't accidentally opt in without understanding the math. Vendors with strong demand signal (verified · 3+ completed events · high review scores) shouldn't need to absorb to compete; vendors building a portfolio may find the badge worth the margin sacrifice. | `0006_vendors_management/0006_vendors_management.md` new "Convenience-fee absorption opt-in (locked 2026-05-16 PM)" section between "Vendor Verification flow" and "Acceptance criteria" (schema column + vendor-side surface + customer-side surface + marketing positioning + cross-reference to 0034 § 6.8); | 2026-05-17 | **Iteration 0016 Setnayan Concierge simplified to single-SKU + 3-day trial + tiered abuse enforcement — supersedes the 2026-05-16 2-tier model (Essentials retired).** Second 2026-05-17 row. Owner directive after reviewing the 2-tier model: prefers the confident single-product posture (one SKU = one product, not a discount stack) and a shorter 3-day trial gated at the account level rather than the event level, to close the multi-account / multi-event trial-cycling vector. **(1) New SKU shape — single tier:** **`concierge_complete`** unchanged at ₱4,999 / 499,900 centavos / 12-month access · full 9-step roadmap · daily nudges · priority vendor matching · honeymoon planning included. The 2026-05-16 `concierge_essentials` SKU (₱2,499 / 6-month) is **retired same-week** (marked `is_active=FALSE` in service_catalog with `-- retired 2026-05-17 · single-SKU model adopted` comment). DIY mode unchanged (free default). **(2) Trial duration + scope tightened:** **7-day card-less preview** → **3-day card-less free trial**. Scope changes from `concierge_preview_used_at` per **event** to `concierge_trial_used_at` per **account** — closes the prior loophole where a couple could create Event A, exhaust trial, then create Event B for another trial on the same account. **(3) Tiered abuse-enforcement framework (NEW V1 surface — flagged explicitly per locked-guardrails rule):** progressive penalties on confirmed multi-account trial cycling: **Strike 1** → `concierge_enforcement_level = 'warning'` (audit only · trial still works · in-app notification) · **Strike 2** → `'trial_banned'` (trial blocked but can still buy ₱4,999 Concierge) · **Strike 3+** → `'full_banned'` (trial blocked AND buy blocked — account loses Concierge access entirely). All transitions admin-actioned via new 0023 Concierge Abuse tab — **no auto-ban**, only auto-flag for review. **(4) Detection trigger — on trial-start attempt** (per owner pick · cheapest detection path · only runs when `start_concierge_trial(event_id)` is called). Similarity check against all `concierge_trial_used_at IS NOT NULL` accounts using weighted signals: same `wedding_date` exact match (high) · same venue name fuzzy match token-set ratio ≥ 0.85 (high) · same venue address (high) · same couple-name overlap (high) · same phone number across accounts (CRITICAL auto-flag) · same payment-method fingerprint BDO last4 / GCash mobile (CRITICAL) · same device fingerprint / IP within 14-day window (medium). Composite score ≥ threshold → flag inserted into `concierge_abuse_flags` table with `status='pending_review'` AND trial-start BLOCKED with copy *"Your account is under review. Contact support if you believe this is in error."* + appeal-ticket CTA. **(5) Admin review queue — new tab in 0023 admin console** (per owner pick) titled "Concierge Abuse" alongside Users / Vendors / Funnels / etc. Lists pending flags with similarity score · which signals fired · linked matched-account event details · two-admin authority NOT required (single-admin per § 9.1 since enforcement is tiered and reversible). Admin actions: **Clear** (false positive · flag closed · no strike) · **Confirm abuse** (strike increment · enforcement level auto-bumps per tier · audit-logged · in-app notification fires). **(6) Appeal path** (per owner pick): banned account sees *"Setnayan Concierge unavailable on this account. Contact support if you believe this is in error."* with a CTA that opens a 0029 help-center ticket routed to the abuse-review admin role. Admin can lift the enforcement level (decrement strike count) with reason logged. **(7) Schema additions:** new columns on `users` — `concierge_trial_used_at TIMESTAMPTZ` (one trial per account) · `concierge_abuse_strike_count INT NOT NULL DEFAULT 0` · `concierge_enforcement_level TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT 'none'` (enum: `'none'|'warning'|'trial_banned'|'full_banned'`) · `concierge_enforcement_at TIMESTAMPTZ` · `concierge_enforcement_by UUID REFERENCES users(user_id)` · `concierge_enforcement_reason TEXT`. New table `concierge_abuse_flags` (flag_id PK · flagged_user_id · matched_user_ids UUID[] · similarity_score NUMERIC · signals JSONB · status enum `'pending_review'|'cleared'|'confirmed_abuse'` · created_at · reviewed_at · reviewed_by · admin_notes) with index `idx_concierge_abuse_flags_status` on (status, created_at DESC). The 2026-05-16 `events.concierge_preview_used_at` column (event-level preview tracker) is **retired in the same migration** — superseded by `users.concierge_trial_used_at` at the account level. **(8) Server actions renamed:** `start_concierge_preview(event_id)` → **`start_concierge_trial(event_id)`** with new signature handling account-level gating + similarity-check pre-flight + abuse-flag insertion on hit + enforcement-level check + idempotency (returns existing trial row if account already in `'preview'` status — but does NOT re-trigger similarity check on re-entry). **(9) Choice card (iteration 0000 § 2.5b):** 3 options → **2 options** (DIY + Setnayan Concierge ₱4,999). "Not ready to commit? Try 3 days free →" link below picker, hidden when `users.concierge_trial_used_at IS NOT NULL` OR `users.concierge_enforcement_level IN ('trial_banned', 'full_banned')`. **(10) Settings tab (iteration 0025):** picker collapses to single-Concierge option · 3-day trial CTA gated by account-level state · new "Enforcement state" surface for warning / trial_banned / full_banned states with appeal-ticket CTA · "Trial used" empty-state when `concierge_trial_used_at IS NOT NULL` (couple has used their one trial). **(11) Dashboard variants (iteration 0021 § 2.0b):** 4-state model preserved (DIY · Trial · Active · Expired) but Trial copy updated from 7-day to 3-day; banner CTAs collapse to single "Continue with Concierge (₱4,999)" button (no Essentials option). Banned account variant shows the explanatory copy + appeal CTA in place of the upgrade banner. | Two drivers. **First, single-SKU = product confidence.** The 2026-05-16 2-tier model still read as a SaaS discount ladder ("we have a cheap option and a real option") rather than a confident product posture ("here's the product · ₱4,999"). The Apple iPhone playbook (one price per model · optional accessories not optional tiers) lands better in the wedding category where couples spend ₱200K–2M on the event itself — Setnayan looks more like a category-defining product and less like a subscription menu. The ₱2,499 Essentials tier was projected to under-perform vs Complete anyway (the savings math at ₱500/6-month-extra didn't anchor; most converters were projected to pick Complete), so retiring it removes a low-conversion SKU + simplifies the marketing surface + removes a "save face" branch that some couples were going to pick for budget reasons rather than fit reasons. Net revenue per Concierge customer is now uniformly ₱4,999 (was bimodal ₱2,499/₱4,999), which simplifies internal forecasting + makes the pricing-page anchor (₱25K coordinator vs ₱4,999 Concierge) cleaner without the ₱2,499 mid-anchor cluttering the comparison. **Second, the 3-day trial + account-level cap + tiered enforcement framework closes a real multi-account abuse vector** the owner identified. The 2026-05-16 7-day per-event preview model had two known holes: (a) one couple creating Event A, exhausting trial, creating Event B on the same account for another trial; (b) one person creating Account A, exhausting trial, creating Account B with a near-identical wedding profile for another trial. Hole (a) closes mechanically with the per-account cap (`users.concierge_trial_used_at`). Hole (b) requires similarity detection — the on-trial-start check + admin review queue + tiered enforcement framework. The tiered enforcement (warn → trial-ban → full-ban) is more forgiving than a single-strike auto-ban because (i) similarity hits include genuine false positives (sibling couples planning at the same venue · wedding-planner accounts managing multiple clients · same venue different couples on different dates) which need admin judgment, and (ii) the progressive penalty creates a forgiveness lane on first offense that reduces customer-support load from legit users wrongly hit on the first flag. Filipino-market consideration: wedding-coordinator accounts and multi-client agencies are a legit segment that will pattern-match on venue/date overlap with their own clients — these are NOT abuse and need admin-confirmable clearing on first flag. The tiered model gives the platform room to learn without permanently banning legitimate edge cases. **Trial duration 7 → 3 days drivers:** (a) 3 days is enough to walk through the 9-step roadmap and see the Concierge value (each step is ~5-10 minutes for an engaged couple); (b) shorter trial reduces account-creation incentive for trial-cycling (3 days of free access is less "worth the abuse effort" than 7 days); (c) the ₱4,999 sticker is now anchored against "a focused 72-hour preview" rather than "a leisurely week" — better matches the premium-product feel that supports the single-SKU framing. **Risk accepted:** lower trial → buy conversion than the 7-day model would have produced. Mitigated by (i) the 9-step roadmap being demonstrably valuable in the first hour (couples see deadline alerts + vendor picks within Step 1-2), (ii) the choice card still offering DIY-free as the no-cost option for couples who aren't ready, (iii) the human-coordinator anchor still doing the heavy lifting on pricing-page conversion. | `CLAUDE.md` decision log (this entry) · iteration table row #0016 (rewritten to single-SKU + 3-day trial + tiered enforcement framing); | 2026-05-17 | **Payment Options Policy Matrix locked — admin console surfaces per-account-type policy grid controlling which payment methods are available to 4 scopes (Customers · Vendors · Certified Vendors · per-Event override).** Owner directive with "always remember" weight. **Four scopes:** Customers (couples paying Setnayan) · Vendors (un-certified / coming_soon) · Certified Vendors (verified vendor-payee side) · Events (per-event override that supersedes account-type default). **Schema (new 0023 § 3.5f):** extend `payment_method_config` with `enabled_for_customers BOOLEAN DEFAULT TRUE` · `enabled_for_vendors_coming_soon BOOLEAN DEFAULT FALSE` · `enabled_for_vendors_certified BOOLEAN DEFAULT TRUE`; new `event_payment_options_override` table (event_id · method_key · enabled · set_by_admin_id · reason · set_at) with PK (event_id, method_key) and index on event_id. New memory file `project_setnayan_payment_options_policy.md` + MEMORY.md index updated. **`.docx` mirror NOT regenerated** (pandoc unavailable). **Engineering implementation pending:** `ALTER TABLE payment_method_config ADD COLUMN enabled_for_customers / enabled_for_vendors_coming_soon / enabled_for_vendors_certified` migrations; `CREATE TABLE event_payment_options_override` migration; 0023 admin console grid UI component (matrix toggles + per-event override sub-view); cart-options server action update to read policy at checkout. **Sequencing note:** adds per-account-type policy primitive to admin payments architecture, complementing yesterday's flat 5.0% inbound fee + today's Maya Bulk Fund Transfer outbound · V1 sparse matrix · V1.5+ auto-expand · no V1 SKU/feature scope expansion. |
| 2026-05-17 | **Setnayan Concierge access switched from fixed-12-months to wedding-anchored with a 24-month cap — fixes the long-engagement edge case where a couple with a 3-year engagement was losing access 24 months before their wedding day.** Third 2026-05-17 row. Owner-surfaced edge-case fix: under the prior fixed-12-month spec, a couple setting their wedding 3 years out and paying ₱4,999 would have lost Concierge 24 months before they actually needed it — a bad customer experience that risked refund disputes and brand damage. **(1) New access-duration formula:** `concierge_expires_at = LEAST(GREATEST(wedding_date + INTERVAL '30 days', activation_date + INTERVAL '12 months'), activation_date + INTERVAL '24 months')`. **(2) Guarantees:** every paying couple gets a **floor of 12 months** of access from activation (so couples with no date yet OR very-soon weddings still get a full year), and a **ceiling of 24 months** from activation (caps margin exposure on extreme long engagements). **(3) Sweet-spot ranges:** wedding 3 months out → 12 months access (12-month floor wins · 9 months post-wedding tail) · wedding 8 months out → 12 months (floor wins) · wedding 12 months out → 12.5 months (wedding+30 wins) · wedding 18 months out → 18.5 months (wedding+30) · wedding 24 months out → 24 months (cap) · wedding 36 months out → 24 months (cap · couple sees 24-month advisory). **(4) NULL wedding_date handling:** at activation, if `events.wedding_date IS NULL` (typical because the choice card per 0000 § 2.5a defers date entry to Concierge Step 1 OR Profile), `expires_at` defaults to `activation_date + INTERVAL '12 months'`. When `wedding_date` is later set/updated, expires_at recomputes per the formula above — but **only extends, never shrinks**. **(5) Wedding-date update behavior:** if couple updates `wedding_date` to a later date after activation, `concierge_expires_at` extends to the new wedding+30 (up to the 24-month cap). If couple updates to an earlier date, expires_at STAYS at the original value (they already paid for that runway; no shrinkage). **(6) Long-engagement advisory:** when `wedding_date` is set or updated to > 24 months from `concierge_activated_at`, a one-time in-app + email advisory surfaces: *"Your wedding is more than 24 months away. Setnayan Concierge covers up to 24 months from your purchase date — you'll lose access ~{N} months before your wedding. We recommend renewing closer to your wedding for full coverage."* Stamp a `concierge_long_engagement_advised_at TIMESTAMPTZ` column on `events` to prevent re-fire on subsequent date updates. **(7) Schema:** the existing `events.concierge_expires_at TIMESTAMPTZ` column from the 2026-05-17 lock is unchanged structurally — just the formula that populates it changes; new column `events.concierge_long_engagement_advised_at TIMESTAMPTZ` added for the advisory de-dup. **(8) Server-action update:** `activate_concierge(event_id, order_id)` computes `expires_at` per the formula at activation time using whatever `wedding_date` value is present (NULL → defaults to activation+12mo). New server action `recompute_concierge_expiry(event_id)` fires from the `events.wedding_date` update trigger (or app-layer hook) — recomputes per the formula + applies the extend-only rule + triggers the advisory if conditions met. **(9) Daily expiry sweep unchanged** — still flips status to `'expired'` when `expires_at < NOW()`. **(10) Refund/dispute exposure capped** — under the prior model, a couple paying ₱4,999 with a 36-month engagement and losing access at month 12 had a strong "I was misled" case; under the new model the worst-case is a couple with a >24-month engagement, and they see the advisory at purchase/date-entry, so they make an informed choice. | Two drivers. **First, the prior fixed-12-months model created a real product-market mismatch for long engagements.** Filipino couples plan 6–24 months out modally (per the locked planning-runway assumptions in iteration 0016 § 1), but a non-trivial tail (anniversary-fresh engagements · couples who got engaged years before they could afford the wedding · couples coordinating with overseas family that need long lead times) plans 24–48 months out. Under the prior model these couples paid ₱4,999 and lost access well before the wedding — the value proposition collapsed precisely when it mattered most. The wedding-anchored formula matches the actual job-to-be-done ("help us plan THIS wedding") without breaking the simple single-SKU pricing model. **Second, the 24-month cap protects Setnayan's per-event economics.** A truly unbounded wedding-anchored model (e.g., access until wedding_date + 30 days with no cap) would mean a 5-year-engagement couple gets 5 years of Concierge for ₱4,999 — that's an effective rate of ₱83/month, well below the per-customer economics this SKU was sized for (~₱416/month over 12 months). Capping at 24 months means the worst-case effective rate is ~₱208/month, still healthy margin, and the affected customer segment (>24-month engagements) is small. The advisory + renewal-encouragement copy turns the cap from a hidden gotcha into a transparent renewal hook. **Third, the 12-month floor protects the short-engagement / no-date-yet case** — couples who buy Concierge before setting a wedding date OR with very-soon weddings still get a guaranteed full year of access including post-wedding planning tail, matching the brand promise that "Access doesn't end at the wedding date." **Filipino-market consideration:** the long-engagement-advisory copy will be in EN-primary luxurious-Filipino-modern voice (per COWORK.md line 74) and will not penalize couples who got engaged years in advance — it surfaces as a friendly "heads-up" not a "you bought wrong" guilt-trip. **Extend-only rule on date changes** is the customer-fair default — a couple who postpones their wedding deserves more runway; a couple who moves it earlier already paid for the longer runway and gets to keep it (no surprise shrinkage). | `CLAUDE.md` decision log (this entry) · iteration table row #0016 (rewritten to reflect wedding-anchored access + 24-month cap framing);
