| 2026-05-18 | **Vendor contracts scope shrink — upload-only · Setnayan hosts PDFs but does NOT facilitate signing. Dual e-sig flow from earlier row 414 retired.** Tenth 2026-05-18 row. Later-same-day amendment to row 414 (which earlier today retired Contract Intelligence iteration 0032 and was scheduled to be REPLACED by the free built-in dual e-signature flow; that replacement flow is now also dropped). Owner directive verbatim: *"we do not need contract intelligence. their contract is not covered by our app. they can just place it there for reference. but we will not make contracts for them."* Net model: Setnayan hosts vendor-uploaded PDF contracts so couples can view + download them for reference; couples and vendors handle the actual signing flow externally (email, in-person, separate e-sig tool of their choice). Setnayan is a document host, not a signing facilitator. **(1) What's removed from the app code (shipped in PR #118 `claude/vendor-contracts-shrink-upload-only` on `iscasasola/setnayan-platform`, auto-merge armed):** the entire signature-capture surface area — `apps/web/app/_components/signature-canvas.tsx` (full file delete, −142 lines, HTML5 pointer-events DPR-aware canvas + PNG data-URL emission); the `sendContractForSignature` server action renamed to `publishContractToCouple` so the publish-to-couple step retains its semantic but loses the "for signature" framing; the `signContractAsVendor` server action (full delete); the entire couple-side `apps/web/app/dashboard/[eventId]/contracts/actions.ts` file (full delete, −115 lines — the only export was `signContractAsCustomer` so the file has nothing left to host); all `SignatureCanvas` mounts + `Image`-of-signature renders + "Awaiting signatures" / "Sign as customer" / "Sign as vendor" UI blocks from both the vendor detail page (`apps/web/app/vendor-dashboard/contracts/[contractId]/page.tsx`, −137 lines) and the couple detail page (`apps/web/app/dashboard/[eventId]/contracts/[contractId]/page.tsx`, −131 lines); the helper exports `parseSignatureDataUrl` · `fetchContractSignatures` · `findSignatureByRole` · `SIGNATURE_MAX_BYTES` · `ContractSignatureRow` type · `SignerRole` type from `apps/web/lib/contracts.ts` (kept the upload + status + cancel helpers, dropped everything signature-shaped). **(2) What's kept (forward compat + minimal viable hosting):** `vendor_contracts` table + every RLS policy on it stays as-is — vendor owns their contracts (matched via `vendor_profiles.user_id`), event members (couple + coordinator) read non-draft contracts on their event, admin reads for moderation; this preserves the option to re-introduce signing later (V1.5+ or V2) without a schema rewrite, and the table is already the right shape for pure-hosting use (file_url + file_name + file_size_bytes ≤25MB + mime_type=`application/pdf` + status + uploaded_by_user_id + event_id + order_id all stay populated by the upload flow). **`vendor_contract_signatures` table stays as dead schema** — kept idempotent (no destructive cleanup migration in this PR, no data loss risk since it never had production rows), to be removed in V1.5+ housekeeping; same goes for the `vendor_contract_check_fully_signed()` trigger from migration `20260518200000_vendor_contracts_dual_esign_retire_0032.sql` (also dead but harmless — fires only on inserts into the dead `vendor_contract_signatures` table, which the app no longer writes to). **Vendor upload flow stays end-to-end** — `/vendor-dashboard/contracts` (list) → `/vendor-dashboard/contracts/new` (upload form, gated to events the vendor has an active chat thread with) → `/vendor-dashboard/contracts/[contractId]` (detail page with publish + cancel buttons). **Cancel action stays unchanged** — vendor can cancel a published contract with a reason; cancellation flows through `cancelled_at` + `cancelled_by_user_id` + `cancelled_reason` columns and both sides see appropriate copy. **DB status enum unchanged** — `status ∈ {draft, sent_for_signature, fully_signed, cancelled}` stays as-is at the DB level for forward compat (no migration touches the CHECK constraint), but the UI semantics shift: `sent_for_signature` is now surfaced as "Visible to couple" in the vendor dashboard list + detail page (the publish action just transitions `draft → sent_for_signature` and stops there), and `fully_signed` is now unreachable in production code paths (no signatures ever get inserted, so the seal-trigger never fires, so the parent contract never flips out of `sent_for_signature`). The mismatch between DB enum semantics + UI semantics is documented inline in `apps/web/lib/contracts.ts` so future engineers reading the code understand why the enum value name is stale. **(3) New UI copy makes the externality of signing explicit + load-bearing.** Vendor detail page reads *"Setnayan hosts this PDF for the couple to reference — we do not facilitate signing. Handle signatures externally (email, in-person, your own e-sig tool) and keep the signed copy with your records."* Couple detail page mirrors it with vendor-attribution: *"This contract was uploaded by {vendor} for your reference. Setnayan hosts the PDF so both sides have a copy on hand — signing happens between you and the vendor outside the app (email, in-person, or your own e-sig tool). Keep a signed copy with your records."* Both blocks treat the externality of signing as the primary message, not a fine-print disclaimer — that's the brand-voice + liability posture this row locks in. List pages on both sides + the publish CTA label ("Make visible to couple") reinforce the same framing. **(4) Why the owner made this call** (drivers behind the further shrink, beyond the verbatim quote above). **First, signing isn't Setnayan's core competency** — wedding-planning artifacts (timeline, vendor pack, Concierge wizard, Save-the-Date, Panood, Patiktok) are the value Setnayan ships; e-signature is a commodity feature already solved by DocuSign · HelloSign · Adobe Sign · Signaturit · plus free workarounds (emailing a signed scan, in-person signing at vendor meetings) that the wedding industry already uses comfortably. Building it in-house gates Setnayan against best-in-class e-sig tooling for no upside, and bundling it as a Setnayan feature signals to couples + vendors that the app is the contract authority — which it isn't. **Second, liability scope shrinks substantially.** A dual-sig flow where Setnayan captures + stores signature PNGs + IP + UA + signed_at + serves them via signed URLs makes Setnayan an arguable party to the contract's execution (or at minimum a custodian of e-signature evidence) under Philippine Electronic Commerce Act (RA 8792) + Rules on Electronic Evidence — even with disclaimers, a couple or vendor in dispute could subpoena Setnayan as evidence custodian, force preservation orders, and surface the platform as a third-party in litigation. Pure document hosting (PDF storage with view/download access) keeps Setnayan in the same posture as Dropbox or Google Drive — a passive document host with no signature authority. **Third, simpler UX for both sides.** Vendors already upload signed PDFs to clients via email constantly — adding a "sign in Setnayan" step is friction, not value. Couples likewise prefer to read + sign on their own time + with their own tooling (e.g., printing for traditional ink signature with family witnesses present, as is common in Filipino wedding contracts). The Setnayan flow shrinks to upload → publish → both sides can see/download → done, which fits naturally alongside the chat thread where the contract was negotiated. **Fourth, couples and vendors handle signing flows wherever they prefer** — the spec does not need to pick a signing UX for the entire ecosystem; the wedding industry's existing patterns (email signed PDFs, in-person at venue site visits, separate e-sig tool for traveling couples) cover the spectrum without Setnayan opining. The "we host the PDF for reference, you sign externally" posture matches how vendors currently operate with non-Setnayan clients, so adoption friction drops to near-zero. **(5) PR #118 ship stats:** net −721 lines / +113 lines across 8 files (`apps/web/app/_components/signature-canvas.tsx` DELETED · `apps/web/app/dashboard/[eventId]/contracts/[contractId]/page.tsx` MODIFIED · `apps/web/app/dashboard/[eventId]/contracts/actions.ts` DELETED · `apps/web/app/dashboard/[eventId]/contracts/page.tsx` MODIFIED · `apps/web/app/vendor-dashboard/contracts/[contractId]/page.tsx` MODIFIED · `apps/web/app/vendor-dashboard/contracts/actions.ts` MODIFIED · `apps/web/app/vendor-dashboard/contracts/page.tsx` MODIFIED · `apps/web/lib/contracts.ts` MODIFIED). Auto-merge armed pending CI (typecheck + lint + secret scan + Vercel preview + Lighthouse). **(6) Affected spec files:** `CLAUDE.md` (this row); `0032_contract_intelligence/0032_contract_intelligence.md` may need a tail amendment noting the dual-sig replacement was also dropped (was already RETIRED earlier today per row 414 — touch-up just clarifies that the replacement flow is upload-only, deferred to next Cowork pass since 0032 is owner WIP in working tree); `0034_payments_and_cart/0034_payments_and_cart.md` may need a touch-up confirming no contract-related SKUs are reintroduced as part of the e-sig drop (none should be — `contract_intelligence_upgrade` + `contract_intelligence_per_contract` stay retired per row 414, no new contract-related SKU added — deferred to next Cowork pass). **(7) Memory + cross-references.** This row supersedes the "Schema · App surfaces · Signing-order policy · V1.1 follow-ups" portions of row 414 — specifically: row 414's `vendor_contract_signatures` table + signing-order policy + `SignatureCanvas` component + sign-as-vendor + sign-as-customer actions + dual-sig trigger semantics are all DROPPED. Row 414's contract-intelligence-RETIRED decision + SKU retirement + Anthropic Console scope shrink + `vendor_pro_weekly` description update + `vendor_contracts` parent table + R2 bucket reuse + RLS pattern + upload flow + cancel action all STAND. V1.1 follow-ups previously noted (PDF stamping, email notifications, audit-trail export) are now N/A — they were features of the sign-in-Setnayan flow that no longer exists. If a future iteration ever re-introduces in-Setnayan signing, the schema already supports it (re-enable the signatures table + trigger + restore the canvas component + actions) so the forward path is preserved at zero cost. **(8) `.docx` mirror NOT regenerated** (pandoc availability unverified in this session — flag for future Cowork pass per established convention). | Owner directive 2026-05-18 — second amendment of the day on contract scope (row 414 retired the AI-analysis SKU + introduced dual e-sig; this row drops the dual e-sig too and lands on pure document hosting). **First and most decisive: signing isn't Setnayan's core competency** — every hour spent on signature-capture UX is an hour not spent on Concierge wizard + Panood OAuth + vendor saturation rules + Pakanta + Save-the-Date, which ARE the V1 differentiators. The e-sig market is mature + commoditized + the wedding industry already uses email-signed-PDFs + in-person ink signatures comfortably; Setnayan adds no value as a signing facilitator. **Second: liability scope shrinks substantially under Philippine Electronic Commerce Act (RA 8792) + Rules on Electronic Evidence** — capturing signature PNGs + IP + UA + signed_at + serving them via signed URLs makes Setnayan a custodian of e-signature evidence and arguably a party to contract execution, exposing the platform to subpoena risk + preservation orders + third-party litigation involvement. Pure document hosting (PDF storage + view/download access only) keeps Setnayan in passive-host posture identical to Dropbox or Google Drive — no signature authority, no evidence custody beyond the file itself. The legal advice owner would otherwise need before launching dual e-sig (electronic signature validity attestation, jurisdiction handling for couples + vendors in different cities, dispute resolution custody policy, NPC-compliant signature-image retention + deletion policy) all evaporates with the pure-hosting model. **Third: simpler UX for both sides** — vendors already email signed PDFs constantly + couples prefer their own tooling (printing for ink signatures with family witnesses, common in Filipino weddings); adding a "sign in Setnayan" step is friction not value, and the upload → publish → view-download flow fits naturally alongside the chat thread where the contract was negotiated in the first place. **Fourth: couples and vendors handle signing wherever they prefer** — wedding industry already has comfortable patterns spanning email-signed PDFs, in-person at venue site visits, separate e-sig tools for traveling couples — Setnayan doesn't need to pick one. **Fifth: code surface shrinks ~721 lines without losing user value** — the signature-canvas component (142 lines), the signing actions (~221 lines across two files), and the signing UI blocks on both detail pages (~268 lines combined) all delete cleanly because they were never the value of the contracts feature; the value is the chat → contract upload → both-sides-can-reference loop, which stays intact. **Sixth: pilot scope (row 8 of today) benefits** — the 5-20 person pilot was going to exercise dual e-sig with real family members signing real PDFs, which carries its own pilot-quality-of-experience risk (canvas signature quality on touch devices, signature image render fidelity across browsers, what-if-trigger-doesn't-fire failure modes); dropping the signing flow before pilot eliminates that risk surface entirely and lets pilot focus on the harder lock/unlock cycle work that does need real coverage. **Flipsides accepted:** (a) Setnayan loses a marketed feature ("vendor and couple both sign in-app") that would have differentiated from raw chat tools — owner judges this OK because the differentiation was thin and the maintenance burden was real; (b) dead schema (`vendor_contract_signatures` table + `vendor_contract_check_fully_signed` trigger) stays in production until V1.5+ cleanup — acceptable because it's harmless (no writes, no triggers fire) and the cleanup is mechanical when prioritized; (c) DB status enum value `sent_for_signature` is stale relative to its UI label "Visible to couple" — acceptable because (i) UI label decoupling from DB enum is a common + survivable mismatch, (ii) renaming the enum requires a migration + a code-wide string replacement which is not worth doing now, and (iii) the inline code comment in `apps/web/lib/contracts.ts` documents the mismatch for future engineers. **Sequencing note:** Tenth 2026-05-18 row · second amendment of the day to vendor contracts scope (after row 414's Contract Intelligence retirement + dual-sig introduction earlier today) · resolves row 414's "Replaced by free built-in dual e-signature" promise by replacing it instead with "Replaced by upload-only hosting; signing handled externally" · no SKU changes (row 414's SKU retirements stand) · no schema changes (`vendor_contracts` + `vendor_contract_signatures` tables stay as-is for forward compat) · ships via PR #118 with net −721 / +113 lines · `.docx` mirror regeneration deferred (pandoc availability unverified, flag for future Cowork pass per established convention). |

Content moat solved by enabling vendors to populate at scale through structured tools, not by hiring an editorial team. **(7) Files created** (spec corpus only): `0043_wedding_type_picker/0043_wedding_type_picker.md` + `0044_per_category_schemas/0044_per_category_schemas.md` + `0045_product_catalogs/0045_product_catalogs.md` + `0046_wedding_showcase/0046_wedding_showcase.md` + `0047_style_driven_marketplaces/0047_style_driven_marketplaces.md`. **Files updated:** `App_Build_Status.md` (headline counter row added "📐 V1.1 spec drafted (2026-05-19) | 5 | 0043, 0044, 0045, 0046, 0047" + 5 per-iteration rows appended after 0037); `02_Specifications/00_Iteration_Connection_Map.md` (new § 7 "V1.1 Iterations — content engine + multi-faith expansion" with iteration roster + dependency arrows preserving strict forward sequencing); `CLAUDE.md` (this row). **No app code changes · no schema migrations · no `.docx` regeneration** (per owner scope choice). **(8) Why this matters strategically.** Session's core insight came from gap-analyzing WedMeGood (India's leading wedding planning platform, 10K+ Play Store reviews, 2M+ photo inspiration gallery, "Genie" concierge, faceted Real Weddings). WedMeGood's content/community moat is the single biggest gap vs Setnayan. Setnayan can't out-content WedMeGood at launch (cold-start on 2M photos) but Setnayan CAN out-STRUCTURE them: per-category schemas (10+ distinct filter UIs vs WedMeGood's one generic vendor profile), product catalogs (compound queries WedMeGood lacks), faceted Real Weddings database (per-filter-combination SEO landing pages WedMeGood doesn't generate), multi-faith taxonomy (INC + Muslim + Cultural coverage WedMeGood structurally cannot replicate). Combined with the 2026-05-18 V1.5→V1 promotion of in-app production tools (Papic/Panood/Pailaw/Patiktok/Pakanta), the V1.1 spec set turns Setnayan's marketplace into infrastructure that is **measurably more complex than WedMeGood across multiple structural dimensions** (~100+ specialized sub-categories vs WedMeGood's ~30, per-category schemas vs generic profiles, compound product-level search vs vendor-only search, multi-faith ceremony × venue compound filtering vs single-faith Indian wedding flow, Stations & Booths category WedMeGood doesn't have, faith-compatibility tags on consumables WedMeGood doesn't have). **(9) Engineering implications post-pilot.** V1.1 base ship (~6-8 weeks post-pilot): 0043 + 0044 + 0045 + 0046 + 0047-Stylist marketplace ship together. V1.1.1 → V1.1.6: remaining marketplace launches (Food → Photo → Music → Attire → Host → Stations&Booths) at ~2-week cadence per 0047 § Phasing. V1.2: remaining ~100 vendor sub-categories get schemas, Pre-Nup Locations marketplace added, faceted SEO landing pages auto-generated, vendor compare view (3-up desktop / swipe-with-sticky-attribute mobile per WedMeGood pattern), product-level SEO URLs. V1.3+: AI-powered natural-language search, ML-driven personalized recommendations, cross-category bundles. Without per-category schemas + product catalogs + showcase auto-population, "venue for vendors" stays an aspiration; with them, it becomes the actual product surface. **Second, multi-faith schema must ship from day 1 to avoid migration trap** — if V1.1 shipped Catholic-only and tried to add INC/Muslim/Christian later, every event row needs backfill, vendor onboarding wizard needs faith-compat retrofit, Concierge wizard needs new branches, showcase taxonomy needs migration, marketplace filters need rework. The cost of being correct from day 1 is one extra week of spec work now vs months of migration pain later. **Third, INC + Muslim are structural moats WedMeGood cannot replicate even if they wanted to** — requires deep PH ethnography (Maranao Singkil vs Tausug Pangalay distinctions), faith-specific vendor recruitment (INC ministers, halal caterers, Muslim Imams via BARMM community trust), faith-rule encoding (INC alcohol-free strict, Muslim halal-only + gender-separation, etc.). (e) **The reception** — narrative + photo grid + optional embedded clip from 0011 Panood broadcast (if couple used Panood + curated 3-5 clips, never full stream). (f) **Our team** — vendor credit cards 2-column grid, each with vendor logo + name + category + couple's 1-line testimonial + **vendor's own approved blurb** + link to vendor profile in marketplace. (g) **By the numbers** — anonymized real-budget brackets (opt-in via 0007 Budget) + guest count + prep timeline. (h) **Photo gallery** — couple-curated subset from 0012 Papic (default 20, max 30 — never raw feed). (i) **Save / share / inspire other couples** — bottom CTA strip with Save · Share buttons (V1.5 may add Comments). Fixed = couples can't reorder/delete sections (consistency across all weddings critical for /weddings browse); within-section is rich (multiple paragraphs/photos/quotes allowed). Couple edits inline (rich-text per-section). Solves cold-start: couples don't stare at blank editor; they refine a draft. New table `event_editorial_vendor_blurbs`: composite PK (event_id, vendor_id) · blurb_text VARCHAR(200) NOT NULL · status (pending / approved / rejected) · submitted_at · couple_responded_at · couple_edit_text (if couple edited before approving) · created_at · updated_at. But Setnayan CAN out-STRUCTURE editorial production: every couple's editorial is auto-drafted from the same 5-prompt wizard reusing the Concierge AI Brain, every editorial follows the same 9-section template (consistent UX + faceted browse), every vendor gets a structured blurb path back into the editorial (vendor-side engagement loop). A 5-prompt wizard → AI-assembled draft → couple-refines flow gets to ~60-70% completion based on the same pattern in vendor-facing wizards (Etsy seller bio AI assist, Substack post starters). Without AI heavy-lift, the editorial system would ship empty for months even with real weddings happening. **Third, Concierge Brain reuse compounds prior investment.** 2026-05-18 row 6 locked the Concierge AI architecture (Cloudflare Workers AI Llama 3.1 8B for DIY · Anthropic Haiku 4.5 for paid tier · curated Filipino-wedding knowledge base · per-event-anchored memory). Same engineering, two distinct user surfaces. **Fourth, vendor blurb workflow creates a sticky two-sided engagement loop.** Vendors get notified when an event they served has Phase 4 active → vendor submits blurb → couple approves → vendor's badge count increments in 0046 → couple's editorial enriches → vendor's profile auto-populates with the wedding showcase → other browsing couples see vendor's "used at N weddings" badge → conversion uplift. Vendors who don't engage with post-event blurbs lose badge count + showcase visibility. This is the engagement gravity well WedMeGood lacks (they don't credit vendors per-wedding consistently). **Fifth, fixed 9-section template is the right consistency-vs-creativity tradeoff.** A flexible block editor (Notion/Sanity style) maximizes creative freedom but kills /weddings browse consistency (some posts long, some short, some photo-heavy, some text-heavy). Fixed sections mean every editorial has the same structural footprint → /weddings browse cards render identically → faceted search returns predictable results → SEO meta-data is consistent across all editorials. V1.1 can add 2-3 alternate templates (Magazine for photo-forward couples · Minimal for text-forward · etc.) without breaking the consistency invariant — each template is fixed within itself. **Flipside accepted:** (a) ~4-6 weeks engineering for editorial system is real V1 scope-expansion cost; mitigation = reuse of Concierge Brain + existing photo/palette/vendor stores cuts net build to ~3-4 weeks of net-new work; (b) heavy-AI draft means some editorials will feel formulaic across the corpus — mitigation = couple's voice survives via inline editing + the 5 prompts are intentionally subjective (favorite moment, message to future couples) so AI output varies meaningfully; (c) vendor blurb workflow adds moderation burden on couples right when they're catching up post-honeymoon — mitigation = T+7 to T+30 submission window aligns with couple's natural return-to-platform moment + couple can bulk-approve all pending blurbs with one click + rejected blurbs are silent (no awkward vendor-couple interaction). **Sequencing note:** Fifth 2026-05-19 row · refinement layer on row 426's Phase 4 lock · no app code or schema migrations this row · Phase A code scope UNCHANGED (proceed when owner says "go Phase A") · editorial system engineering proceeds V1 sequenced for Dec 1 2026 couple-launch cutover · per memory PR auto-merge rule. |

Backfill migration for existing events to create `bride` + `groom` `event_moderators` rows runs once at deploy. **(8) `.docx` mirror NOT regenerated** (pandoc availability unverified in this session — flag for future Cowork pass per established convention). **(9) Sequencing note.** Seventh 2026-05-19 row · spec drafting + cross-iteration amendments · no SKU changes · no schema migrations · no app code · V1.2 work sequenced post V1.1 base ship · pilot scope unchanged · per "Setnayan PR auto-merge" memory rule subsequent PRs get `gh pr merge <num> --auto --merge`. | Owner directive 2026-05-19 — confirmed scope via AskUserQuestion ("Spec + amend existing iterations" + "Default-hide on attire + designated surprise items"). **First, multi-moderator + multi-payer is the structural answer to PH wedding multi-funding reality.** Filipino weddings are not couple-only purchases — they're multi-payer events with parents · sponsors (ninong/ninang) · entourage attendants · sometimes external planners all contributing financially and operationally. Adding 0048 + 0049 turns the platform into an actual wedding-community coordination layer, not just a couple-side tool. **Second, surprise restrictions are non-negotiable for PH cultural fit.** The bridal gown reveal is the single most-emphasized PH wedding moment — couples and their families take it seriously. A platform that accidentally leaks the gown to the groom via shared cart history or budget visibility would be perceived as fundamentally broken. Row-level RLS + default-hide rules + aggregate budget rounding + calendar title sanitization + per-template notification routing all combine to make leakage technically impossible (not just discouraged). This is the kind of detail competitors entering PH would miss → defensibility moat. **Third, the iteration split (0048 access model + 0049 cart attribution) is the right architecture.** Two iterations because two distinct concerns (who can do what · who pays for what). Same engineering team can ship both V1.2 together but spec-level separation keeps each iteration's surface area manageable. Spec captures this as a flagged dependency; owner can confirm at convenience without blocking 0048/0049 schema/UI work. **Sixth, V1.2 is genuinely additive — Pattern A backwards-compat preserved.** Existing V1.1 single-couple events continue to work exactly as today. Refinement layer on the Sixth 2026-05-19 row (Traffic monetization V1.1 scope lock) — adds 14 hard tenets to 0039 § 3.3a that future engineering cannot drift past without an explicit decision-log override. **Tenets locked:** (1) Static media only — no animation · no video · no audio · no rich media · image + text only (enforced at AdSense publisher console). (2) Default to non-personalized ads (NPA) for EVERYONE — even Accept-All visitors get contextual-only ads in V1.1; behavioural personalization is a separate later decision requiring its own row + Privacy Policy update. (3) Restricted unit sizes — 300×250 mobile · 728×90 tablet/desktop · 336×280 desktop article only; forbidden 970×250 billboard / 300×600 half-page / 320×100 large mobile banner / 970×90 large leaderboard (hard-coded in `apps/web/lib/ads/sizes.ts`). (4) Lazy-load — IntersectionObserver triggers SDK mount at rootMargin 200px below fold; no AdSense fetch on visits that don't reach the slot. (5) "Advertisement" microcopy label above every unit — 11px neutral gray, non-clickable, loader-injected. (6) Quiet container styling — 1px subtle border · 12px padding · 16px margin against content · no shadow · no accent · no animated hover. (7) Generous whitespace — min 48px vertical breathing room (CSS `margin-block: clamp(48px, 8vh, 96px)`). (8) No ad in the first viewport — ad mounts at least 100vh from page top (computed at mount-time, not page-template configurable). (9) Visitor-side dismiss — "×" affordance per slot; cookie `sn_ad_dismissed_v1` persists dismissed slot-IDs for 24h. (10) Page-load weight cap — ads-loader JS payload <80KB gzipped on first-paint; CI bundle-size budget enforces; PRs raising the cap fail build + require explicit owner sign-off + decision-log row. (11) One ad per page — even on long-form editorial · no "second ad at 2,000 words" pattern. (12) Quarterly visual audit by owner — 5 random article pages + 5 vendor pages + 3 marketing pages reviewed; "off-brand" units logged in `09_Operations/Ads_Visual_Audit_Log.md` + corrected within 48 hours. (13) Affiliate recommendation cards (0038) NEVER styled as ads — standard editorial card pattern from `/blog`, 12px gray disclosure microcopy beneath CTA, visible-but-not-heavy-handed. (14) Audit-resistant kill-switch — 0023 § 5.1 activation toggle remains the ultimate clean guarantee. **Below-the-fold scope widened from "vendor landing pages only" to EVERY surface** — owner directive overrides earlier 0039 § 3.3 row's permissive "below-the-fold on vendor landing pages" wording; no above-the-fold AdSense placement anywhere. **Acceptance tests added to 0039 § 9:** rows #17 (lazy-load), #18 (forbidden sizes rejected), #19 ("Advertisement" label always renders), #20 (NPA on Accept-All), #21 (static media only), #22 (page-load weight cap), #23 (dismiss persists per session). **0038 § 2.4 amended** to explicitly require editorial card pattern + 12px gray disclosure microcopy (not banner-ad styling). **Spec drift prevention:** any future engineering PR that touches `apps/web/lib/ads/` or `apps/web/styles/ads.css` must reference this row OR a superseding owner sign-off row · CI bundle-size + size-allowlist guards prevent silent regression. **No code in this row** — pure spec refinement on top of Sixth 2026-05-19 row; engineering hand-off list in `V1_Gap_Analysis_Status.md` 2026-05-19 addendum still canonical. **Worktree note:** session's primary worktree (`inspiring-kare-5ae933`) is an empty 1-commit fresh repo, not the `setnayan-platform` monorepo; engineering work for 0038 + 0039 sequenced for a future session against the actual monorepo worktree. |

Vendor experience preserved (event-context view, not moderator-personal-context). Surprise leak risk handled separately by 0048 visibility tags (bridal_gown chat thread auto-hides from groom regardless of moderator chat permissions). **(6) Sequencing note.** Ninth 2026-05-19 row · pure memory + spec rule alignment · no schema migrations · no SKU changes · no app code (the V1.2 engineering picks up the new rule when it lands per Seventh 2026-05-19 row timeline) · `.docx` mirror regeneration deferred (pandoc availability unverified). | Owner approval 2026-05-19 — single must-do item from the post-V1.2-spec-commit owner action list landed cleanly. **First, the rule update was the only blocker for 0019 V1.2 amendment consistency** — every other V1.2 spec change (0048 + 0049 + amendments to 0007/0021/0028/0034) was already self-consistent. With this approval, the full V1.2 spec set is internally coherent and ready for engineering pickup whenever V1.2 sequencing fires. **Second, permission-gating is the right shape for the cultural reality.** Forcing parent → couple → vendor relay matches no real PH wedding coordination pattern; opening it up unconditionally would lose the couple-gatekeeper protection that prevents vendor spam + vendor reaching wrong family members. The middle ground (couple grants `can_message_vendors` per moderator) gives the couple agency without the relay friction. **Third, the change is fully backwards compatible.** Existing V1.1 single-couple events behave identically — `event_moderators` rows for bride + groom both have `can_message_vendors=TRUE` by default; no other moderator rows exist; vendor chat surface is identical to prior behavior. The new behavior only activates for events with non-couple moderators (V1.2 onward). **Flipside accepted:** (a) the memory rule is now ~3x longer because it must describe permission-gating + role defaults + visibility-tag interaction; acceptable because the rule was always implicit-context-heavy and the explicit version is clearer for future agents; (b) couples who don't explicitly grant `can_message_vendors` to a parent will have a parent who cannot message vendors directly — recovery is one toggle in `/dashboard/{eventId}/moderators`; not a hard problem; (c) if a moderator messages a vendor and the couple later removes that moderator, the thread is preserved but the removed moderator loses access — vendor's existing replies continue routing to remaining moderators per 0048 data preservation rules. **Sequencing note:** Ninth 2026-05-19 row · memory + spec rule alignment · no schema migrations · no SKU changes · no app code · pilot scope unchanged · `.docx` mirror regeneration deferred (pandoc availability unverified). |
